#5125. CFTC Makes Historic Move With Blockchain Derivatives Approval

The U.S. Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) has opened historic doors for blockchain technology adoption in traditional financial markets as it approves exchange and clearinghouse applications for blockchain-based derivatives market infrastructure.

On Nov. 16, CFTC Chairman Michael Selig announced that the commission had given the green light to exchange (designated contract market or DCM) and clearinghouse (derivatives clearing organization or DCO) applications for a company using blockchain technology for the execution and settlement of derivatives contracts.

As per the official statement, Selig considers this a major step forward for U.S. financial markets. He said that blockchain-based market infrastructure is compatible with current U.S. financial regulations.

"This approval makes clear that there is a regulatory path for companies that want to innovate and use blockchain technology to modernize how derivatives are traded and settled,"  he elaborated.

Selig pointed out that the U.S. derivatives markets have seen a number of technological advancements, from physical trading floors to electronic trading platforms.

Now, with blockchain technology, he believes there is an opportunity to further modernize financial market infrastructure without losing regulatory oversight and investor protections.

Notably, there is no technical architecture requirement imposed on derivatives exchanges and clearinghouses under the CFTC's current regulatory framework.

Therefore, firms that leverage blockchain-based networks can apply for regulatory approval under the same set of rules and regulations that apply to traditional financial market participants.

However, Selig also clarified that blockchain adoption is not a license to escape regulatory oversight.

Companies will still need to comply with rules covering anti-money laundering (AML), client protections, market monitoring, risk mitigation and other measures designed to protect the integrity of financial markets.

Simply put, the CFTC is looking to bring blockchain technology into regulated derivatives market infrastructure while maintaining the same standards as traditional financial market participants.

For some time, the CFTC chairman has been pushing for a scenario where U.S. companies developing emerging financial technologies can operate in the country without having to look to international markets.

The latest developments further reinforce the message that the U.S. is open for business for companies building blockchain-based financial market infrastructure, according to Selig.

#5124. Hyundai is recalling nearly 30,000 Kona Electric vehicles due to an airbag software defect

Hyundai issued a recall affecting nearly 30,000 Kona Electric vehicles due to a software error that could cause the airbag to fail to deploy in a crash.

In a letter sent to the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration on November 4, Hyundai said it was recalling certain 2019 to 2023 Kona Electric and 2019 to 2022 Veloster N vehicles.

The recall impacts 37,901 vehicles in total, including 29,058 Kona Electric and 8,843 Veloster N models.

According to documents submitted to the NHTSA, the issue stems from a software error with the airbag control unit (ACU) that can cause the airbag malfunction indicator light (MIL) to come on and the airbag system to deactivate under certain driving conditions.

The data may become corrupted during a system reboot or power interruption while the system is writing to the ACU’s memory. When this happens, the airbag MIL will illuminate to warn drivers.

As of October 22, 2026, Hyundai is aware of 331 alleged incidents related to the issue. However, none have resulted in a crash, fire, or injury.

Owners can take their vehicle to the closest dealer, where it will update the ACU software, free of charge.

Hyundai expects to mail out owner notification letters on January 1, 2027. In the meantime, owners can contact Hyundai’s customer service at 1-855-371-9460 with any questions or concerns. Hyundai’s recall number is 311.

You can also contact the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration Vehicle Safety Hotline at 1-888-327-4236 or visit the NHTSA.gov website for more information. The NHTSA campaign number is 26V728.

#5123. Strategy Adds 485 Bitcoin as It Repurchases $228 Million of STRC Preferred Shares

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Strategy has added 485 Bitcoin to its already enormous treasury, but its November 16 disclosure carries another figure that deserves equal attention: the company also repurchased about $228 million of STRC preferred shares. The update is a reminder that digital-asset treasury companies make capital-allocation decisions across both their crypto holdings and the securities used to finance them. Counting BTC alone cannot tell investors whether each step improves their economic position.

Strategy says it bought 485 BTC between November 9 and November 15 for approximately $33.4 million, at an average acquisition price of about $68,887 per coin. The purchase raised aggregate holdings to 850,849 BTC. The company reported an aggregate acquisition cost of around $64.2 billion, equivalent to roughly $75,452 for each Bitcoin held across its history. Those cost figures are not the current market value of the treasury, and the average changes when additional purchases are made. The purchase window also matters because it spans a period of falling Bitcoin prices rather than a single quoted trade.

STRC is part of Strategy’s preferred-capital structure. The company has used such instruments alongside equity and other financing tools to build and manage its balance sheet. Buying back preferred shares below their stated amount can affect obligations, market confidence and the securities still outstanding. It also uses capital that could otherwise serve different purposes. Investors therefore need to compare the BTC acquisition with the repurchase, cash reserves and dividend commitments. Describing the update as an unqualified large Bitcoin bet overlooks the simultaneous management of financing instruments.

For related context, see our earlier report on Twenty One Capital's bitcoin treasury results.

Strategy’s treasury size is a headline measure of exposure, but a common shareholder owns an interest in a company with other securities and obligations ahead of ordinary equity. Increases in BTC can coexist with changes in share count, leverage, dividend requirements and the value of preferred securities. Simple “Bitcoin per company” comparisons do not measure everything shareholders receive. The company provides additional proprietary metrics intended to describe its strategy, but it warns that these are not identical to profitability or conventional balance-sheet valuations.

Bitcoin’s November decline puts the resilience of capital structures under greater scrutiny. A company can keep buying assets while still facing pressure if its shares, preferred securities or access to funding weaken. The key disclosures to monitor are future BTC purchases, security repurchases, cash and liquid reserves, and any changes in capital-raising conditions. The latest acquisition is independently meaningful, but its significance for investors depends on the full balance sheet and the prices at which capital is issued and retired.

Capital structure deserves particular attention because Strategy finances parts of its Bitcoin accumulation through instruments with distinct investor claims. Preferred shares, common equity and debt can behave differently during sharp BTC drawdowns, especially when market prices diverge from accounting values. The relevant questions are not just how many coins were bought but what resources funded the transaction, which obligations were reduced and what exposure remained per common share. Investors should consult the full financing disclosures rather than treating a gross BTC increase as automatic shareholder accretion.

For related context, see our earlier report on JPMorgan's Strategy shareholdings disclosure.

The company has clearly continued acquiring Bitcoin through weakness. The more complete story is that its treasury strategy also includes active management of preferred claims, making both sides of the November 16 announcement essential to understanding the business.

Reporting sources: Strategy November 16 investor release · The Block figures and context

#5122. Anthropic IPO Price Could Top $80 And Pre-IPO Traders Are Copying the SpaceX Script: What Traders Should Expect

The Anthropic IPO may price above $80. Hyperliquid’s pre-IPO contract sits 24% higher, the setup SpaceX had before it listed.

The Anthropic IPO could price above $80 a share on Wednesday evening, above the $70 to $78 range the Claude AI maker set last week, The Wall Street Journal reported on Tuesday. The newspaper cited stronger-than-expected investor demand. Its advisers have discussed a valuation of $1.5 trillion to $1.6 trillion.

The shares are due to start trading on Nasdaq on Thursday, November 19, under the ticker ANT.

⚠️ What if $ANT mirrors $SPCX after the IPO? pic.twitter.com/pMd9aC7KQb

— Rand Group (@randgroup) November 18, 2026

Crypto traders have been betting on the Anthropic IPO for weeks. On Hyperliquid, a pre-IPO contract that tracks one ANT share trades near $99, about 23% above $80.

We have seen this setup before. The night before SpaceX listed in June, the same kind of contract traded 28% above its $135 IPO price. SpaceX then closed its first day 19% up, below what the contract had promised.

So what should traders know before the bell rings on Thursday?

Anthropic launched its roadshow, the week when a company pitches its shares to large investors, on November 10. Its announcement set the terms:

Demand has run ahead of that. Pricing above $80 would lift the valuation toward the $1.5 trillion to $1.6 trillion the advisers discussed. The final price is set after the US market closes on Wednesday, and the shares open on Thursday.

That price is what large investors pay in the Anthropic IPO. Most retail traders will buy at the opening price instead, which can be far higher.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best New Crypto to Hodl

Hyperliquid, a crypto exchange, lists contracts that let traders bet on a company’s share price before it lists. We compared its Anthropic contract with the one it ran for SpaceX, counting back from each listing day.

The SpaceX contract was right that the shares would jump, but too optimistic about how far. If Anthropic followed the same pattern, a 7% gap from Wednesday’s contract price would put ANT’s first close in the low $90s. That is a pattern from one listing, not a forecast.

EXPLORE: Bitcoin Price Tumbles 19% Ahead of Anthropic IPO: Is SpaceX Crash Repeating?

On the hourly chart, Hyperliquid’s ANT contract trades at $98.83, down from $138 when it started trading on October 26.

The number to watch before the Anthropic IPO opens is tonight’s final price. Every dollar above $80 shrinks the contract’s 24% premium, and with it the room for the first-day pop that pre-IPO traders are betting on.

#5121. Major Crypto-Stealing Exploit in JavaScript Emerges: Zoom, CNN, HSBC Affected

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Malicious JavaScript code snippets were planted on the websites of 178 organizations.

The exploit originated inside Optimizely's Web Experimentation platform, a tool that many large companies use for A/B testing, which means showing users multiple versions of a page to determine which one is more effective.

Report URI estimates that on November 16 the code sat in 188 of the 1,611 Optimizely snippets it crawled, and the real number is most likely higher.

Among the websites that could become victims of the hack are some of the largest names in the world: Zoom, the BBC, CNN, Fox News, Salesforce, GitLab, IKEA, HSBC, Equifax, Air France-KLM, Ledger and Peacock. BleepingComputer confirms that the script loads on many of them.

Essentially, the script's goal was to swap a wallet address on the page for the attacker's one, capture card numbers, take screenshots of pages and more. It was a whole stack of tools used to steal personal data, wallets and other assets.

According to Report URI, the attacker's wallets received about $7,040 within the first nine hours. That is a drop in the ocean considering the names involved, though the losses from card skimming are unknown. A card skimmer was added later that day, and a hidden iframe tried to load an iOS exploit chain on older iPhones.

Optimizely confirmed that an unauthorized party had exploited a vulnerability in Experimentation to add malicious JavaScript to certain customer projects. How the flaw worked is yet to be confirmed.

If successful, it could become one of the largest supply-chain attacks we have ever witnessed. Considering the attack came from a third party, we might see one of the largest service migrations in the world, as none of the third-party services and libraries can be trusted nowadays if used through an API.

Cryptocurrency services delegate certain tasks to third-party providers all the time, so witnessing a similar situation in our field is more than a possibility.

For product owners: stay cautious and rethink the use of services prone to exploits. For users: stay vigilant and try not to put all your eggs in one basket.

#5119. Bitmine Adds 7,653 ETH as Ethereum Treasury Climbs Above 6.08 Million Tokens

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Ethereum treasury company Bitmine says it added 7,653 ETH over the past week, taking its reported holdings to approximately 6.08 million tokens. That is an increase in the unit count, but not a guarantee that the dollar value of the position grew at the same time. Ether has been under heavy selling pressure, and the company’s reported combined crypto, cash and marketable securities holdings were worth about $11.8 billion in its November 16 update. The contrast highlights the risks of evaluating a digital-asset treasury through a single metric.

Bitmine’s investor-relations page lists a November 16 release reporting an ETH holding above 6.08 million. The Block says the latest weekly addition was 7,653 ETH, while ether prices had declined sharply. Buying more tokens increases the number held, but changes in the price can overwhelm the value of incremental purchases over a short period. Treasury investors should therefore track both coins and fair-market value. It is possible for a company to accumulate strategically while shareholders experience a substantial decline in the marked value of the underlying asset.

Bitmine has framed its strategy around acquiring a material share of Ethereum’s circulating supply and building associated staking infrastructure. The larger the treasury becomes, the more its exposure concentrates in a single volatile asset. The company’s long-term thesis may connect ETH ownership with network activity and institutional tokenization, but that remains an investment view rather than a guaranteed economic result. Significant purchases also raise questions about how they are financed and how much incremental exposure existing shareholders receive after dilution or other capital-structure changes.

For related context, see our earlier report on institutional Ethereum yield-fund developments.

Bitmine has also discussed staking and validator operations as ways to earn rewards using Ethereum holdings. Staking can produce token-denominated revenue and contribute to network security. However, rewards vary with network conditions and do not remove the risk that ETH falls in dollar terms. Running validators involves technical, operational and potentially slashing or counterparty risks depending on the arrangement. A headline yield can obscure the difference between earning additional tokens and protecting shareholder purchasing power. Investors need transparent reporting on costs, locked assets and realized rewards.

The relevant questions include how much ETH is owned, what it cost, whether assets are encumbered, how staking contributes to cash or token income, and which claims sit ahead of common equity. Short-term market capitalization or portfolio value can fluctuate far more quickly than business operations change. The reported weekly purchase shows continued accumulation, while price declines mean the company’s valuation remains highly sensitive to ether markets. Watching both acquisition activity and funding terms will be more informative than treating chairman Tom Lee’s commentary as a price signal.

The key measure for an ETH treasury is not the gross holding figure alone. Issuance of new equity, financing costs, staking restrictions and any pledges against assets can change what existing shareholders effectively own. A company that buys more tokens while heavily diluting its share count may not increase exposure on a per-share basis. Conversely, a disciplined allocation can be valuable even during a short-term price decline. Quarterly cash-flow and capital-structure details will reveal more about sustainability than a single announcement of token purchases.

For related context, see our earlier report on digital-dollar liquidity trends.

Bitmine’s ETH count has risen, but the company is not insulated from the asset’s drawdown. The next disclosures should be read across holdings, financing, staking operations and balance-sheet value rather than as a contest to accumulate the largest possible token count.

Reporting sources: Bitmine investor-relations announcements · The Block report

#5118. NEAR Token Recovers to $3.26 as Governance Finalizes 1.6% Inflation Cut

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

House of Stake ratified HSP-029 on Nov. 16, and the proposal lowers the maximum annual issuance of NEAR Protocol from 2.5% to 1.6%. On Tuesday, the price of NEAR dipped to $3.12 in the morning and then went back up to $3.26.

HSP-029 was written by Sal Ternullo, CEO of SVRN, and opened for forum discussion on Oct. 7. The vote closed on Nov. 2, and 99.96% of the veNEAR cast was in favor of the proposal; no veNEAR was cast against it. The Security Council's 14-day window to veto the proposal closed on Nov. 16 with no veto, according to House of Stake.

This is the second inflation cut for NEAR in just over a year. In October 2025, the maximum issuance was halved from the original 5% to 2.5%, and in September 2026, the realized issuance stood at 2.45%, according to HSP-029.

At 1.6%, NEAR will have "one of the lowest annual issuance rates among major blockchains," House of Stake said.

According to House of Stake, the reduction will start one quarter after ratification, and validators still have to adopt the protocol upgrade. The issuance cap will reach 1.6% after 24 months.

Ternullo estimated in HSP-029 that NEAR will avoid issuing 11.8 million tokens over two years. At the current price, these tokens would be worth around $38.5 million.

The proposal also calculated that the staking yield of NEAR will go down from around 5.27% to around 3.37% if the staking ratio stays the same. More than half of all NEAR is not staked, according to House of Stake.

BitMEX co-founder Arthur Hayes recently named NEAR among four tokens that he expects to rise before the end of the AI bubble. Hayes set targets of $6 for NEAR, $2,800 for ETH, $1,800 for ZEC, and $40 for VVV.

"The size is small to medium as conviction is meh, but if the music is playing, you gotta dance," Hayes wrote on X.

The Four Horsemen shall pump and then usher in the end of the AI bubble: $ETH = $2800 $ZEC = $1800 $NEAR = $6.00 $VVV = $40 The size is small to medium as conviction is meh, but if the music playing you gotta dance.

Total volume through NEAR Intents, the cross-chain liquidity layer of the protocol, has reached $39 billion, and the service was recently integrated into Talos OEMS, an institutional trading platform.

#5117. Meta-backed 250 MW solar farm breaks ground in Arkansas

Arkansas is getting another 250 megawatts (MW) of solar power, and Meta has signed up to buy it.

EDP Renewables North America (EDPR NA) broke ground on Cypress Knee Solar in Chicot County, in the southeastern corner of the state, on November 4. The solar farm is expected to be completed by 2028, bringing several hundred construction jobs and an estimated $25 million in county revenue over its lifetime.

Meta’s long-term power purchase agreement helps it work toward its pledge to match 100% of its annual electricity use with renewable energy generation. Don’t confuse that pledge with Meta powering its data centers with renewables around the clock, though – Facebook and Instagram’s parent company is also backing new gas plants to meet its growing power demand.

The companies announced the deal in May. Cypress Knee is their third clean-energy agreement, bringing the total capacity covered by Meta’s deals with EDPR NA to 545 MW.

“As energy demand continues to rise, projects like Cypress Knee Solar help strengthen grid reliability while creating jobs, generating meaningful local revenue, and delivering long-term benefits to the communities that host them,” said EDPR NA CEO Sandhya Ganapathy.

EDPR NA says it has already contributed more than $77,000 during project development, including money to restore the Lakeside High School greenhouse, resources for the Lakeside Robotics team, and funding for the Lake Village food pantry.

#5116. Lighter Reopens Withdrawals After Nonce-Reuse Error Exposes Trader API Keys

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Decentralized derivatives venue Lighter says deposits and withdrawals have resumed following a November 16 security incident that initially froze access. The latest explanation points to unsafe signing code used by affected trader accounts, not an announced compromise of the protocol’s smart contracts. That distinction is central to the incident: a cryptographic error in an external trading integration can expose account authority even when a venue’s core software behaves as designed.

Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery traced a withdrawal of 10,393,057 USDC from a Lighter-related Ethereum contract on November 16. The funds were converted into ether, and investigators followed part of the subsequent movement through multiple wallets. Those observations establish a visible transfer trail, but they are not the same thing as a complete, audited final-loss figure. Earlier reports also cited higher possible amounts covering more than one account, which Lighter had not confirmed at the time. The safe description is an observed approximately $10.4 million USDC outflow and an incident whose recovery accounting may evolve.

According to Lighter’s reported postmortem, some affected accounts used unofficial or custom signing code that reused one-time cryptographic values, known as nonces. Reusing these values with the wrong signature scheme can allow an attacker to derive secret signing material from information that was supposed to be public. Once an API signing key is compromised, apparently authorized instructions can be sent through normal account capabilities. This is not a conventional stolen-password story, and it underscores why teams should use well-tested official signing libraries rather than copy cryptographic routines into trading bots.

For related context, see our earlier report on the Unocoin wallet incident.

Lighter activated its Guardian safeguard during the incident, temporarily restricting withdrawals and related activity while its Security Council acted. The exchange says roughly $5 million USDC was returned to affected traders and that Guardian mode was subsequently deactivated. Its official announcements channel states that deposits and withdrawals are back to normal, with pending requests being processed. That $5 million figure should not be described as full recovery of the separately observed $10.4 million transfer. The remaining exposure and final accounting need a detailed reconciled report.

The venue says it has changed how API signatures are exposed and now requires wallet signatures for transfers into public pools. Further planned controls include IP allowlists, scoped permissions, per-key limits and monitoring for repeated nonces. For a trader using custom automation, the immediate lesson is to audit signing code, rotate uncertain keys and understand what each API credential can authorize. Smart-contract audits remain important, but they cannot substitute for secure key handling in external client software.

Nonce-reuse vulnerabilities are particularly serious because digital signatures rely on the uniqueness and secrecy of certain temporary values. If an attacker can infer a private key from flawed signatures, simply restarting a user interface or changing a password may not fix the exposed credential. The scope depends on which keys were affected and whether they could authorise actions beyond trading. Users should follow the protocol team's specific revocation and key-rotation instructions rather than assuming that reopening withdrawals means every associated security risk has ended.

For related context, see our earlier report on the Kava Lend exploit.

This is now a restoration-and-root-cause story, not simply a withdrawal-halt headline. The strongest follow-up will be a fully reconciled account of losses, recovered funds and the effectiveness of the new controls under live trading conditions.

Reporting sources: Lighter official announcements feed · Bitquery transaction investigation · CoinDesk initial incident coverage

#5115. Crypto Wallet Ledger Confirms Security Incident Involving Third-Party Service

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ledger, a crypto wallet firm, said it had experienced a security incident related to Optimizely, a third-party service used on its official website, but reassured its users about the security of their devices and private keys.

In a post, Ledger said it was aware of a security incident impacting Optimizely, a third-party service provider used by many other sites as well.

We are aware of a security incident affecting Optimizely, a third-party service used on https://t.co/d2pWxdG49X, alongside numerous other websites. Our team has remediated the issue, and the malicious code is no longer being served through our website. This incident does not affect the security architecture of Ledger devices. Your private keys remain protected by your Ledger device. For more information from Optimizely: https://t.co/CxzmBxYadb

Website optimization platform Optimizely said it had discovered and contained a security incident affecting some of its Web Experimentation customers on November 16, 2026. Optimizely stated that the vulnerable path has now been blocked, the injected code has been removed, and customers have been contacted directly.

Along these lines, Ledger confirmed that its team had remediated the issue and that the malicious code was no longer being served through its website.

Ledger emphasizes that the incident did not affect the security architecture of Ledger devices and that user private keys remain protected by their Ledger device, thus reassuring users that the incident had not compromised the core security of its wallet products.

According to researcher Scott Helme, on November 16, 2026, malicious JavaScript was served to visitors of 178 organizations' websites across several sectors through the Optimizely Web Experimentation platform. The websites included Zoom, the BBC, CNN, Salesforce, GitLab, IKEA, HSBC, Equifax, Air France-KLM, Ledger, and Peacock.

Most of the malicious code has now been removed, with only 1 of the 188 infected snippets found, down from 169.

The main point is that trusted JavaScript is not always trustworthy. A third-party dependency can exploit a website without ever breaking the website's own code or infrastructure.

#5114. DTCC, Barclays Say Tokenization's Real Prize Is Unlocking Idle Collateral

DTCC, Barclays Say Tokenization's Real Prize Is Unlocking Idle Collateral

The biggest payoff from tokenizing traditional assets won't come from simply putting them onchain, but from putting idle collateral to work, according to executives at the Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation (DTCC) and Barclays.

"You can tokenize an asset, but if you can't do anything with it, what's the point?" Val Wotton, DTCC's global head of equities solutions, said at Solana's Breakpoint conference in London. "For me, it really is around collateral and liquidity."

Scott Lucas, head of digital assets at Barclays, agreed. A lot of collateral sits "relatively idle" at central securities depositories, he said, and much of the early value for banks will come from moving it more efficiently between their own accounts, before extending that to clients. "There's a lot of money in good housekeeping," he added.

The pair spoke on a panel days after DTCC announced that its tokenization service will connect to Solana, with tokenized assets expected on the network in the second half of 2027.

The service, which launched on October 28, can tokenize assets held at DTCC's depository, starting with Russell 1000 stocks, ETFs tracking major indexes, and U.S. Treasury bills and bonds, Wotton said. The tokens carry the same legal and economic rights as traditionally held securities, and adding Solana reflects DTCC's strategy of being "open," "interoperable" and "multi-network," he added.

Wotton said the biggest opportunity lies in "liquidity optimization," which he said could save "millions, if not billions" of dollars by letting firms move assets that are currently stuck when markets are closed.

Large financial institutions today over-fund and over-collateralize every day because they treat collateral as "just in case," he said. Moving assets onchain, around the clock, would let firms manage collateral "just in time," freeing up "a significant amount of balance sheet" to put to other uses. He added that the ability to move assets and settle them atomically could be "extremely powerful" in times of market stress.

Lucas pointed to Barclays' participation in DTCC's tokenization launch last month, one of the bank's first public moves into the space, and described the efficiency gains from managing its own collateral as good housekeeping.

Barclays is also working with Ironlight on tokenized structured products, Lucas said, though he stressed "it's not a done deal." He added that he would like to see Solana adopted by other central securities depositories around the world, building a shared infrastructure layer across Asia-Pacific, Europe and the United States.

Both executives said adoption remains early. Wotton said the industry is "still in the crawl stage," and that traditional and digital rails will exist side by side "for quite a long time," though DTCC's clients are already tokenizing assets daily.

Lucas said he tries to stay "healthily skeptical" about the speed of adoption while making sure Barclays is ready for its clients. At a recent traditional finance conference in Miami, he said, "every institution we met had money on the table," making investments and building products, a shift from a year earlier, when most were still asking questions.

Barclays has been gradually expanding its crypto activities. The bank invested in U.S. stablecoin startup Ubyx earlier this year and has reportedly weighed a push into tokenized deposits and stablecoin payments, according to Bloomberg. It was also among the investors backing crypto custodian Copper in 2022.

#5113. Kia’s EV5 spotted with an updated design and a huge new infotainment display

Kia’s Sportage-sized electric SUV, the EV5, was spotted with a distinct new style and an overhauled interior, including a huge widescreen infotainment screen.

The EV5 is the fully electric SUV we want, but can’t have in the US. After launching it in China in 2023, Kia introduced the global version last year, which is now on sale in Europe, Canada, the UK, and other markets.

It has already been a big part of Kia’s comeback in China and is expected to become one of its best-selling EVs.

Kia appears to be preparing an updated version. A new EV5 model was recently spotted by ShortsCar in a South Korean parking garage without camouflage, and it’s expected to be the updated version.

You can see in the video that the design has changed quite a bit. Up front, the hood and headlights remain about the same, but new design elements like a black accent light are added.

It also features a unique bumper design, similar to that of the rugged EV5 WKNDR Concept Kia revealed last November at the Guangzhou Motor Show.

The biggest changes are inside, where the interior appears to be completely overhauled. Instead of the current large display screen spanning from the driver to the center, the new EV5 features a display that extends from the center to the passenger side.

There also appears to be an added slim display in front of the steering wheel. Since Hyundai has recently added a slim display to vehicles equipped with its Pleos infotainment system, the updated Kia EV5 is expected to feature the new setup.

However, this is still speculation and has not been confirmed. The center console has also changed. Instead of the physical buttons, it now has a cover, giving it a cleaner overall look.

According to a sticker on the back, the updated EV5 is set to launch in the Philippines. Given Kia initially launched the EV5 in China in 2023, it will be due for a facelift around 2027.

Kia has yet to confirm whether and when it will launch the updated EV5 in other global markets, such as Europe or Korea. It will likely arrive after the China-made version, like the current EV5. We should learn more details soon.

#5112. Former Robinhood Engineer Pleads Guilty to Trading Crypto Listings Through Hyperliquid

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

A former Robinhood software engineer has pleaded guilty to using confidential information about forthcoming crypto listings to trade perpetual futures on Hyperliquid. The US Attorney’s Office for the Southern District of New York says Huaisong Xiang agreed to forfeit $125,523 in proceeds from the scheme. The case is a useful reminder that moving a trade to a decentralized venue does not erase legal duties tied to information obtained through an employer. It is also important to describe this as a guilty plea by one defendant, not a judgment against the entire platform or everyone named in earlier proceedings.

Federal prosecutors say Xiang had access to internal plans detailing which digital assets Robinhood would add and when public announcements were expected. Between 2025 and 2026, he used that information to take positions in perpetual futures linked to those assets before listings became public. Such instruments allow a trader to speculate on price changes without buying the underlying tokens, and they can be held without a fixed expiry if funding requirements are met. The information advantage came from alleged misuse of confidential business plans, not from a flaw in the exchange’s public data feed.

The Justice Department’s November 16 release says Xiang pleaded guilty to one count of wire fraud before US District Judge Colleen McMahon. He agreed to forfeit the stated proceeds, and sentencing is scheduled for February 25, 2027. The offense carries a statutory maximum of 20 years, but that is not a forecast of what sentence the judge will impose. This reporting describes the admitted conduct as set out by prosecutors and the plea. Any allegations against another individual should remain separate unless they are themselves resolved by court action.

For related context, see our earlier report on Hyperliquid trading and open-interest trends.

Hyperliquid is a decentralized venue for perpetual derivatives, but the duties at the center of the case arose when confidential employer information was misappropriated. An employee’s ability to execute a trade without a conventional broker does not make the information public or remove obligations to protect it. On-chain settlement can also leave a detailed transaction trail, although linking wallets to specific people requires evidence. The case illustrates why compliance policies must cover external crypto platforms and derivatives, rather than only the trading accounts an employer officially monitors.

Companies planning token listings need clear access controls, internal approval records and policies that explain what staff may trade and when. Prosecutors’ earlier account described employees with access to nonpublic listing communications. Decentralized markets give informed actors new instruments, but do not change the underlying conflict between privileged information and fair market access. For traders, the case is not an argument that every price move before a listing is illicit; proof of confidential access, duties and misuse is the key distinction.

For compliance departments, the case illustrates why handling confidential listing plans resembles handling other market-sensitive corporate information, even when the resulting trade takes place on a decentralized venue. Firms may need clearer access controls, monitoring of employees' external accounts and written policies on information obtained through employment. Trading venues can publish transactions, but linking a wallet to the person who controls it often requires additional evidence. Regulators and prosecutors will continue to evaluate conduct under the applicable statutes rather than using blockchain transparency as an exemption.

For related context, see our earlier report on an earlier crypto-asset security case.

The plea adds a concrete enforcement example to a debate often framed in abstract terms. It shows how traditional fraud theories can reach misconduct involving on-chain perpetuals while leaving the status of ordinary public-information trading to be assessed on its own facts.

Reporting sources: US Justice Department November 16 statement

#5109. DeFi Development Adds 9,482 SOL as Treasury Grows for Tenth Straight Week

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

DeFi Development Corp. has reported another increase in its Solana treasury, adding 9,482 SOL and SOL equivalents to reach approximately 2.62 million. The November 16 SEC-filed statement describes ten consecutive weeks of holdings growth and a separate streak of 31 business days of dividends on its CHAD preferred stock. The pairing illustrates how companies built around crypto treasuries increasingly operate two different propositions: directional token exposure for common shareholders and an income-oriented capital instrument with its own terms.

The filing lists approximately 2,619,311 SOL and equivalents, up from 2,609,829 in its previous update. Management says the accumulation streak began in September following a return to active buying in late August. Its activities include acquisitions, staking, validator operations and other on-chain opportunities. That mix matters because not all increases in token inventory necessarily correspond to purchasing the same number of SOL on a spot exchange. A complete shareholder analysis should separate bought tokens from rewards, then compare the increase with changes in the company’s shares and outstanding claims.

The company says increasing SOL per share is a strategic objective. A coin count divided by a share count can illustrate one dimension of exposure, but it does not replace net asset value, cash-flow analysis or a review of dilution. Capital raised to buy SOL may increase total tokens while changing ownership percentages. Some holdings may also be subject to staking arrangements or operational constraints. Investors should examine the definitions attached to any company-specific per-share indicator before concluding that a higher treasury automatically improves common-equity returns.

For related context, see our earlier report on Solana corporate treasury results.

The firm’s variable-rate Series C perpetual preferred stock, CHAD, carries a reported 14% annual dividend rate as of the update. The company says it has made payments for 31 consecutive business days. Those distributions are subject to the preferred security’s terms and declarations by the board; a stated annualized rate is not an ironclad future payment guarantee. Common stockholders and preferred investors face different risks and rights. Presenting the preferred yield as though it were a return paid by holding SOL would be inaccurate.

Solana’s price can move substantially faster than a weekly treasury increase, so a company could report more tokens while its dollar portfolio value falls. Staking rewards are also variable and bring their own operational requirements. For DeFi Development, the most informative future disclosures will combine updated token counts with cash reserves, financing costs, issuance, validator income and the amount available to common equity. The accumulation streak is a measurable development, but its investment significance depends on what the company gave up to extend it.

Treasury-company comparisons also require attention to liabilities and timing. Two firms holding similar numbers of SOL can have very different shareholder economics if one borrowed to finance purchases, has preferred dividends to meet or issued substantially more common stock. Reported SOL-per-share figures can be useful, but the numerator and denominator should be consistently defined. Investors should also distinguish income from staking from changes in SOL's market value. Neither measure on its own guarantees liquidity to support future payments or operating expenses.

For related context, see our earlier report on Solana infrastructure updates.

This update is evidence of continued SOL accumulation and preferred-dividend activity. It is not an assurance that either share class will preserve its value, and the reported yield should not be confused with a guaranteed return on the underlying cryptocurrency.

Reporting sources: DeFi Development Corp. SEC exhibit

#5108. Solana Drops 'Mainnet Beta' After Six Years: Here's What 1.0 Is All About

Solana Drops 'Mainnet Beta' After Six Years: Here's What 1.0 Is All About

Solana is officially out of beta, more than six years after launching a blockchain that has been handling real money the whole time.

The Solana Foundation on Monday retired the “mainnet beta” label and introduced Solana 1.0, a shared version number for the network's underlying rules. The change is primarily about how developers and validator operators coordinate software updates, not a fresh boost to transaction speed or any other meaningful improvement over what users already have.

Solana launched its mainnet beta on March 16, 2020, and kept the experimental-sounding name even as users traded tokens, built applications, and moved assets across the chain.

Nothing happens to those assets now. “Your SOL, accounts, assets, applications, and transaction history remain the same,” the Foundation said. Users don't need to swap tokens, move wallets or install anything to keep using the network.

Solana has two major validator clients: Agave, developed by Anza, and Firedancer, built independently by Jump Crypto. Each has its own code, release schedule and version numbers, which can make it harder to tell whether different software releases support the same network features.

Solana 1.0 creates a common reference point for the protocol itself. An Agave release and a Firedancer release can support the same protocol version without sharing a software version number.

That distinction matters as the network relies on more than one software implementation. A bug in one client need not affect every validator, although software diversity alone cannot guarantee the network will stay online.

“Announcing a protocol version does not activate every associated improvement at once,” the Foundation warned. Operators still need to check whether their particular software supports a planned upgrade and follow its activation instructions.

The Foundation said it coordinated the change with Anza and the Firedancer team. Its announcement does not describe a new voting system or a change in who governs Solana; the network's governance process for proposals remains separate.

The Foundation credits engineering work that preceded Monday's announcement, particularly Alpenglow and Firedancer. Solana's Alpenglow upgrade went live on October 21, replacing the system validators had used to reach agreement since launch.

Alpenglow targets transaction finality of roughly 150 milliseconds, compared with about 12.8 seconds previously. Finality is the point at which a transaction is considered settled; that improvement came from the October upgrade, not from calling the network 1.0.

Firedancer also reached mainnet in 2025, giving validators a separately developed alternative to Agave. That reduces dependence on a single codebase, though the extent of the protection depends on how widely different clients are actually used.

Solana has reasons to be careful with promises of reliability. Its network went offline for nearly five hours in February 2024, following an outage lasting almost 19 hours a year earlier.

Documentation and block explorers will gradually adopt the new mainnet name, according to the Foundation. Its upgrade tracker still lists Agave 4.4 as planned for November and Agave 4.5 for December, each with its own release and activation requirements.

The $0.00000413 level, which was the point of reversal back in July, is already lost, with the price dipping to around $0.0000037 during the drop.

Divergence of moving averages could be hinting at a lack of momentum change, suggesting increasing conviction in the current market. The trend is unlikely to flip here, unfortunately.

With the RSI at the bottom, the next step should be either a sideways trend or a gradual recovery. The market is currently moving purely on speculative accumulation that was triggered by the massive price drop across multiple assets.

The market is weak and might get weaker, but the most selling pressure SHIB could face has already been injected.

#5106. Ford’s electric pickup is suddenly selling like hotcakes at 50% off used

Once America’s most popular electric pickup, the Ford F-150 Lightning is finding a second life on the used market.

The F-150 Lightning is hard to pass up at 50% to 60% off sticker price, even used. With new models no longer available, Ford’s electric pickup is suddenly selling like hotcakes on the used market.

After the last model rolled off the assembly line last December, CEO Jim Farley said the decision to end production came as the “very high-end EVs, the $50K, 70K, $80,000 vehicles, they just weren’t selling.”

At 50% to 60% off, it now looks like a bargain in the used market. “When Ford was selling it brand new, the math was complicated,” Jimmy Douglas, CEO of Plug, a wholesale EV dealer, recently told Bloomberg.

On the used market, however, “the Lightning is 50% to 60% of the sticker price and that is really a sweet spot,” he added.

Used Ford F-150 Lightnings are selling in about 20 days, less than half the 44-day average for used vehicles in the US. According to Edmunds.com, prices are now up 4% to about $50,000 since the start of the year.

Douglass said that close to one-third of used models are sold by rival dealerships that would normally flip them back to Ford. However, many are opting to sell them directly because of demand.

Chase Norris from Colorado called the electric pickup a bargain. He purchased a used Lightning for around $40,000 and considers it a major upgrade from his previous gas F-150.

EV Group CEO and cofounder Alex Lawrence, which sells pre-owned electric vehicles, said he can’t get his hands on any reasonably priced used F-150 Lightnings currently.

He said many Lightning buyers use it as a second truck to save money during their weekly commute.

It looks essentially the same as the gas-powered F-150, offers instant torque, and produces zero tailpipe emissions, but the electric pickup failed to meet Ford’s expectations because of its higher price.

The base F-150 Lightning Pro debuted in 2021 with a starting MSRP of $39,974, but that was quickly raised to around $60,000 a year or so later.

According to Edmunds.com, the average transaction price for the F-150 Lightning in its first year of sales was $79,000 because Ford focused on higher-priced trims. The gas version’s ATP was about $19,000 less.

Even with the higher sticker price, Ford was losing a considerable amount on each Lightning it produced. In Q1 2024, Ford lost more than $100,000 on every EV it built.

Next year, Ford will take another crack at the electric pickup market with the Fathom, the first vehicle based on its “groundbreaking” new Universal Electric Vehicle (UEV) platform.

Ford said the Fathom will start at $28,350 for the standard-range version. Even with the $1,595 delivery fee included, the electric pickup will start at under $30,000.

As the first vehicle built on its UEV platform, Ford promises the Fathom will be a step-change from its first-generation EVs.

According to Ford, the electric pickup will offer more interior space than a Toyota RAV4 and will be the first vehicle with Apple Maps directly integrated into the navigation system.

We also know the electric pickup will have a NACS port after a few prototypes were spotted charging at a Tesla Supercharger this summer.

Ford reportedly aims to sell around 100,000 Fathoms in its first year. CEO Jim Farley said on the company’s third-quarter earnings call last week that the launch remains on track for Q4 2027.

Farley called the Fathom a “breakthrough product” and said that the MSRP under $30,000 is perfect for today’s market.

At least some F-150 owners are already trading in for lower-priced options. A member of the Ford F-150 Lightning Owners Group, JF, said that although it was a great ride, “Carvana gave us just a little less than we paid new,” adding, “R2, here we come!”

When Torque News reached out, JF said he “really liked the Lightning,” but wanted something smaller. “With the current increase in resale value, the time to trade seemed right,” JF added.

The Rivian R2 (see our review) Performance with the Launch Package is now available to order starting at $57,990 with an EPA-estimated range of up to 330 miles and 656 hp. The R2 Premium will go on sale later this year, starting at $53,990

In Spring 2027, Rivian will launch the R2 Standard, starting at $44,990 with an estimated range of up to 275 miles and 350 hp.

So were they losing $100k / truck, or the average loss when all development was included ended up being $100k? Big difference. I don't believe the cost to build each one was $170k+. That makes no sense. They just priced them too high to amortize the development costs over enough vehicles to make them profitable. Every vehicle has to sell a minimum # before they start to make a profit.

Surging F-150 Lightning sales on the used market show buyers want electric pickups. But at the right price.

At $60,000 to $70,000, the Lightning wasn’t so attractive, but at $40,000 used, it suddenly is. Perhaps Ford is on to something with its next-gen EV pickup, the Fathom, set to go on sale starting at under $30,000.

Will it deliver on its promise? Ford has yet to reveal battery specs, towing capacity, range, or any other specs. We should learn more soon.

#5105. Bet25.com Launches Zero Margin Sportsbook to Eliminate Hidden House Edges

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Every sports bettor deals with the same issue: traditional sportsbooks hide extra profit margins within their odds. Behind flashy ads and complicated bonuses, regular bookmakers quietly lower payouts. When building a parlay or combo bet, these hidden cuts stack up fast.

Bet25 is changing this with the Zero Margin Sportsbook, setting the new rules of sports betting. Built on honesty, clear numbers, and fair pricing, Bet25 removes traditional bookmaker tricks, so the real payout is received.

In a standard sportsbook, the odds you see are not the true odds. The bookmaker trims 5% to 10% off the price of each game to guarantee their own cut before you even place a bet.

On a single match, this might look small. But on combo bets, it becomes a serious problem. Because combo odds multiply together, the hidden house cuts multiply too. Every leg you add gives you a payout that is much lower than the actual math says it should be.

The Bet25 Zero Margin Sportsbook is not a temporary promo or a short-lived boost. It is a completely different way to run a sportsbook:

A normal bookmaker takes a cut on pick 1, pick 2, pick 3, and so on. Bet25 charges one single 5% fee whether you pick one game or five.

The difference is easy to see on a multi-leg ticket. Take a simple 5-leg combo where each pick has a true 50/50 probability. A regular bookmaker will usually offer around 1.87 on each outcome to keep their cut, but a zero-margin sportsbook will price it at 2.0, again because of no hidden margin inside.

Same five picks. Same $100 bet. Exactly the same risk. But Bet25 pays out $755 more because no hidden cuts were multiplied along the way.

Almost every betting site claims to have “the best odds.” Bet25 doesn’t need hype or marketing talk. Instead, the advice is simple: just compare.

Open a betting site side by side with the Bet25 Zero Margin Sportsbook. Check any match, build a 3-leg or 4-leg combo on both, and look at the final payouts. You will see the difference right away. It is not marketing – it is pure math.

Turning a concept this disruptive into reality depended on having the right technological backbone. Bet25 teamed up with Vegangster as its primary platform partner, working side-by-side from initial ideation straight through to full deployment. Vegangster’s flexible, cutting-edge architecture gave the team the technical freedom to execute bold and complex features without friction.

In an industry where legacy tech often limits innovation, having a responsive partner who can translate ambitious mechanics into reality has been essential to Bet25’s launch.

Sports betting should be about making smart picks and getting paid fairly when you are right. By creating the new rules of sports betting, removing the hidden house edge, and offering naturally better odds, Bet25 gives players a clear, honest alternative to old-school sportsbooks.

#5104. Cypherpunk Reports 8,967 ZEC Mined as Zcash Treasury Nears 342,000 Tokens

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Publicly traded Cypherpunk Technologies has reported the first results from its Zcash mining operation, producing 8,967.29 ZEC in just 44 days. The company says those tokens represented approximately $9.9 million of mining revenue in the third quarter, while its treasury later grew to about 341,859 ZEC through purchases and continued production. The figures are impressive in isolation. But investors need to separate operating earnings from accounting gains on an appreciated token reserve, especially after ZEC’s volatile price action.

Cypherpunk’s November 16 filing says the mining fleet began operation on August 18 and delivered about 95% uptime through September 30. The company reported revenue corresponding to 8,967.29 ZEC, with a stated average cost of mining revenue of approximately $104 per token before depreciation. Once depreciation of equipment is included, the company reported a higher per-token cost near $237. The distinction is crucial when comparing mining economics across companies because machines lose value over time even if their near-term power and hosting costs look low.

Cypherpunk says holdings reached around 341,858.75 ZEC after adding October mining output and recent market purchases, equivalent to roughly 2% of the circulating supply at its reporting date. Its strategy therefore combines an operating mining business with a substantial asset inventory. Both components are sensitive to the cryptocurrency’s price. Tokens generated through mining may be held rather than sold to cover expenses, which can expand exposure and affect cash needs. A higher coin count is not the same as higher spendable cash in the company’s bank accounts.

For related context, see our earlier report on the recent Zcash NU7 protocol upgrade.

The Block reported approximately $2.6 million of quarterly operating profit and substantially greater net income driven by unrealized increases in the value of the ZEC treasury. Valuation gains can be economically relevant, but they can also reverse without an operational change. Investors should read the quarterly income statement and cash-flow statement together. A business with rising book profits may still need external financing to buy new equipment or meet operating expenses if it is retaining mined tokens. The source of earnings determines how repeatable they are.

The company says its fleet represented a material part of Zcash network hashrate at launch, while broader network competition has already changed that percentage. Management has discussed additional equipment purchases for 2027, subject to commitments and delivery. More hardware can produce more hashes, but miner returns depend on network difficulty, rewards, uptime, electricity and ZEC’s price. The most useful follow-up will compare actual production and operating cash with capital spent, rather than treating a treasury-size target as proof of long-term mining profitability.

Mining economics can shift even when a company executes its own operating plan well. As competitors add machines, a given fleet's fraction of network hashrate may fall, reducing expected token output at the same energy cost. Rewards also depend on protocol rules and equipment performance, while treasury purchasing introduces separate exposure to spot prices. Reporting those risks alongside current revenue helps readers understand why one profitable early period does not establish a permanent advantage. A full-year record will offer a stronger measure of the fleet's competitiveness.

For related context, see our earlier report on Bitcoin mining-difficulty changes.

Cypherpunk now has measurable mining output as well as a sizable ZEC balance. The challenge for investors is to value those as two connected but distinct businesses, avoiding the temptation to treat unrealized token appreciation as recurring operational income.

Reporting sources: Cypherpunk Q3 financial results, SEC exhibit · The Block coverage

#5102. E-quipment highlight: Jungheinrich launches new forklift for Asian markets

Like many equipment brands, German forklift maker Jungheinrich has been struggling to crack the Asian industrial markets – but the company is set to change all that with the launch of a new line of 1.6- to 3-ton class electric forklifts designed specifically to navigate the region’s tight warehouses and factories.

Jungheinrich launched the next generation of its EFG MB and MC electric counterbalance forklifts, giving businesses more flexibility to match truck performance with the battery options, charging systems, and operator features that meet their specific needs.

“Different operations have different requirements,” said Lisa Jing Xue, Managing Director at Jungheinrich’s Qingpu Plant, China. “With the EFG M series, customers can configure the (forklift) truck around their application, workload and operators – whether they need greater maneuverability for confined warehouse spaces, or a stable, powerful truck for demanding transport and stacking work.”

Forklift buyers can choose between two performance packages, prioritizing energy efficiency for everyday material handling or more driving and lifting muscle for more demanding, high-throughput operations. The higher-performance package comes standard on models rated for 2.5-3.0 tons, with a choice of either lead-acid or lithium-ion batteries with 346 or 690 Ah of capacity available to suit different workloads and shift patterns. Both ship with regenerative braking tech that can recover up to 30% of the energy used in stopping and lowering loads, too, helping to keep these machines running longer between charging sessions.

“We carried out extensive customer research to understand what the market needs,” added Jing Xue. “Customers can invest in the performance required today and add further capabilities as their application demands.”

Optional features include fingertip controls, a larger 4″ digital display, upgraded LED lighting, and operator-assistance (ADAS) systems, while flexible seating and cabin configurations are designed to keep drivers comfortable through long shifts. A slim B-pillar and glass roof help visibility in busy warehouses, while the four-wheel EFG MC gets automatic cornering speed reduction as standard. Integrated telematics are also standard, giving fleet managers access to utilization data, maintenance planning and pre-operational checks from day one.

Jungheinrich says the order books for both the three- and four-wheel electric forklifts are open now, ready to be supported by the company’s 6,000+ person strong service network across the Asia-Pacific region.

#5099. Grayscale Says November Crypto Slump Reflects Stablecoin Exits and Tech-Sector Stress

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Grayscale’s latest market note offers a two-part explanation for the sharp decline in crypto prices: money moving out of stablecoins and pressure spilling over from technology equities. Head of research Zach Pandl argues that fundamental adoption trends remain intact, even while Bitcoin and other large assets struggle. That is the assessment of an asset manager with a commercial interest in digital assets, not a settled finding that the sell-off is over. Its value lies in examining the mechanisms rather than repeating the optimistic conclusion as fact.

The November 16 research note points to accelerating withdrawals from stablecoins after the late-October Binance security incident. When investors redeem dollar-backed tokens for bank deposits, the supply of on-chain dollars can contract. That may reduce the liquidity available for trading and collateral, although it does not mean each redeemed dollar was about to buy Bitcoin. Other drivers can change supply, including arbitrage and issuer operations. Grayscale views off-ramping as one contributing factor to the November weakness. Confirming its influence requires tracking issuance, redemptions and market activity across venues.

The firm also identifies pressure in Nvidia and other AI-linked stocks as a possible source of portfolio de-risking. Investors holding growth technology and crypto exposure may sell both when financing conditions worsen or their risk limits tighten. There is a further link through physical infrastructure: some Bitcoin mining firms are developing high-performance computing services for AI operators. Those connections do not make Bitcoin a direct claim on AI company earnings. They suggest overlapping investors and capital requirements through which stress can travel across otherwise different sectors.

For related context, see our earlier report on stablecoin supply contraction.

Grayscale notes that Bitcoin fell below its 200-day moving average on November 12, a level followed by some trend-oriented trading strategies. Leveraged positions and thinner weekend order books can make subsequent price moves more abrupt. Those mechanisms may amplify a decline without identifying a single fundamental cause. Importantly, technical signals are not universal commands: different funds use different models and time horizons, and a moving-average breach does not determine the market’s next direction. The research describes a chain of plausible forces, not a proven one-factor explanation.

Grayscale believes medium- and long-term investors may find current valuations attractive because institutional stablecoin and tokenization activity continues to develop. The note also acknowledges inflation, interest-rate uncertainty and geopolitical risk. Those headwinds could persist longer than optimistic scenarios assume. Readers should distinguish the factual observation of a significant drawdown from the manager’s valuation judgment. ETF flows, stablecoin supplies and changes in rates can help test the thesis, but none offers a reliable standalone timing signal.

Another test of the explanation is whether on-chain capital actually returns when equity-market stress eases. A rebound in technology shares accompanied by ongoing stablecoin redemptions would suggest the factors are not moving in lockstep. In contrast, renewed stablecoin issuance without a broader Bitcoin recovery would show that liquidity availability alone is insufficient. Such cross-checks are more informative than assigning every daily price move to the same narrative. They also allow readers to separate Grayscale's interpretation from the specific market data used to support it.

For related context, see our earlier report on recent bitcoin ETF outflows.

The report provides a framework for thinking about November’s crypto decline, not proof that prices have bottomed. Its strongest contribution is highlighting how stablecoin redemptions, shared growth-stock positioning and leveraged market structure can interact during a difficult month.

Reporting sources: Grayscale November 16 research note

#5098. Tokenized stocks are exactly where stablecoins were in 2020 – How?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Stablecoins started as a way to hold dollars on-chain. Then people started lending, trading, and earning with them. In time, DeFi grew up around them.

Tokenized stocks are heading down the same route now, and the resemblance is uncanny.

What’s worth noting here is how much of it is actually being used.

According to data from Token Terminal, tokenized stocks deposited into DeFi are now worth about $300 million. A year ago, that was at approx. $12.5 million.

This number didn’t move at all through late 2025. Things picked up in the spring, blasted through $100 million by around August, and then shot past a quarter million in September.

It’s also a bigger share of the market now. That $300 million is 6.3% of the $4.8 billion tokenized stock market cap. Last year, only 2.4% was being put to work.

Because once they’re there, holders can borrow against them, earn fees by providing liquidity, or chase yield.

And every new way to use them, is essentially one more reason not to cash out.

Tokenized stocks now have a market cap of about $4.8 billion. Stablecoins crossed that exact number in March 2020. Since then, stablecoins have grown, 59 times over! The number is at $285 billion now.

Tokenized stocks are moving fast too. They were worth only a few tens of millions in early 2025.

But why? A blockchain is basically a ledger for assets, and it gets more useful when it holds things people really want. U.S Stocks are about as in-demand as it gets.

Stablecoins didn’t get to where they are without the effort.

They made it through the COVID crash, the Terra collapse, FTX, and the USDC de-peg after SVB failed. Market cap fell by 28% between February 2022 and October 2023, then spiked up to a high by September 2024.

What kept them going though? Cash and T-bill reserves backed every token, plus people who actually wanted to use them.

Tokenized stocks follow the same 1:1 idea.

Except the risk factor is higher. So is the potential for gains.

#5097. Ripple-Backed Evernorth Cuts XRP Holdings by 60%

The Ripple-backed XRP treasury firm Evernorth has suffered substantial losses on its XRP ventures amid the persistent volatility facing the broad crypto market since the third quarter of 2026.

The firm has reported a massive write-down of its XRP holdings for Q3 2026 in a recent filing with the U.S. SEC, drawing attention from the broader crypto community.

On Tuesday, November 17, Evernorth reported a substantial decline in the value of its XRP holdings after the firm wrote down over $505 million in its latest financial filing with the SEC.

Notably, Evernorth issued a Form 10-Q for the quarter ended September 30, 2026, disclosing that it holds around 346 million XRP, now valued at about $342 million.

This has drawn attention across the XRP community and sparked discussions about the growing impact of persistent volatility on investors’ funds, as Evernorth’s XRP report marks a sharp drop from its initial valuation of $847 million.

Apparently, the firm reported that the tokens are now valued at about 40 cents per dollar of their initial worth. This means that the leading XRP treasury firm has reduced the value of its XRP holdings by nearly 60%.

It is important to note that the massive drop in the value of Evernorth’s XRP holdings does not mean that the firm has sold off its XRP. Rather, it marks a write-down in its previously listed XRP value.

#5096. Binance Hack-Linked Wallet Moves 34,279 ETH, but Investigators See No Confirmed Sale

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

A wallet linked by blockchain investigators to the October 29 Binance breach has transferred 34,279.66 ETH to three newly created addresses, ending a period of inactivity. The ether was worth roughly $63 million at prices used in Tuesday’s reporting. That is a significant movement of suspected stolen funds, but a wallet transfer is not automatically a sale, a successful laundering step or proof that the recipient controls are independent of the sender. Investigators had not identified the new addresses as exchanges at the time of their initial reports.

Monitoring groups MistTrack and Solid Intel reported the transfers on November 17. MistTrack described three movements of approximately 12,000 ETH, 12,000 ETH and 10,279.66 ETH. The receiving wallets were newly created, and the monitored funds had not moved again when the analysts published their findings. Public transaction records make it possible to trace the amounts and destinations, but they do not disclose the human operator behind each address. The link to the earlier breach rests on investigators’ attribution of the sending wallet, not a label embedded in the blockchain itself.

The October attack involved vastly larger losses than Tuesday’s movement. US investigators attributed the incident to a North Korean hacking group, and authorities have announced substantial asset restraints and seizures. Blockchain intelligence firms have also tracked funds moved through different assets and protocols. These parallel developments should not be added together casually, because frozen, seized, recovered and still-controlled amounts describe different legal and operational states. The fresh ETH transfers are one piece of a complex post-incident trail rather than an update to the full net-loss number.

For related context, see our earlier report on Coinbase's custody and security precautions.

Crypto trading commentary sometimes treats any transfer from an exploit-linked wallet as preparation to dump tokens. That can happen, but the evidence here does not yet establish a market sale. Transfers may consolidate funds, redistribute control or precede another operation. Until an address is linked to a trading venue, bridge, mixer or other service, the immediate observable fact is the destination change. Even a later exchange deposit would need careful interpretation: deposits are not identical to executed sell orders. Reporting should preserve those steps rather than collapsing them into one dramatic conclusion.

Ethereum’s public ledger lets investigators monitor known addresses and alert service providers if assets reappear at identifiable points. Token issuers, exchanges and law enforcement may each have different powers depending on the assets and jurisdictions involved. Native ETH cannot simply be frozen by an issuer in the way some centrally administered tokens can. That difference complicates recovery when funds leave services that can intervene. The next useful evidence will be further movements, identification of services receiving the funds and official updates to the case.

Investigators can improve attribution confidence by comparing the sequence, amounts and transaction timing with addresses already associated with the incident. Yet public labels can be incomplete, and a newly created wallet may simply be another address controlled by the same actor. Readers should resist claims that funds have reached a criminal group, a particular country or a cash-out desk unless that identification is supported by additional evidence. Future investigative notices should specify their degree of confidence and distinguish observed transfers from hypotheses about their purpose.

For related context, see our earlier report on the Unocoin wallet compromise.

The latest transfer warrants close monitoring, but it should not be reported as the disposal of $63 million in stolen ether without further proof. The distinction between observable transactions and inferred intent is especially important in a security incident of this scale.

Reporting sources: CoinDesk report of MistTrack and Solid Intel monitoring · MistTrack transfer report
