#5094. Janus Electric secures battery supply deal for its battery-swap semi truck

Australian conversion specialists Janus Electric have been celebrating a growing slate of orders for their Class 8 electric truck conversions, which ditch diesel in favor of advanced battery-swap electric power – but exactly how they’d deliver all those trucks was still a question. Now, we have an answer: Electrovaya will supply the batteries.

The concept of dropping an electric power unit into a Class 8 semi makes perfect sense, as most of these trucks are built as gliders. That is, rolling body and chassis without an engine and transmission that’s ready to receive power from Cummins, Detroit, International, or Volvo Penta. That’s the role that Janus Electric sees itself occupying, and it seems to be working.

Janus’ reservation book crept past 70 units this past summer, helped along in part by California’s HVIP Program and the US EPA’s Diesel Emission Reduction Act (DERA) Grants. When the HVIP voucher is combined with the Port of Los Angeles’ “Plus” support, customers can received up to $166,000 per truck conversion. Factor in the massive fuel savings of electric and the classic good looks of square-nose Western Star truck body, and it’s easy to see why they’d be a popular choice.

As Jerry Seinfeld taught us however, it’s one thing to take a reservation – it’s another thing to deliver. To help make it happen, Janus has inked a battery supply deal with Electrovaya that will see a $26 million initial purchase orders for Electrovaya battery packs to demand for Janus’ products in the US and Canada, along with the battery company purchasing approximately $1.4 million in Janus stock with (what seems to be) actual money.

“Electrovaya is an important partner to Janus Electric, and we’re particularly pleased it has decided to invest directly in our business,” explains Ben Hutt, Managing Director and CEO of Janus Electric. “The investment is a strong vote of confidence in our technology and approach to electrifying heavy transport.”

The Janus Electric-repowered trucks carry a stout, 480 hp electric motor drawing power from a standard 310 kWh battery pack, with up to two battery packs (one on each side) of the truck. In practice, that gives the Janus trucks up to 370 miles of loaded range. After that, the batteries can be DC fast charged in under 90 minutes, or swapped for fresh ones in about four minutes without too much in the way of specialized equipment. That’s not just Janus’ killer app, it means that a battery supply deal like this one with a strategic partner who’s invested in the fledgling company’s success is such a huge deal.

“Reliable battery supply is critical to our growth,” adds Hutt, “and Electrovaya’s expanding North American manufacturing capabilities will be important as we scale. Both companies see considerable opportunity in this market, and we look forward to building on our partnership.”

For their part, Electrovoya executives seem to know their role here, and seem excited to be a part of what Janus is doing. “Class 8 trucking is one of the most exciting and demanding frontiers in electrification, and Janus has demonstrated real commercial traction with fleets in the US, Canada and Australia,” said Dr. Raj Das Gupta, CEO of Electrovaya. “Janus has developed a practical solution to one of the major challenges facing heavy transport, enabling diesel-powered trucks to be converted to electric without compromising their commercial operations.”

Electrovaya will be expanding its American manufacturing capacity with lithium-ion cell, module, and battery pack production at its new, potentially BABA-compliant facility in Jamestown, New York, which is expected to come online in 2027.

#5093. Satoshi-Era Bitcoin Holder Wakes Up After 15 Years, Refuting $250 Billion ‘Lost’ Claim

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

A Bitcoin address that had not moved coins since February 2011 transferred 75 BTC on Nov. 17, according to Galaxy Research. At the current price of $64,935 per BTC, the coins are worth around $4.87 million.

The address is listed as a defendant in a $250 billion lawsuit in New York Supreme Court, in which the plaintiffs are trying to have early Bitcoin wallets declared "lost." Galaxy Research said that several other addresses from the same list have also moved coins in 2026.

According to Galaxy Research analysts, early on Nov. 17, in block 976,209, the address 1ALpEWtXApR19hLXvcG8NfkPyGsUMhFPMS sent coins for the first time. It was first funded on Feb. 8, 2011. At that time, Bitcoin was trading at about $0.90. After 15.8 years, the coins went to an unknown address with a 399-satoshi fee, or about 26 cents, for a paper gain of more than 7,000,000%.

Onchain databases list the wallet as Noah Doe #3409 (Salomon Client Dusted). That label is how it ended up among the defendants in the high-profile case ABC Company, XYZ Company, and Noah Doe v. John Does 1–39,069.

According to the lawsuit, an anonymous plaintiff using the pseudonym Noah Doe and two companies want the rights to 39,069 Bitcoin wallets that have not moved coins for more than five years. The plaintiffs said that these wallets hold about 3.8 million BTC, and at the current price, the coins are worth around $250 billion.

The plaintiffs filed the lawsuit under New York's lost-and-found law, Article 7-B of the Personal Property Law. Under the law, a person who finds lost property has to give it to the police, and the property goes to the finder if the owner does not claim it in time. In the lawsuit, the plaintiffs valued each address at less than $10, and they argued that the waiting period for such items was one year.

They "found" dormant addresses with an algorithm, saved them onto USB flash drives and took them to the New York Police Department (NYPD). Then they served the owners on the blockchain, through microtransactions with embedded messages (OP_RETURN). The practice is known as "dusting," and the campaign is tied to Salomon Brothers Strategic Advisors. Once the waiting period ran out, the plaintiffs asked the court to recognize the coins as theirs.

The case has been put on hold several times over motions to dismiss and amicus curiae briefs from outside experts. At a hearing on Sept. 8, the Bitcoin Policy Institute asked to intervene as a defendant, and The Digital Chamber asked to join the case as amicus curiae. Galaxy Research described it as a case "in which anonymous plaintiffs are seeking to claim legal title over Satoshi's coins."

Galaxy Research's Will Owens, who attended the hearing, said that The Digital Chamber's arguments were "well pleaded" and that "the judge sounded sympathetic to them."

Critics of the lawsuit argue that Article 7-B was written for things you can pick up and hand over for safekeeping. It wasn't written for blockchain records when the "finder" doesn't hold the private keys.

In September, Galaxy head of research Alex Thorn also said that the Lost and Found Property Law includes "protection from 'Noah Doe-style' dormancy attacks on legal ownership."

#5092. 'Tokenization Is Not the Finish Line': Jupiter to Let Users Borrow Against Bullish Shares

'Tokenization Is Not the Finish Line': Jupiter to Let Users Borrow Against Bullish Shares

Jupiter, the largest trading platform on Solana, is bringing Bullish's shares onchain as collateral, as part of a push to put tokenized assets to work.

The company will launch a dedicated, permissioned market on its lending platform, Jupiter Lend, starting with Bullish's NYSE-listed BLSH stock, Jupiter tweeted on Monday. More issuer tokens will follow, though the market won't be available in all jurisdictions, it added.

A NYSE-listed company is bringing its own shares directly to Jupiter Lend.

Today at Breakpoint, we announced our partnership with @Bullish to launch a dedicated, permissioned $BLSH market.

For the first time, Issuer Tokens can be used as collateral inside Solana’s… pic.twitter.com/rWWqwOcWmK

— Jupiter (@JupiterExchange) November 16, 2026

Unlike many tokenized stocks, which give holders a claim on a share, Bullish's issuer-sponsored token "is itself the share," Jupiter COO Kash Dhanda said in a presentation at Solana's Breakpoint conference in London.

Users will be able to borrow against them with fully onchain liquidations in an "institutional-grade lending market," and integrations with qualified custodians, he said. Bullish acquired Equiniti, one of the world's largest transfer agents, earlier this year, Dhanda noted.

Dhanda framed the announcements around what he called the "post-tokenization era." Most tokenized assets today are "parked, not productive," he said.

Of the roughly $460 billion in tokenized assets tracked by data platform RWA.xyz, only about $39.5 billion is actually onchain and can be moved off the issuer's platform, and just $3.4 billion is used in DeFi, according to DeFiLlama, Dhanda said, a "utilization gap" of 99%.

"There is this myth, and this is the thing that's misunderstood: 'If you put it on chain, they will come'," he said. "This is not the field of dreams," he added, arguing that, "Tokenization is not the finish line. It is actually the starting line."

Jupiter launched two other products alongside the Bullish market. The Jupiter Global Passport lets users verify their identity to buy permissioned assets, while keeping their information as private as possible using zero-knowledge data sharing, Dhanda said. It is live with KYC verification, with onchain credit scores and proof of wealth to follow, and the company is exploring licenses in different jurisdictions.

Jupiter Liquidity Services, meanwhile, will help asset issuers design their markets, seed initial capital, find market makers and integrate with Jupiter's routing so that their tokens are available across the platforms that use it. "You integrate once, you will be available everywhere," Dhanda said.

"Shallow credit markets don't drive liquidity on chain," he added. "If you cannot do more with your onchain asset than you can do with the off-chain version of it, you're probably not going to switch."

Jupiter says it has handled more than $500 billion in trading volume so far this year and paid out $162 million in yield. The Breakpoint announcements follow the company's launch last week of token baskets, yield vaults and a rebuilt portfolio, and come as other venues bring tokenized stocks to Solana. Trading platform Formation has notified the SEC of plans to trade tokenized U.S. stocks on the network.

Jupiter powers token swaps on The Information Exchange, Decrypt's trading platform.

#5091. US Senators Question SEC Tokenized-Stock Exemption Over Wallet Transfers and Investor Protection

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Four Democratic US senators have pressed the Securities and Exchange Commission for answers about how its new approach to tokenized stock trading will protect investors and prevent illicit transfers. Their November 16 letter, reported by The Block, takes aim at gaps they see in the SEC’s five-year innovation exemption for certain trading venues. The development is an oversight challenge, not a court ruling or a notice that the exemption has been cancelled. It goes to a central difficulty for tokenized securities: a regulated purchase can become a blockchain transfer to another address.

The SEC introduced its approach in September, allowing qualifying tokenized-securities venues to operate under a defined exemption from some ordinary exchange-registration requirements. The aim is to permit experimentation with trading stocks in token form while the agency assesses a new market structure. A token representing a security still raises questions about ownership, trading rules and investor rights. The exemptions and their precise conditions matter more than a general statement that securities have moved on-chain. Not every blockchain token, broker or venue automatically qualifies for the same regulatory treatment.

Senators Angela Alsobrooks, Ruben Gallego, Mark Warner and Catherine Cortez Masto reportedly told SEC Chair Paul Atkins that the framework lacks sufficiently specific safeguards around know-your-customer and anti-money-laundering procedures. A buyer verified by a regulated venue might later transfer a token to a wallet whose controller has not undergone the same checks. This problem is not solved merely by verifying the identity of the first purchaser. The letter asks how secondary transfers and the reach of compliance obligations would be managed once tokens circulate beyond the initial venue.

For related context, see our earlier report on Hong Kong's developing licensing rules.

The senators raised concerns about retail-investor protections and pricing, and reportedly asked whether companies could opt out of having their shares tokenized. Those questions are distinct from financial-crime screening. A token that references equity needs a clear relationship to the rights and obligations of the underlying security, as well as fair information about execution and ownership. If an issuer’s wishes, the investor’s expectations and the platform’s legal structure do not line up, technical transferability alone cannot resolve the conflict. Policymakers must address those issues in enforceable terms.

The lawmakers asked the SEC to explain its approach and urged a transparent rulemaking process for changes in the regulatory treatment of tokenized securities. Such a letter can affect oversight and public debate, especially as control of congressional committees changes, but it is not itself binding legislation. Venues exploring tokenized equities should continue to follow the actual exemption terms and any subsequent SEC guidance. The next concrete milestones are the agency’s response, proposed revisions if any, and how early trading models implement identity, custody and transfer restrictions.

For related context, see our earlier report on new institutional crypto-custody arrangements.

Tokenization can change how securities move, but it does not automatically settle which rules should accompany them. The dispute now concerns the design of that regulatory perimeter, with no basis to describe the senators’ letter as an immediate prohibition on tokenized stocks.

Reporting sources: The Block report of four senators’ letter

Driven by fear prevailing across the crypto markets, led by heavy Bitcoin [BTC] and Ethereum [ETH] losses, LINK fell below the long-term swing support of $10.61.

Crypto analyst FundingVest observed that certain altcoins saw sizeable outflows from Binance. Chainlink was one of them. By comparison, Bitcoin saw moderate inflows while Ethereum showed slight outflows.

Shrinking supply on exchanges was a bullish signal for the altcoin, even though it ceded the $10.61 swing level to the bears. However, it is not a guarantee of a price rally by itself.

The exchange outflows are overshadowed by the persistent heating signal on the spot volume bubble map. Red bubbles signal increased trading activity in LINK markets.

Heating volume signals alongside a steady price correction from $14.3 to $10.6, a 25.7% decline in just over a month, was a sign of sustained selling.

Such pressure on LINK might be difficult to shrug off, especially now that the price structure was also bearish.

Earlier this month, AMBCrypto had drawn attention to the $10.61-$10.87 area. Key swing levels here marked it as a demand zone, one that bulls managed to defend in late October.

The weekly structure was bullish, though the altcoin has witnessed a deep correction. Using the impulse move higher to $15.77 earlier this year, Fibonacci retracement levels were plotted.

Additionally, this rally in August had also left behind an imbalance (white) that was being tested at the time of writing.

A bullish reaction from $8.88, the 78.6% retracement level inside the fair value gap, would be a strong sign of a bullish reversal. However, a weekly session close below $8.40 would be a warning sign that bearish pressure remained dominant.

#5089. Energica rises from the ashes with plans for cheaper electric motorcycles

The Italian electric motorcycle maker, which ceased operations in 2024 after entering judicial liquidation, has announced a major revival under new international ownership. And while the Energica name is returning, the company behind it is going to look quite different this time around.

Perhaps the biggest change is where the motorcycles will actually be built.

Energica says motorcycle production is moving to Asia, while design, engineering, research and development, as well as responsibility for technical and quality standards, will remain in Italy. The new manufacturing setup is expected to significantly reduce production costs, allowing Energica to cut motorcycle prices by around 20% compared to its previous models.

That could be a big deal for a company whose motorcycles were widely praised for their performance and technology, but were also firmly positioned at the premium end of the market.

The revival comes after Energica was acquired by Energica Pte. Ltd., a Singapore-based company backed by international private investors. Importantly, much of the technological know-how isn’t disappearing overseas with the manufacturing. Energica says its core engineering and design leadership remains in Italy, including longtime CTO Giampiero Testoni and designer Paolo Imeni.

The first motorcycle to make its comeback will be the Energica Experia, the company’s adventure-oriented electric sport-tourer. After that, Energica plans to progressively restart production of the Ego, Eva Ribelle, and EsseEsse9, eventually bringing its entire previous lineup back to market.

At least initially, those motorcycles will use their existing platforms, which should help Energica get bikes rolling off the production line faster without having to develop and homologate entirely new machines. But upgrades are already planned, including a new battery and improvements to range, efficiency, performance, and reliability.

Energica is also specifically targeting Europe, the United States, and Australia as it rebuilds its dealer and service networks. Existing owners haven’t been forgotten either. The company says service has already resumed and spare-parts shipments have restarted to dealers in several countries.

For anyone who followed the electric motorcycle industry over the last decade, Energica’s disappearance was a particularly painful one. This wasn’t some startup with a couple of renderings and a dream. Energica built some of the highest-performance electric motorcycles on the road and spent four seasons supplying the motorcycles for the FIM MotoE World Cup, experience that helped develop its high-voltage and DC fast-charging technology.

Now the company gets something relatively rare in the motorcycle industry: a second chance.

And if moving production to Asia really can knock around 20% off Energica’s prices while retaining the Italian engineering that made the bikes special in the first place, Energica 2.0 might have a considerably better shot at turning great electric motorcycles into a sustainable business.

It seems like moving toward Asian manufacturing is all but inevitable here. It was the expensive European manufacturing of low-volume motorcycles that weighed so heavily on Energica’s balance sheets in the years up to their financial troubles, and so shifting production to countries with both the expertise and the cost benefits for motorcycle manufacturing simply makes sense.

Energica isn’t alone in discovering the economics of moving electric motorcycle production to Asia. Zero Motorcycles spent years manufacturing its bikes in California before beginning a major shift toward production in the Philippines in 2023, including both complete motorcycles and major subassemblies. Now the company works with multiple factories, including major manufacturers like Zongshen in China, to reduce production costs for its electric motorcycles while still maintaining the ‘designed in California’ marketing angle.

It’s too early to tell how the new strategy will play out for Energica, but the fact that the company is now following a more well-worn trail is a positive sign for those who have spent the last two years hoping for a revival.

#5088. Ripple-Backed Evernorth Takes Massive $505 Million XRP Hit

Ripple-backed XRP treasury company Evernorth Holdings has disclosed a significant decrease in the book value of its crypto asset holdings, with total impairment charges amounting to around $505 million before going public on Nasdaq.

Per an SEC filing on Nov. 13, the 346.3 million XRP tokens that Evernorth Holdings owned had a book value of just $341.9 million as of Sept. 30, 2026.

This is compared to the firm's initial accounting of the crypto assets at roughly $846.6 million, with an average acquisition or contribution price of $2.44 per XRP. This represents a nearly 60% decline in book value.

As per the latest filing, Evernorth recorded around $271 million in XRP impairment charges in the first nine months of 2026. This is in addition to the $233.7 million in impairment charges recorded in 2025.

Interestingly, only $6.9 million of the recent impairment charges occurred in the third quarter.

This also explains why Evernorth's XRP assets had a book value so far below their market value at the end of September.

Evernorth calculates impairments based on the lowest observable intraday price of XRP for each batch of tokens acquired. Once the assets are written down, the company is unable to reverse the previous losses if the price rises.

For reference, on Sept. 30, XRP closed the day trading at around $1.49 on Coinbase. At that price, Evernorth's 346.3 million XRP tokens would have been worth roughly $516 million, much higher than the disclosed book value of $341.9 million.

The recent disclosure comes around a month after Evernorth completed its business combination with Armada Acquisition Corp. II on Oct. 9. It started trading on Nasdaq under the symbol XRPN on Oct. 12.

Following the business combination, Evernorth held around 473 million XRP tokens and had also raised gross cash proceeds of around $300 million.

Its investors include Ripple, SBI Group, Arrington Capital, Pantera Capital, Kraken and GSR.

On Oct. 14, Evernorth announced collaborations with Ripple, Kraken, Coinbase and BitGo to implement institutional investment strategies starting in Q4 2026.

Meanwhile, its latest SEC filing did not include details on the amount of XRP deployed to generate yield or the returns generated from it.

#5086. Top 13 Telegram games to try out in November 2026

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Telegram has become more than just a communication app for people around the world. What started out as just a messaging app has grown into an ecosystem of its own. Users can enjoy casual games, puzzles, and simulation-based games. Accessibility is another key reason why players are attracted to Telegram games; it has an easy point of entry and reward mechanisms as well.

Here are our top picks for the best Telegram games in the month of November 2026:

Chat Wars is a traditional role-playing game that is built around fantasy world-building, heroes, guilds, resources, and strategic decisions. Players start by building their characters and can collect resources, improve equipment, participate in activities, and contribute to their guild.

Combat, crafting, and resource management are all part of the game’s overall experience, so players can become part of a larger experience rather than just collecting points. Guild participation is a major part of the game, and groups can cooperate, coordinate, and work towards shared objectives.

Catizen lets users develop, combine, and manage digital cats. The main objective of the game is to collect new cats, merge similar ones, expand facilities, complete missions, and grow. Players can collect rewards, unlock new buildings, and participate in seasonal events.

The game has a more holistic approach than many other tap-to-earn games as it focuses on community interactions and long-term engagement. The game has evolved over time into an ecosystem of its own with a native token called CATI.

A very popular game in the space, Hamster Kombat also has a tap-to-earn format where the original gameplay revolved around managing a virtual crypto exchange owned by a CEO who was a hamster. Now the experience has evolved into HamsterVerse, bringing additional games and experiences into the ecosystem.

One such experience is Hamster GameDev, which allows players to manage a virtual game development studio where they can hire team members, create games, upgrade their operations, and generate virtual income. Instead of a repeated tapping gaming, this gives it a more structured managment style traditional approach.

Pig of Ton is a hyper-casual merge-and-battle game that integrates character collection, merging mechanics, and competitive rankings. The game has futuristic cyber pigs that can be collected, upgraded, and developed, and are a central part of the gameplay.

The futuristic pig theme gives it a very distinct identity in the space, and instead of using traditional fantasy characters, cyber pigs are its central characters.

PlayDeck has a large collection of casual games in a single Telegram Mini App, making it convenient for players who enjoy playing in new genres. It gives players a chance to explore challenges, puzzles, and reaction-based games.

PlayDeck offers convenience and accessibility to all its users and can compete with other open PlayBoxes, spin the Wheel of Luck, and earn $PlayCoin.

MemeFi is a tap-to-earn combat game where players fight meme-inspired bosses, earn crypto rewards, and make character progressions. The main USP of the game is its combat, where players go up against enemies and defeat challenging opponents.

Users can also create various clans, manage their energy levels, or use boosters. MEMEFI is the utility and governance token of the platform and has a total supply of 10 billion.

Rocky Rabbit is a tap-to-earn mini-game that was built on the TON network. Players need to train their rabbits in the game, participate in duels and complete any tasks to rank higher. The game takes its inspiration from fitness and boxing.

The platform’s native token RBTC (RabBitcoin) was launched on TON in 2024, and players can join competitions and battles, earn rewards, and bonuses for referrals.

A famous retro-style pixel mini-game on TON, Yescoin lets users swipe their fingers across the screen to catch falling coins similar to Fruit Ninja. Players can complete missions, collect coins, upgrade their skills, and be a part of the community.

Players can buy upgrades and power-ups to boost their earnings, as it is managed by an energy system that regenerates over time. There are quests, missions, events, and various other options for players to earn rewards throughout the game.

A play-to-earn mini-game built around the Minesweeper mechanics that mixes it with dungeon exploration. Players need to explore a 5×5 grid where they tap blocks one by one and earn gold. If they choose the block that has a trap, the game is over.

As a bonus after every round, there is an option to play a clicker side game where players need to tap the skull to farm gold. Besides the game itself, players can collect keys for passive income and tickets for daily cash prizes.

Pixel Tap is a cyberpunk combat-themed mini-game where players can tap the screen to earn in-game coins, upgrade robot fighters, and take part in real-time battles against each other. It is part of the wider Pixelverse ecosystem, and the game revolves around tapping mechanics and PvP battles.

In-game currency can be earned through referrals, daily tasks, battles, and general activity. Players can get into battles and earn points and tokens in PvE and PvP settings. $PIXFI is the native token of the platform, and rewards can be earned in it.

Egg Drop is a play-to-earn game on the BNB Chain where players drop virtual eggs into target zones and score points while collecting points and levelling up “Birdies”. The eggs fall under various categories, such as white, yellow, royal, energy, punch, shield, attack, and steal types.

Accessibility and engagement are important parts of the game where the sessions are short and easy to navigate and aimed at users looking for time-intensive games. Token incentives and collected items can be accumulated through regular activity in the game.

RollerTap has a tapping mechanism where players collect RCC or Roller Community Coins that are in-app currency. The rewards can be used to increase their tap level and how much they earn overtime creating a system built around steady activity and upgrades.

Leaderboards in the game also allow players to compete with each other and track their rankings. They can also collect cards that boost their profits and take part in limited eveents and challenges for extra rewards.

A post-apocalyptical setup where players have to survive the zombie attack and earn the $Bombie token, this Telegram game was launched in 2024. The objective is simple: shoot the zombies, defeat the bosses, and upgrade your weapons and gears.

Players can earn the token through the play-to-earn model and also by taking part in airdrops and boss raids. There are community events that help players interact with each other as well.

Telegram games give an easy and convenient way for players to engage with a lot of these tap-to-earn apps. They started out as simple games and have evolved into much more complex ecosystems of their own overtime, offering a wide variety of gaming experience.

Each game brings something different and can be appealing to a different type of gamer. However, it is advisable to do your own reseach before monetarily investing in any game.

#5085. Tesla to end Cybertruck production as sales stall at ~10% of plan

Tesla is ending Cybertruck production. CEO Elon Musk announced early Friday that the automaker will “wind down” the electric pickup at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of 2027, about three and a half years after the first deliveries.

Musk says it’s to make room for more Cybercab production. But Giga Texas isn’t short on space. The Cybertruck is short on buyers, and it sells at roughly a tenth of the 250,000 units a year Tesla built its line for.

Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production

That’s all he said. Nothing yet on how long Tesla will keep selling inventory or what happens to parts and service for existing owners.

Tesla doesn’t break out Cybertruck deliveries. It lumps them into “Other Models,” along with the Model S, Model X, and now the Semi.

That bucket tells the story anyway. Tesla delivered 8,295 “Other Models” in Q3 2026, down 48% year-over-year and the lowest since Q2 2021. With Model S and Model X production over since April, we estimated that 7,000 to 8,000 of those were Cybertrucks.

That’s a pace of about 30,000 trucks a year. Tesla planned the Cybertruck line for 250,000 units annually, and its latest shareholder letter still lists more than 125,000 units of installed capacity. So the truck is selling at about 12% of plan, and roughly a quarter of what’s actually installed.

The US numbers from Cox Automotive are worse. Cox estimates 3,519 Cybertruck sales in Q1 2026 and 3,744 in Q2, down from 14,416 in Q3 2024.

And not all of those were real customers. S&P Global Mobility data showed SpaceX bought 1,279 Cybertrucks in Q4 2025, and Musk’s companies took 1,339 in total, about 19% of US registrations that quarter. If it wasn’t for Musk’s self-dealing, the Cybertruck would probably be dead already.

Tesla tried to fix demand. It launched a cheaper rear-wheel-drive version in 2025 and pulled it months later, then brought a cheaper dual-motor trim to customers this summer. And then it raised prices by $5,000 in August. The dual-motor now starts at $74,990.

The “make room” explanation doesn’t hold up. Giga Texas has more than 10 million square feet of factory floor, by Tesla’s own count, and the Cybertruck line is already running at about a quarter of its installed capacity. Most of it is sitting idle.

The Cybercab also isn’t at a volume where it’s fighting anyone for floor space. Texas had 463 Cybercabs registered as of late October, and Tesla built a separate line for its “unboxed” manufacturing process.

If Tesla needs more Cybercab capacity down the road, it has the space. What it doesn’t want is to keep paying for a truck with its own stainless steel body, its own castings, and its own supply chain at 30,000 units a year.

Let’s call this what it is. Tesla isn’t ending the Cybertruck because the Cybercab needs room. It’s ending it because nobody’s buying it at anything close to the volume Tesla planned, and supporting a low-volume vehicle this unique is expensive.

I wouldn’t be surprised if Tesla lost a couple of billions on the Cybertruck experiment. Between R&D, pushing suppliers to produce 250,000 trucks per year, and deploying its own production line capable of 125,000 trucks per year, Tesla has invested billions. And with only ~70,000 trucks delivered in roughly 3 years, it probably never made any positive cash flow from the program.

They are just in-between growth phases. No need to panic just because they have discontinued three vehicles and the last successful vehicle they launched was the Model Y in early 2020.

The Cybercab line gives Musk a forward-looking reason to wrap a product failure in. Tesla did the same thing when it ended Model S and Model X production this year to free up Fremont for Optimus. It’s a lot easier to say “we need room for the future” than “this didn’t sell.”

We’ve said it many times: the Cybertruck is genuinely impressive to drive. Steer-by-wire and rear-wheel steering are real engineering, and nobody else has put them in a production pickup. But Tesla built a 250,000-unit factory for a polarizing $75,000+ truck that can’t be sold in Europe or most right-hand-drive markets. It just cancelled it for Australia and New Zealand two weeks ago. The math was never going to work.

It’s truly a sign of Tesla today. Musk used to talk about having a more traditional electric truck design if Cybertruck fails, but I doubt we will see that. Again, Elon is betting the house on autonomy.

If you’re buying an EV, powering it with home solar can cut your fuel costs to nearly zero. With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. And with lease and PPA options, you can do it with zero upfront cost and start saving immediately. If you want to find the best deal, check out EnergySage. It’s a free service with hundreds of pre-vetted installers competing for your business, so you save 20 to 30% compared to going it alone. No sales calls until you pick an installer. #5084. Bank of America Joins XRP ETF Holders in New Q3 SEC Filing

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. Bank of America has disclosed a position in an XRP exchange-traded fund in its latest quarterly 13F filing with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission. The filing, dated Nov. 16, shows that the bank held 13,260 shares of the futures-based Volatility Shares XRP ETF (XRPI), worth about $106,600, at the end of the third quarter.

Bank of America held 13,000 shares of the fund in the first quarter and 13,260 shares in the second quarter, according to its earlier 13F filings. The same filing lists more than $685 million in BlackRock's IBIT, $13.58 million in Bitwise and $950,000 in VanEck.

Seven major banks disclosed XRP fund holdings worth a total of $29.4 million in filings published between Nov. 9 and Nov. 16. Italy's Intesa Sanpaolo disclosed the largest stake among them, at $27.21 million. The bank held 712,319 shares of the Grayscale XRP Trust ETF, worth about $20.52 million, and it also opened a new position of 402,750 shares in the Bitwise XRP ETF, worth about $6.7 million.

UBS and Morgan Stanley disclosed XRP fund holdings of $1.91 million and $116,000, respectively. Most of the UBS position was in the Volatility Shares XRP ETF, and Morgan Stanley held funds from Franklin, REX-Osprey and Bitwise.

JPMorgan Chase held 539 shares of the Bitwise fund, worth about $9,000, and National Bank of Canada held 330 shares of the same fund, worth about $5,500. Bank of Montreal held REX-Osprey and ProShares Ultra XRP shares worth $4,300.

The crypto portfolio of Goldman Sachs grew to $2.64 billion by the end of September, according to its 13F filing. The bank held about $1.28 billion in Bitcoin ETFs, and BlackRock's IBIT and Fidelity's FBTC accounted for $778 million and $398 million of that amount, respectively. Goldman also held about $1.22 billion in Ether funds, and BlackRock's ETHA accounted for $603 million of that amount.

Goldman held $144 million in XRP ETFs at the end of the third quarter, up from $87 million at the end of the second quarter. The bank's 13F filing for the first quarter showed no XRP or Solana ETF holdings, and Goldman still held no Solana funds at the end of September.

The bank held shares of five spot XRP funds from Franklin Templeton, Bitwise, Canary, Grayscale and 21Shares, and its Franklin Templeton position was worth almost $48 million.

Besides, trading firm Jane Street reported $23.5 million in XRP funds, and Simplex Trading disclosed a $14 million stake in the Grayscale fund. EP Wealth Advisors raised its Volatility Shares XRP ETF holding by 37% to 18,400 shares, worth about $148,000.

A 13F filing only shows long positions as of Sept. 30. It doesn't show whether the banks hedge those holdings with shorts, or what they did with the shares in October and November. Since then, XRP has fallen below $1, and on Tuesday morning, XRP was trading at $0.966.

#5083. Saylor’s Strategy Keeps Buying Bitcoin, Adding 'A Little Orange' Through the Dip

Saylor’s Strategy Keeps Buying Bitcoin, Adding 'A Little Orange' Through the Dip

Strategy kept buying Bitcoin through a brutal market stretch, adding 485 BTC worth roughly $33.4 million and repurchasing $228 million of its preferred stock, as chairman Michael Saylor urged the market to rally with a characteristically cheeky nudge.

The company disclosed the purchase in a filing Monday. The modest addition, small by the standards of a firm that once bought tens of thousands of coins at a time, lifted Strategy’s total holdings to 850,849 BTC as of Nov. 15, alongside $4.9 billion in U.S. dollar assets.

Strategy, the largest corporate holder of Bitcoin, remains far ahead of any other treasury company.

The buy caps a stretch of smaller, steadier accumulation paired with stock buybacks. Strategy recently added $82 million in Bitcoin while repurchasing $188 million of its STRC preferred shares, part of a shift toward what it calls a capital-management approach, buying back stock when it views the shares as undervalued rather than relentlessly stacking coins.

Bitcoin has slid sharply in recent days, trading well below the six-figure highs it touched earlier in the cycle, and the downturn has squeezed Bitcoin treasury firms whose valuations rise and fall with the asset.

Saylor, undeterred, posted the holdings update to X alongside the firm’s trademark Bitcoin tracker, and followed it with a quip aimed at the slumping market: “This dip could use a little orange,” a nod to Bitcoin’s signature color.

The purchase lands during a precarious moment for the treasury-company model Strategy pioneered. Index provider MSCI has finalized rules that could eject Bitcoin treasury firms like Strategy from its widely tracked equity indexes, classifying companies whose balance sheets are dominated by a single asset as “non-operating” and therefore ineligible.

Removal would force index-tracking funds to sell the affected shares, potentially pressuring prices and shrinking the premium these firms have long commanded over the value of their Bitcoin.

For now, Strategy is doing what it has always done, buying Bitcoin and talking it up, even as the market tests its conviction.

The combination of a small purchase and a sizable buyback underscores the balancing act: keep accumulating, defend the stock, and weather a downturn that has turned the once-unstoppable treasury trade into a far more fraught proposition.

#5081. Bitcoin's $90,000 Comeback Now Unlikely, Novogratz Warns

Galaxy Digital CEO Mike Novogratz said that the odds of Bitcoin hitting $90,000 by the end of 2026 are slim in light of prevailing macroeconomic headwinds and the growing likelihood that the Federal Reserve may hike interest rates.

During a live event with Anthony Scaramucci at the Digital Assets Forum in New York City, Novogratz was asked if Bitcoin could get back to $90,000 by the end of this year.

"Less likely than it was. You need something to happen," Novogratz said.

His gloomy outlook comes as Bitcoin has struggled to rebound after a devastating drop that saw its price dip as low as $62,000 over the weekend. Bitcoin's price stood at around $64,600 on Nov. 17. The largest cryptocurrency by market capitalization has declined for six consecutive weeks.

A shift in the U.S. monetary policy outlook, namely the prospect of more rate hikes from the Federal Reserve, has been largely to blame for Bitcoin's decline, according to Novogratz.

"At the start of the year, everybody thought the Fed was going to be cutting now," he said.

But high inflation and ongoing supply shocks have forced investors to readjust their expectations.

"Inflation's going up. We've got inflation going up," Novogratz said, warning that inflationary pressures could become increasingly entrenched.

"The Fed's going to hike. At least two more, probably three more," he said.

Novogratz predicted that the Fed would raise rates by 25 basis points in December and then hike again at its next meeting.

He said that higher rates, at least in the near term, tend to weigh on Bitcoin. But the higher cost of government borrowing could eventually provide support for the cryptocurrency.

"Yeah, later," he said in response to the suggestion that higher borrowing costs could be a tailwind for Bitcoin.

"First, you're gonna hike rates. Oh, sell gold, right? You're going to go to the dollar. It's so ingrained in peoples' heads," he said.

"Carry costs going up," he said. Bitcoin doesn't offer the same yield as interest-bearing dollar assets.

"You don't make any interest on Bitcoin in dollars," he said.

In particular, Novogratz said that the massive rise in Treasury yields has been particularly detrimental to the crypto market.

"Literally 40 basis points in like four days of trading. So that's why crypto's down," he said.

He also said that the massive crypto hack could still be weighing on investors' minds, adding to market jitters.

"I think in the back of their minds is the hack," he said.

#5080. BYD’s electric hypercar hits 312 mph, first production car over 500 km/h

BYD’s Yangwang U9 Xtreme just reached 503.17 km/h (312.66 mph) on a closed highway in Abu Dhabi, making it the first production car ever to go faster than 500 km/h.

The FIA and Guinness World Records both recognized the run. It’s the all-electric hypercar’s second top-speed record in just over a year, and the one it broke was its own.

BYD announced the record on X this morning, saying the U9 Xtreme “has done the impossible” and calling it “the fastest production car on the planet.”

Abu Dhabi closed a 10 km section of the Sheikh Khalifa Bin Zayed International Road (E11), in the emirate’s Al Dhafra Region, for the attempt. That gave the team a 7 km straight. Marc Basseng, the German racing driver who set the car’s previous record, was behind the wheel again.

It took two days. Basseng maxed out at 490 km/h on the first day, per the FIA. After an overnight round of changes to the rear wing, damping, and tire pressures, he got to 503.17 km/h on his second attempt of November 1.

The previous mark was 496.22 km/h (308 mph), set at Germany’s ATP Papenburg proving ground in September 2025. That run had already pushed the Bugatti Chiron Super Sport 300+ and its 490.48 km/h from 2019 off the top of the list.

And it comes just days after BYD released footage of a U9 Xtreme crashing into a guardrail at about 280 mph during an earlier high-speed test. The driver walked away unharmed. BYD went right back out and hit 457.89 km/h on the next run.

The U9 Xtreme is the track-focused version of the Yangwang U9, the flagship supercar from BYD’s luxury brand. It uses four electric motors, each rated at 555 kW and spinning up to 30,000 rpm, for 2,220 kW combined. That’s over 3,000 PS, or nearly 3,000 hp.

It runs on a 1,200-volt architecture with a track-grade version of BYD’s Blade Battery. Those are LFP cells, the same chemistry BYD puts in its cheapest city cars.

For context, the Rimac Nevera R, which snagged a pile of EV world records last year, tops out at 431.45 km/h. The U9 Xtreme also holds the production EV lap record at the Nürburgring Nordschleife at 6:59.157.

Only 30 will be built, and BYD is charging around $3 million apiece. Don’t expect to see one in the US, where BYD doesn’t sell passenger cars.

The biggest one: this is a single-direction run. The two Guinness titles listed for the attempt, highest speed by a production car and by an electric production car, are both specified as “single direction.” BYD’s post claims four Guinness records, but the FIA’s announcement only lists those two for this run.

Production car top speed records have traditionally been the average of two runs in opposite directions, within an hour, to cancel out wind and road grade. That’s how Koenigsegg got its 447.19 km/h record with the Agera RS in 2017.

Bugatti took heat for exactly this in 2019, when the Chiron hit 490 km/h one way on a pre-production car. Bugatti itself said it wasn’t an official record.

The car also ran on bespoke semi-slick tires developed with Giti Tire. It’s not clear whether the 30 customer cars will get them.

A Chinese automaker best known for $10,000 city cars now builds the fastest production car in the world, and it’s electric. Not long ago, the conventional wisdom was that EVs couldn’t sustain high top speeds because the battery and motors would overheat long before the car ran out of road. BYD just ran 503 km/h on LFP cells. LFP cells!

But we’d hold BYD to the same standard we held Bugatti to. A one-way run on a highway with a 7 km straight is a great demo, not a definitive record, but impressive nonetheless.

Roadster 2 will now never be a record holder. It simple can't compete with today's hypercars nevermind those in 2028 when we actually see one leave a factory. Although with Tesla's record, it's usually 4 years from demo to production so that makes it 2030 for the Roadster 2.

And either way, let’s be honest: this is about marketing. Nobody is going to drive one of these 30 cars at 500 km/h. But it works. It’s a halo for Yangwang ahead of its new ultra-luxury sedan and for BYD’s push into Europe, and it makes the “Chinese cars are cheap copies” crowd look out of date.

Meanwhile, Tesla promised a Roadster that would hit over 250 mph back in 2017. Nine years later, it’s still not in production.

The real question now is whether Bugatti or Koenigsegg answers with a two-way 500 km/h run. Koenigsegg says its Jesko Absolut can theoretically do it, but it has never attempted a run. Is BYD what finally makes them try?

If you’re buying an EV, powering it with home solar can cut your fuel costs to nearly zero. With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. And with lease and PPA options, you can do it with zero upfront cost and start saving immediately. If you want to find the best deal, check out EnergySage. It’s a free service with hundreds of pre-vetted installers competing for your business, so you save 20 to 30% compared to going it alone. No sales calls until you pick an installer. #5079. Stellar Floats XLM Rewards Pool After 1,000% Payments Jump

Home » Trendsetter News » Stellar Floats XLM Rewards Pool After 1,000% Payments Jump

Stellar is exploring an XLM rewards pool funded by network fees as monthly payment volume rises tenfold.

Stellar is considering a proposal to reward XLM holders who lock tokens and contribute utility to the network. Crypto market commentator All In Crypto highlights the idea in a video reviewing Stellar’s third-quarter webinar, asking whether rising network activity can translate into stronger demand for XLM.

The proposal would put network-generated fees into an XLM rewards pool. But Dixon, speaking during the webinar, stresses that it remains an early concept open to feedback, not an approved change or a promised payout.

“I’m not sure this is the right proposal,” she says, urging participants to read the accompanying paper and explore its interactive dashboard before commenting. Her central argument is that “utility should drive XLM.”

The webinar reports that monthly payment volume on Stellar rose from roughly $1.8 billion in January to $18.5 billion in October. October’s figure was 89% higher than September’s, following eight consecutive months of growth.

Another $7.5 billion reportedly moved during November’s first 10 days. Those figures describe money moving across the network, however, not purchases of XLM or returns accruing to token holders.

All In Crypto is openly bullish but acknowledges uncertainty over the token’s future value. That distinction matters: greater use of Stellar’s infrastructure does not, by itself, establish how much additional XLM users need to hold.

The webinar also describes a U.S. Bank pilot in which the bank issued a dollar-backed stablecoin called USBDC and used it to settle a cross-border payment between its North American and European entities. According to the presentation, minting, redemption, freezing and claw-back controls operated at the protocol level, without custom contract logic.

Stellar’s Project Throughput offers another glimpse of its ambitions. The webinar cites early benchmarks of roughly 1.2 million payments per second on one machine and about seven million across eight machines.

Those are technology-preview results, not reported production-network throughput. The project is available on Futurenet with open-source code for developers to test.

Now, the unresolved question is less whether Stellar can process more activity than how that activity benefits XLM.

The rewards proposal could create another connection between network use and token ownership, but its design, eligibility requirements and eventual adoption remain unsettled.

#5077. Hoskinson: Cardano Not Done Yet

Charles Hoskinson made sure to clear up misunderstandings about whether he thought the work on Cardano was done.

"I never once said Cardano is finished," Hoskinson said in a Nov. 15 post on X.

The comments came after he talked about the completion of Cardano's original roadmap, which raised some questions about his role in the blockchain's future.

He also called out the CEO of the Cardano Foundation, Frederik Gregaard, for not mentioning the Midnight chain when discussing everything the network has accomplished.

In his latest video, uploaded on Nov. 17, Hoskinson celebrated the fact that Midnight had just brought on board its first UK-based banking partner, Monument Bank.

Monument Bank is the first UK bank to offer interest-bearing tokenized retail deposits on a public blockchain, according to an announcement from the Midnight Foundation.

Up to £250 million in customer deposits will be added to Midnight in the first phase, and each tokenized deposit will be backed 1:1 by sterling cash held at the bank.

Monument Bank customers can now earn a fixed 5.2% annual equivalent rate on deposits for 12 months, all without having to deal with cryptocurrencies directly.

Hoskinson was very excited about the scale of the initiative, comparing the planned amount of deposits with the current decentralized finance ecosystem on Cardano.

"It's five times the TVL of Cardano," he said.

The £250 million is the planned initial size of the banking product, not a verified figure for how much is currently deposited on Midnight.

Hoskinson called this one of the most important milestones in his career.

"This is just one of the many things to come, but I think it's one of the most significant things of at least my career," he said.

He added that the team behind Midnight had moved the originally scheduled hard fork from December to November to accommodate the technical needs of Monument Bank.

Despite the milestone, Hoskinson emphasized that it's only the first step in a much larger mission: bringing traditional financial assets onto Midnight.

Hoskinson thinks the main barrier to cryptocurrency adoption is the lack of trust among consumers. He believes that simplifying the user experience without compromising regulatory requirements will allow billions of people to join the blockchain economy.

Hoskinson also shared that there's a big event coming up for the Midnight Foundation on Dec. 15. Developers will showcase future products and technological advancements scheduled for 2027.

#5076. Nvidia Stock Has a 20% Rebound in Sight—If Bulls Can Clear a Hurdle

Nvidia Stock Has a 20% Rebound in Sight—If Bulls Can Clear a Hurdle

If you were looking to buy Nvidia stock at a discount, last week may have been the best time to shop. Shares tanked following the Jensen Huang scandal involving an affair with an alleged Chinese spy, falling as low as $148.80.

On that drop, Nvidia shares touched a major support and then bounced after dropping 38% from its all-time high.

But if you didn’t buy the bottom, the second best time may be this week. The stock is trading at around $159, already up 8% from the lowest point last week. For traders relying on reading the charts, the current price of NVDA is running into another level that could decide whether the recovery has legs.

At around $159 on Monday, its weekly chart now places the $164.40 zone directly in the bulls' way.

That level previously acted as support, a price where buyers stepped in. After the breakdown, it is turning into a level to watch as investors who bought earlier may look to sell once they get their money back. But it could mark a bounce point based on the longer timeframes—as it has for at least three times since September 2025.

If Nvidia can reclaim $165 and hold above it, the next prominent resistance on the chart sits around $188 to $190. That would represent almost 20% upside from Monday's $159 price, although only about 16% from the breakout level, with possible selling pressure along the way.

Failure to clear $164.40 would put $152.62 back on the radar. Another break below that yellow support would weaken the case that last week's bounce marked a bottom.

The exponential moving averages, or EMAs, still offer some encouragement. Nvidia's 50-week EMA remains above its 200-week EMA, a bullish long-term configuration that gives more weight to recent prices but hasn't yet flipped bearish.

The shorter-term signals look less friendly. The Average Directional Index, or ADX, is around 23, suggesting a weakened trend (which is natural after the FUD episode). Its directional readings favor sellers. ADX measures trend strength, not direction by itself.

The Relative Strength Index, or RSI, is near 31.6, barely above the conventional oversold threshold of 30. That can make a relief bounce more plausible, but oversold stocks can keep falling. Neither indicator confirms a reversal yet.

Nvidia's underlying business hasn't suddenly stopped growing. The chipmaker reported $96.2 billion in quarterly revenue in August, up 106% year over year, including $89 billion from its data center business.

Investors will also be watching profit margins and the enormous financing commitments tying Nvidia to the wider AI infrastructure boom.

Nvidia co-founder Jensen Huang resigned on November 12 amid reports of extramarital affairs involving Alice Huang, a businesswoman U.S. intelligence suspects of spying for China, and Nvidia executive Kimberly Powell. Reuters subsequently reported a third alleged relationship with another employee.

Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress is serving as interim CEO while an independent investigation into possible foreign-intelligence targeting continues. The espionage allegations have not been established by an independent investigation, much less in court, but were powerful enough to oust the man who led Nvidia for 33 years in record time.

The Financial Times reported that Huang has spoken with allies about returning, while a petition calling for his reinstatement has attracted more than 178,000 signatures, including Elon Musk. The board has not announced plans to bring him back.

The argument sounds familiar: Huang may be too important to Nvidia to keep out. Sam Altman returned to OpenAI days after being ousted in 2023.

Other cases of CEOs too big to let go have been Steve Jobs with Apple, Jack Dorsey with Twitter and Starbuck’s Howard Schultz. Many of these leaders have turned into the face of their companies, making it hard for the markets to trust in any successor.

For bulls, $164.40 remains the line to beat, and charts point to this outcome being quite possible. Assuming, of course, Nvidia's next big reveal is about chips and not a fourth love affair.

The asset has reached the area of the previous recovery, which will most likely push the market to the limit, as most traders want to break even on their positions rather than keep losing money. A potentially better time to buy XRP could be tied to the overall market recovery, hence the occurrence of a better market catalyst, which doesn't exist at this point.

The confirmation of a market recovery would only come if we witness specific signs of a bull run on larger-cap altcoins: higher highs and constant inflows into institutional products like ETFs.

Dogecoin lost more than 30% of its value in the breakdown that started on October 8. It destroyed all the gains Dogecoin had made in the past: September's peak is gone, and the price dropped to the lowest level in 4 years. However, it's not all doom and gloom.

DOGE is trading at $0.05963, which is around 43% lower than the September peak near $0.105. Dogecoin has been slowly tumbling for weeks, showing a continuous bearish dynamic, and it is now below all the moving averages. Volume spiked during the drop and has faded over the last two days.

According to multiple long-term momentum indicators, Dogecoin is extremely oversold. RSI and MVRV both suggest a bounce coming in the foreseeable future. While it's not guaranteed, obviously, there is potential. Most altcoins didn't see any spikes in selling because they were already oversold. The $0.068 area, where Dogecoin held in July and August, is already lost.

The cryptocurrency market moves in cycles, and the recent risk-on cycle in the global economy was exclusive to AI and tech stocks. The crypto market, on the other hand, missed out on it, and with no substantial gains and increased pressure in November, we got the double hit that destroyed hopes for a quick recovery.

Crypto might benefit from a transitional period on the market, even though it's not guaranteed by any means, as both risk tolerance and available funds are running dry.

Solana has almost reached the threshold of $43 billion market capitalization, according to data from CoinMarketCap. The asset is slowly moving back into the bullish zone after it joined other altcoins in a massive price drop. That's around 70% of XRP's $61 billion.

Solana mostly followed the general uptrend but tumbled quickly due to the general reversal of the cryptocurrency market. SOL is trading at $73.75, down around 41% from the late September peak near $125. It dipped to approximately $67.5 during the drop and has bounced for two days, with RSI turning up from the oversold zone.

Every moving average on the chart sits above the price, with the nearest one around $87. Investors aren't buying en masse, but there is some accumulation, which could be tied to automated market making.

The correction on Solana is no different from other assets, and, unfortunately, SOL didn't show any additional resilience on the market.

From a price development perspective, the only positive outcome we are witnessing is the bounce from the August low zone, which might become support at some point, though that is not happening yet.

The dip below $70, which was the point of reversal back in August, is where some buy orders might be concentrated.

Currently, Solana is stuck in the approximately $70-$75 price range, and it seems unlikely to break out until it gets back to the moving averages at around $94-$96.

#5073. XRP’s 10-Year RSI Low Fuels Debate Over a Bottom

Home » Trendsetter News » XRP’s 10-Year RSI Low Fuels Debate Over a Bottom

Analysts see possible recovery signals, but extreme oversold conditions don’t confirm a reversal: further deficit remains possible.

XRP’s daily relative strength index has fallen to its lowest reading since 2016, according to chart analysis cited by Matt, the cryptocurrency YouTuber presenting the new Moon Lambo video episode.

His central argument: extreme selling pressure may signal an opportunity, even as XRP price trades below $1 and investors remain deeply fearful.

In the recording, dated November 17, 2026, Matt puts XRP just below $0.96 and Bitcoin at $64,571. He also cites a Crypto Fear and Greed Index reading of 12 out of 100, firmly in “extreme fear” territory. Those figures are presented in the video rather than independently verified market data.

The most striking figure comes from Crypto Insight UK: an XRP daily RSI of 16.17, described as its lowest in a decade. Matt acknowledges the limitation directly: “Just because we’re oversold doesn’t mean we can’t go lower.”

The weekly chart offers a less straightforward signal. Citing analyst D Greg Crypto, Matt describes XRP’s weekly RSI falling below 20, recovering that level and then quickly testing it again—an unusually compressed sequence compared with previous recoveries.

Under that analysis, holding above 20 could support a potential double-bottom momentum structure. A sustained break below it would instead leave room for further capitulation. The distinction matters: an extreme indicator reading is not the same thing as a confirmed price bottom.

Matt also highlights Charting Guy’s view that XRP resembles a previous cycle’s bottoming process, with a weekly bullish divergence and a possible re-accumulation pattern. That analyst maintains a minimum xrp price target of $4.36, not a forecast for the eventual peak.

The timing is considerably less certain. Charting Guy suggests possible altcoin seasons in summer 2027 and late 2028, while Matt argues that Bitcoin’s direction will remain important for XRP and the wider market.

Matt’s own response is to keep holding rather than trade the volatility. He identifies himself as an enthusiast without a financial background, not a financial adviser.

For now, the consequential question is whether exhausted selling develops into sustained buying. Moon Lambo presents several arguments for recovery, but its own weekly-RSI discussion leaves a clear risk unresolved: XRP price could remain under pressure before any broader market rebound takes hold.

#5072. Zoomlion says its new, 1,600 hp hybrid tractor is the world’s most powerful

Chinese equipment brand Zoomlion showed up to this week’s China International Agricultural Machinery Exhibition with a new ultra-high-horsepower hybrid tractor that sets a new benchmark, offering more than 1,600 hp while cutting back on both fuel and fumes.

Zoomlion calls its new 9DX hybrid tractor a radical rethink of what a modern ag unit can be, combining the all-day pulling power of a conventional diesel machine with the efficiency, accuracy, and instant torque benefits of electric drive. The company claims its hybrid setup can reducing fuel consumption, emissions, and deliver lower operating costs, without asking massive industrial farming operations to walk away from diesel entirely.

The tractor itself gets a headline-grabbing 1,600 hp Zoomlion 9DX features an 800V hybrid electric powertrain and continuously variable transmission that delivering a peak torque of 7,200 Nm (~5,300 lb-ft) of torque. Getting all that power to the ground is a four track tank-drive system riding on Zoomlion’s in-house developed 762 mm (~30 inch) wide tracks that are designed to distribute the tractor’s considerable weight for maximum traction and minimum soil compaction. What’s more, the high-traction treads can push the big machine to hit a maximum speed of 40 km/h across grades and slopes of up to 12 degrees.

The 9DX also gets a high-capacity hydraulic system capable of delivering 636 liters per minute of flow (~170 GPM), allowing it to work with some seriously heavy duty implements like ultra-wide seeders and variable-rate fertilizer applicators.

The Zoomlion 9DX was one of several new machines at this year’s exhibition, which hosted more than 3,000 exhibitors and vendors – but the big hp numbers all over the show made an even bigger impression, leading AGCO’s director of global electronic and electrical engineering, Nicolas Meunier, to tell Chinese State news agency Xinhua that the country had moved on from producing mostly low-horsepower tractors to building some seriously large machines.

There’s no official word on specs and batteries, but the company has developed an 800V modular battery pack system for its other equipment offerings that can scale from 140-800 kWh of capacity while other top-shelf Zoomlion tractors featuring an ag version of Cummins’ 15 liter turbodiesel.

The 9DX tops off what’s sure to be another record year for Zoomlion, which is coming off strong early export quarters shipping into Germany, Australia, and the United Arab Emirates (UAE).

#5071. Here’s how Ledger’s website was hit by the Optimizely malware attack

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On the 16th of November, Ledger’s public website was infected with malware designed to swap crypto addresses and steal card details. Security researcher Scott Helme traced the code to Optimizely, a website testing provider. His team found compromised scripts tied to 178 organizations, including Ledger, the BBC, and Zoom.

In practice, the malware could swap a copied crypto address for one belonging to the attacker. So if a visitor pasted that address and approved the payment, their crypto could go to the attacker instead.

So far, researchers have traced the malware to Ledger’s public website. Whether it also affected Ledger’s hardware wallets or the Ledger Wallet app remains unclear.

Just over a month earlier, though, Ledger had faced a separate attack involving physical devices. In that case, Ledger confirmed that some wallets sold through a reseller contained unauthorized hardware.

At the time, AMBCrypto reported that the October theft had reached $91 million across more than 470 crypto addresses.

Report URI recorded the cleanup of Ledger’s script at 20:41 UTC that day. Early on the 17th of November, Optimizely said,

We’ve fixed the vulnerability and removed the injected code from affected projects.

Report URI traced about $7,040 in crypto to addresses linked to the attack on the 16th of November. When Galaxy Digital’s Alex Thorn looked at where the money came from, he estimated that victims sent about $2,300. He said,

Most of the rest was the attacker’s own money. The attacker appears to have been very sloppy with their onchain hygiene

However, the number of Ledger visitors who lost funds remains unclear.

A similar breach had hit Polymarket a few weeks earlier. In that case, the malicious code could act on behalf of traders who were already signed in.

On the 29th of October, it sold some traders’ positions and moved funds out of their wallets.

Polymarket paused international trading to stop further losses. It later announced $3.9 million in reimbursements, including about $1.2 million for losses during the pause. By the 30th of October, the company said it had fully refunded 689 of the 726 affected wallets.

#5069. Fed Is Modeling AI 'Super Intelligence' Scenarios, Including Job Losses and a Stock Market Slump

Fed Is Modeling AI 'Super Intelligence' Scenarios, Including Job Losses and a Stock Market Slump

Federal Reserve Chair Kevin Warsh said Monday that central bank staff have started modeling how artificial intelligence could reshape the U.S. economy, including scenarios where the technology raises unemployment even as the economy grows.

Speaking at the Fed's Productivity and Jobs conference in San Francisco, Warsh described three possible paths: a productivity boom benefiting workers, a boom that leaves workers behind, and an AI investment bust that drags down stocks and economic growth.

“These are illustrative scenarios, not forecasts,” Warsh said, explaining that staff are using simplified economic models to test what might happen over the next several years. The Fed is preparing for different outcomes, not predicting which one will materialize.

Warsh called the technology “super intelligence,” or SI. That language is now official Fed terminology after an October change following a Donald Trump administration executive order, rather than a scientific determination that today's AI systems have already surpassed human intelligence.

In the most optimistic scenario, AI helps companies produce more with the same resources, raising the country's economic output above its historical growth rate. Workers share in those gains, and unemployment stays close to the level economists associate with a healthy job market.

In the second scenario, AI still makes the economy grow faster, but businesses automate jobs more quickly than displaced workers can find new ones. Unemployment rises despite the productivity gains.

The third scenario assumes AI investments fail to deliver the returns Wall Street expects. Companies spend heavily on infrastructure, stock prices rise on expectations of huge profits, and those expectations eventually prove too optimistic.

“Equity valuations could fall, leading to an economic slowdown,” Warsh said. In that scenario, unemployment rises more sharply, and the economy eventually recovers without the promised productivity boost.

Those risks are already receiving attention elsewhere at the Fed. Governor Michael Barr warned Monday that the AI construction boom is increasingly financed through complex arrangements, including long-term computing leases, energy contracts and financial guarantees.

The Fed estimates that the potential maximum value of these off-balance-sheet obligations now exceeds $3 trillion. Those commitments are not the same as debt already owed, but they show why disappointing AI revenues could create problems beyond the technology companies building data centers.

Warsh did not propose an interest-rate change in Monday's remarks. The Fed's next policy meeting is scheduled for December 8 and 9.

#5067. Bitwise CEO Urges Americans to Hold Crypto as Banking Giants Go Down

Following multiple technical outages at major U.S. banks that left thousands of people unable to access banking services or make transactions, Bitwise CEO Hunter Horsley has urged Americans to keep some of their funds in crypto or stablecoins as a backup plan.

"Rolling bank outages in the AI era," he said.

"Consider having some stablecoins or crypto just in case."

Large U.S. financial institutions such as JPMorgan Chase, Bank of America and Wells Fargo experienced widespread complaints about technical outages on Nov. 16.

Customers said they were having trouble logging into their online accounts, viewing their balances and conducting transactions.

According to Fox News, the incident affected some of the biggest banking names in the U.S. Thousands of people reported the issue via the outage-tracking platform Downdetector.

Zelle, a popular payment service that people use in the U.S. to transfer funds between bank accounts, was also reportedly affected.

As of the evening of Nov. 16, Chase had fixed the technical issue, according to the bank. It said transactions were back to normal.

The outage reports initially pointed to multiple banking systems going down at the same time. But further investigation revealed that problems at Chase spilled over into transactions with other banks. However, the Zelle app itself continued to work.

Stablecoins such as USDT and USDC aim to track the value of the U.S. dollar as closely as possible. They can be an option for those looking to hold digital funds without the extreme price fluctuations of crypto assets like Bitcoin.

For instance, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of Chicago, participants in blockchain networks can transfer dollar-based stablecoins 24/7. However, cashing them out into traditional money may still require access to traditional financial institutions.

#5066. Ripple CTO Emeritus Reveals Why XRP Took BTC’s Code

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple CTO Emeritus Reveals Why XRP Took BTC’s Code

Here’s why XRP’s Ledger still supports the original elliptic curve that Bitcoin uses for signatures.

David Schwartz, Ripple’s CTO Emeritus and one of the original architects of the XRP Ledger, explained a deliberate early design choice this week.

Responding to a post about the ledger’s technical roots, he wrote: “We did things that way in the very early days because we didn’t want to face potential criticism that our choices of those core crypto functions were less secure than bitcoin’s.”

The discussion began with XRPL validator Vet, who noted that the ledger’s earliest code included helper functions and comments from Bitcoin’s original client, written by Satoshi Nakamoto and copied by early developer Jed McCaleb.

Did you know all of us on the XRP Ledger use design choices by Satoshi daily?15 years ago, the XRP Ledger started out using some Bitcoin code, including helper functions from the Bitcoin client that Satoshi wrote.Jed copied Satoshi's timekeeping code into the XRPL, including… pic.twitter.com/IZOaa4w0bz

Most of that code was removed by the end of 2014; in December of that year, then-engineering lead Nik Bougalis deleted the last of the timekeeping routines along with one of Satoshi’s comments.

Two design choices remain. The XRP Ledger still supports the secp256k1 elliptic curve that Bitcoin uses for signatures (it also supports the newer Ed25519 curve, so not every account relies on secp256k1).

Separately, every XRPL address is still generated the Bitcoin way: the public key is hashed with SHA-256 and then with RIPEMD-160 before encoding. Those steps are used by every account on the network today.

David Schwartz’s explanation is straightforward. In the network’s first years, the team preferred to reuse cryptographic primitives already proven in Bitcoin rather than introduce untested alternatives that crypto critics, particularly in the Bitcoin community, could attack as weaker or experimental.

We did things that way in the very early days because we didn't want to face potential criticism that our choices of those core crypto functions were less secure than bitcoin's.

Using the same curve and address-generation method removed one potential line of criticism before it could be raised. Ripple CTO Emeritus has noted it is impossible to know whether different choices would actually have drawn that criticism; the point was to avoid having to defend the cryptography at all.

The early code sharing was limited and temporary. The consensus mechanism, transaction model, and most of the ledger’s architecture were built independently.

It's hard to say. Obviously, we never got those criticisms. But it's hard to know if we would have. I'm not sure we would have come up with better ones had we made our own decisions anyway.

What survived are two well-understood cryptographic building blocks that have remained secure and widely implemented for more than a decade. Later additions, including Ed25519 support, gave users and developers additional options without removing the original path.

The exchange is a small window into how the XRP Ledger was constructed: deliberately conservative on core cryptography in its first years, then gradually cleaned of borrowed code once the project had its footing. The two pieces are still in daily use by everyone on-chain.
