#5064. Liebherr expands excavator range with new, 250-tonne electric model

It takes some seriously heavy equipment to make an ultra-class mining truck look little, but that’s exactly what German equipment giant Liebherr does best, and its newest, 250-tonne production excavator keeps that tradition alive – only this time, it’s electric.

Liebherr began converting its diesel-powered mining excavators to electric in limited numbers back in 2024, and electric power has proven to be effective – moving millions of tons of dirt while saving millions of dollars in fuel and maintenance costs. Now, the company is translating that testing experience into regular production practice with the launch of a new, all-electric machine. Meet the R 9300 G8-E.

Based on the proven R 9300 G8 platform, the new Liebherr R 9300 G8-E packs an 850 kW (~1,140 hp) electric drivetrain designed to IEC standards and supports 6-6.6 kV power supplies. The company says its electric setup offers customers the same hydraulic power and bucket capacity (1.8 tonnes/cubic meter, or ~1.5 short tons/cubic yard) as the diesel version.

All that electric power ships with advanced electronics and operator assistance systems designed to maximize both efficiency and safety while giving mine operators more data to work and plan with. Liebherr describes its take on ADAS thusly:

The R 9300 G8-E comes with Liebherr Power Efficiency – Hydraulic Control as standard, which adapts hydraulic power to suit the machine’s load profile, reducing overall engine load factor. Thanks to its Generation 8 electronic architecture, the R 9300 G8-E is compatible with a range of Liebherr’s IoMine technology products, including its range of excavator Assistance Systems, the semi-autonomous Bucket Filling Assistant, the Skyview vision system as well as the data visualisation tool, Data View. Customers also have the option of installing Liebherr’s autonomous cable reel solution onto the excavator to assist with cable management and support machine mobility on site.

Like its repowered cousins, the big electric excavator does seem to have on-board batteries, but is primarily driven and operated with grid power, reducing charging and re-charging concerns and enabling round-the-clock operations for as long as grid power holds.

Liebherr touts the benefits of new R 9300 G8-E’s quiet, emissions-free running throughout its official announcement, but doesn’t stop there. The company says its EV, which makes do with thousands of fewer moving parts in its drivetrain, can also reduce maintenance requirements and associated operating costs in certain applications and site conditions while improving workplace conditions for the people who work in and around the machine.

Operating electric machinery inside mines must be mindblowingly good. Location is quite fixed, so you can just lay a HV cable to the grid with a spool at the vehicle end for local mobility. No noise, no vibration, no dirty emissions that have to be ventilated out for you not to die, instant torque, massive power and less maintenance on top?

The electric drivetrain also reduces vibration inside the cab, improving operator comfort, limiting fatigue, and potentially contributing to a safer working environment.

#5063. XRP Recovery: Price Prints First Uptrend Since September

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP has finally printed a local reversal: the price is forming one higher high after another. There's a good chance this quick recovery turns into something more significant sooner than we anticipate. XRP is trading at $0.9927, up around 11% from the $0.89 low of the drop. The price is back at the $0.99 area, which was the August low.

Hopefully, bulls pick up the rally, though for now the momentum is slow. The descending volume on the price upside suggests fading. The divergence of moving averages is an anti-volatility signal right now rather than anything else. All of them still sit above the price, with the nearest one around $1.13.

Multiple indicators suggest a retrace, but the overall market picture is grim, to say the least. XRP hadn't been below $1 since mid-August, when it dropped under that level for the first time since November 2024. It mostly followed the general uptrend but tumbled quickly due to the general reversal of the cryptocurrency market.

The worrying part is, ironically, the recovery of RSI, which is slowly moving above the oversold zone and opens up the way for an additional leg down. RSI is around 33 now, up from its low near 27.

The issue is the historic pattern XRP is forming: a constant downtrend with occasional recoveries that quickly turn into new breakdowns. In early November, XRP bounced from around $1.20 to the $1.30 area. The breakdown that followed took the price down by around 30% in under two weeks.

The downtrend is way stronger than the bullish reversal attempts XRP had earlier this month. Unfortunately, it can create a sustainable downtrend that can last for months, and considering XRP's price of $0.99, this is not something investors want.

The market is weak, and it might get weaker. The confirmation of a market recovery would only come if we witness specific signs of a bull run: higher highs and sustained buying.

#5060. Marathon Digital Cannot Stop Selling Bitcoin

Bitcoin mining giant MARA Holdings, formerly known as Marathon Digital, continues to offload its Bitcoin holdings, with another multimillion-dollar transfer spotted as the company's selling spree shows no signs of slowing down.

According to blockchain analytics platform Lookonchain, MARA transferred another 366 BTC, worth approximately $23.33 million, from its wallets on Nov. 18.

The latest transaction follows a series of substantial Bitcoin sales by the mining company throughout 2026.

Lookonchain estimates that MARA has already sold a staggering 40,581 BTC this year, generating approximately $3.03 billion at an average selling price of $74,446 per coin.

However, it remains unclear whether the latest 366 BTC transfer represents another completed sale or an internal movement of funds.

MARA's aggressive selling strategy is confirmed by its latest quarterly financial disclosures.

According to its Nov. 9 SEC filing, the company sold approximately 26,984 BTC for $1.9 billion during the first nine months of 2026.

Following the end of September, MARA liquidated another 13,597 BTC at an average price of approximately $81,039, raising an additional $1.1 billion.

These transactions bring its total disclosed Bitcoin sales to 40,581 BTC.

Notably, the company used $750 million from its latest sales to fully repay Bitcoin-backed loans, releasing 17,477 BTC previously pledged as collateral.

Interestingly, MARA has explicitly signaled that it intends to continue selling Bitcoin as part of a broader shift in its corporate strategy.

However, it began allowing sales of newly mined Bitcoin in 2025 before expanding the policy in 2026 to include coins already held on its balance sheet.

In its latest shareholder letter, MARA said it plans to continue monetizing Bitcoin to strengthen its financial flexibility.

The company specifically identified artificial intelligence infrastructure, upcoming convertible debt obligations and potential share repurchases as reasons for building up cash reserves.

The selling comes as MARA faces considerable pressure on its core mining business.

Third-quarter revenue declined 35% year over year to $164.2 million, compared to $252.4 million in the corresponding period of 2025.

#5059. Trump Backs Jensen Huang’s Return to Nvidia as Sam Altman Says 'Heroes Have Flaws'

Trump Backs Jensen Huang’s Return to Nvidia as Sam Altman Says 'Heroes Have Flaws'

President Donald Trump joined Elon Musk on Monday in supporting Jensen Huang’s return as Nvidia CEO, while OpenAI chief Sam Altman expressed empathy for the ousted co-founder without endorsing his reinstatement.

“I would certainly not mind seeing that,” Trump told reporters Monday as he left the White House for Asia, according to Reuters. “He could return. There’s a big group of people that would like him to return.”

Trump said Huang had “learned a big lesson,” and questioned claims that Alice Huang was a Chinese spy. “Nobody’s proven that she’s a spy,” he said, per The Guardian.

Trump also dismissed concerns that his ties with Huang threatened national security and defended Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick amid questions over enforcement of AI-chip export controls.

Musk also said Friday he was signing a petition demanding Huang’s reinstatement.

“A petition is underway to reinstate Jensen and I’m signing it too,” Musk wrote on X. Epic Games boss Tim Sweeney and Replit CEO Amjad Masad are also listed among the supporters.

More than 182,000 people had signed the open letter by Monday. It seeks Huang’s return during an independent investigation, although signatories’ identities and affiliations are not independently verified.

Speaking at Semafor’s Silicon Valley & The World conference Monday, Altman said Huang had “long been a hero of mine. Heroes have flaws.” He described feeling “personal strong empathy” for the former Nvidia boss.

He has reason to understand a boardroom revolt. OpenAI fired Altman in November 2023, only to reinstate him less than five days later after employee and investor backlash. Still, asked whether Huang should return, Altman said: “I don’t think I know enough to answer that question.”

Altman said he struggled to imagine Nvidia without Huang, but argued the giant chipmaker was big enough to retain investor trust. That is sympathy—not an endorsement.

Huang, who founded Nvidia in 1993, stepped down Thursday after admitting infidelity. Reports later linked him to Alice Huang and two Nvidia employees, one a subordinate.

Alice Huang previously worked at Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer facing scrutiny over possible diversion of advanced chips to China. Megaspeed denies wrongdoing; espionage allegations remain unproven.

Washington is tightening export controls on advanced AI chips, including new licensing requirements for shipments to Singapore and Malaysia, as officials seek to prevent diversion to China.

Trump’s backing also puts him at odds with Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent, who said Sunday that Nvidia should look outside its ranks for a replacement. “They need a dramatic shake-up,” Bessent told Fox News.

Nvidia’s board has made no decision to reinstate Huang. CFO Colette Kress is serving as interim CEO, while the Financial Times reports Huang’s allies are lobbying for his return and Arm CEO Rene Haas is a potential successor.

Employees have also pushed for reinstatement. An internal discussion drew more than 1,600 comments, including calls for Huang’s return.

Nvidia shares opened 2.7% lower Monday, wiping out roughly $100 billion in market value at the open.

Huang could face congressional questions on China chip exports in early December. Nvidia reports earnings Tuesday, its first financial update under Kress.

#5058. Top 7 cloud mining platforms of November 2026

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Cloud mining allows users to mine cryptocurrencies by renting computing power. They can mine crypto without buying or owning any equipment or worrying about its maintenance. Mining is an essential part of the cryptocurrency space, and with cloud mining, the space is opened up for people with little to no technical knowledge.

Cloud mining provides an alternative to anyone in the crypto space who wants to participate in Bitcoin mining without buying any expensive mining equipment or managing hardware. Here is our list of the top 7 cloud mining platforms of the month:

NiceHash has a marketplace that provides an alternative to conventional cloud mining contracts instead of functioning as a fixed-term mining provider. Instead of functioning as a fixed-term mining provider, it connects hashrate buyers and sellers through a marketplace.

For users who want to understand mining costs and rewards and monitor prices, it is a great option. The platform also supports proof-of-work algorithms and lets users mine Bitcoin and other PoW assets through pools of their choice, where payouts are settled in BTC.

KuMining is associated with the KuCoin ecosystem and gives users a chance to explore cryptocurrency mining contracts without having their own mining machines. Users can compare various available mining contracts in terms of hashrate pricing, mining durations, and choose the right one depending on their requirements.

Since KuCoin is an established exchange ecosystem, KuMining gained a lot of attention through it. Users who understand exchange-based accounts and digital asset transactions can find the platform to be useful. It is a good option for existing crypto traders who want to use mining services related to an exchange.

ViaBTC is popular and known for its mining pool services and lets users connect their mining equipment to a shared mining pool and take part in cryptocurrency reward distribution. Most of the platform’s services are for users who are already part of mining hardware operations and want to establish a pool rather than attempting to mine on their own.

Multiple cryptocurrencies and mining-related tools on the platform allow users to monitor account balances, payout information, and mining performance. ViaBTC can be great for anyone who wants to research professional mining operations and alternative mining pool services.

Hashing24 is for users who want to focus on accessing mining capacity through contract-based arrangements. For anyone wanting to be a part of cloud mining without buying ASIC hardware or managing it.

It has been a part of the cryptocurrency space since 2012 and focuses primarily on Bitcoin mining. Users can buy mining power that is measured in terahashes, with contracts running over different periods of time depending on the plan.

BeMine has fractional ownership of ASIC mining hardware rather than relying on renting traditional cloud mining contracts. The mining company is based on the principles of security and transparency. Mining can be done without the owner’s direct participation.

Users can also buy 1/100 of a share of any specific ASIC, which can be a viable option for anyone who wants to start mining without buying an expensive machine entirely.

BitFuFu is another popular option for users, and it operates in the Bitcoin mining infrastructure sector and offers options for miner sales, mining equipment hosting, and cloud mining. The platform aims to empower the global Bitcoin network and offers a one-stop solution for a cheap and easy way to earn Bitcoin.

There is an online interface that lets users monitor mining-related information and account activity, making it easy and convenient for those who are new to the space.

Bitdeer provides cloud hashrate services, infrastructure services, and mining hardware solutions for a wide range of users. The cloud mining products let users buy computing power without buying or maintaining any mining machines. They can also choose the hashrate capacities and mining durations that suit their requirements well.

The company is also involved in large-scale mining infrastructure and operates mining facilities and provides services connected to cryptocurrency mining hardware.

Cloud mining is an alternative and interesting way for users to become a part of the cryptocurrency space. While the crypto mining space may look daunting for a lot of people, cloud mining provides an easy segue into it.

Every platform mentioned on this list has its own advantages; however, it is advised to do your own research before investing.

#5057. Vitalik Buterin Wants Crypto to Be More Like Buying a House

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin has said that crypto needs some of the features that made housing a safe investment for earlier generations. "We need the equivalent of those good properties that housing investment has, for modern digital assets," Buterin wrote in a post on X on Nov. 18.

The post came a day after economist Peter Tulip published his own post on housing in Raleigh, North Carolina. According to Tulip, looser zoning rules in the city set off a construction boom and pushed real rents down 15% between 2022 and 2025.

In his post, Buterin first pointed to a benefit of young people putting their savings into stocks and crypto instead of real estate.

"One benefit of younger generations investing more in stonks and other digital things instead of housing is that it reduces perverse incentives that drive people to push to keep housing expensive, instead of rejoicing when prices drop as we do for all other life necessities," he wrote.

Still, the Ethereum co-founder said that housing has had one advantage over stocks and crypto. He described it as "a culture and policy-encouraged default of investing in a safe non-crazy thing that you can't easily liquidate."

"Modern platforms encourage the opposite of this, leading to more irresponsible behavior," Buterin added.

Ether was trading at $1,808 on Wednesday.

Buterin has written about housing and urban planning before. He has supported the YIMBY movement for denser development in cities for several years.

In 2022, Buterin argued for a land value tax. According to him, such a tax would lower the upfront cost of buying a home and discourage speculation on vacant lots.

In 2025, the Ethereum co-founder called the housing market a "confusing market of peculiar NFTs." According to Buterin, what is really being sold under the label of square footage is the right to live in a particular city. At the time, he also said that legalizing dense development speeds up the economy and benefits even people who prefer single-family homes.

Home » Altcoins » Solana (SOL) » Solana’s $1,100 Price Target Hinges On Tokenized Stocks

Additional priority fees and tips to boost Solana’s chain revenue, placing it among the strongest tokenized market contenders.

With Solana (SOL) changing hands just above $70, Bitwise’s new long-term prediction of $1,100 sounds utterly bullish. However, the experts behind the financial behemoth back this up with a new shift that has just started to manifest itself.

Bitwise’s recent report states that roughly 5% of all global stocks are likely to be tokenized. Real Economic Value (REV), the key metric defining how much actual fees users pay the network, comes into play as a long-term utility hallmark.

Solana’s (SOL) tokenized-stock related trading activity now consumes approximately 2% of all turnover, while Bitwise expects this niche to be responsible for majority of on-chain operations by 2031. In the next five years, Solana is expected to compete against Ethereum’s Layer-2’s.

The cumulative $11.68 million inflow this week across Solana’s Spot market price-tracking ETFs has broken the 6-week long downward trend of continuous outflows due to investors selling off positions. As they increase shares again, bulls are looking for bottom confirmation at $70.

The estimated $152 trillion in global listed-company value could translate into a hefty liquidity boost when tokenized on the blockchain.

#5055. MEXC Announces Global Card Service Discontinuation

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

MEXC confirmed that its Global Card service will be discontinued on November 18, according to the exchange's announcement. The company cites service adjustments by its third-party card issuer. From the deadline, existing cards can no longer be used for transactions, and new card applications are suspended.

Users have two things to take care of before the service ends.

First, the remaining card balance has to be withdrawn: the Withdraw button on the Global Card page sends the funds to the Fiat account.

Second, subscriptions linked to the card should be cancelled or switched to another payment method. Unfortunately for cardholders, recurring payments will most likely fail if they stay on the old card.

If there's still a balance when the service ends, MEXC will credit it to the Spot account, so users will simply receive their funds back. It's important to keep in mind that pending card transactions need time to settle, which is why the balance snapshot comes on November 21, three days after the shutdown. The amount will be credited within three business days of the snapshot. Users do not need to take any action.

Refunds for previous card transactions will be credited to the Fiat account once MEXC receives them. Pending cashback will be credited automatically as scheduled. Elite VVIP users with zero-fee benefits will be refunded too, though the eligible fees return to their Spot accounts only before December 15.

Transaction history, card orders and cashback records stay available on the Global Card page for three months after the service ends, so it's worth saving anything needed before access ends.

Cryptocurrency services delegate certain types of services to third parties all the time, so witnessing a similar situation is more than a possibility here. Realistically, the shutdown is somewhat inconvenient rather than dangerous, as the balances are returned automatically. For users: stay vigilant and try not to put all your eggs in one basket.

#5054. Solana Foundation takes ‘majority interest’ in CoinGecko – What changes next?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The Solana Foundation now holds a “majority interest” in CoinGecko, its Chief Product Officer Vibhu Norby said on X.

CoinGecko described the arrangement as “backing” when it announced the news at Breakpoint in London on the 17th of November. Neither announcement gave the exact stake, price, or governance terms.

Four days earlier, CoinGecko had promoted independent ownership as a reason to choose its data service over Binance-owned CoinMarketCap. It now plans to explore trading alongside its market data.

The Foundation said it planned to bring Solana-powered trading to CoinGecko’s website, app, and GeckoTerminal. Notably, CoinGecko said it was still exploring trading features.

For Solana, that could mean reaching traders where they already look up prices. CoinGecko draws well over 50 million monthly sessions, according to the Foundation.

On the 17th of November, Solana [SOL] recorded $50.44 billion in 30-day DEX spot volume, whereas Ethereum [ETH] recorded $53.86 billion.

Norby also flagged broader coverage of stocks and real-world assets [RWAs]. AMBCrypto reported that Solana’s RWA value reached $5 billion in October.

By the 12th of November, tokenized equity supply stood at $607 million, with xStocks accounting for 75%.

CoinGecko’s API guide warned that exchange ownership could raise questions about bias in rankings and trading volumes. AMBCrypto covered similar concerns when Binance bought CoinMarketCap in 2020.

After Norby’s disclosure, an X user called the new arrangement “a credibility problem.”

CoinGecko said its methods would stay “transparent, publicly accessible.” Readers can check how it calculates prices, but the announcements leave open who can approve changes to those rules.

Brera Holdings disclosed board rights in a separate 2025 deal involving Solmate. A later filing specified that the Foundation could nominate up to two directors, depending on board size. For CoinGecko, board seats and approval rights remain undisclosed.

#5053. Lucid teases its cheaper sub-$50,000 Cosmos EV in new video and images

Lucid has released a new teaser video of what looks like the Cosmos, its upcoming sub-$50,000 midsize electric SUV and the car that’s supposed to turn Lucid into a volume automaker.

The clip gives us the clearest look yet at the Cosmos’ lighting, front and rear, after months of camouflaged prototype sightings. But the car itself is still about a year away.

Lucid posted the 1 minute 34 second video on X yesterday. The caption talks about “elevating the experience of movement” and says Lucid can’t wait “to share this feeling with even more people.” It doesn’t name the vehicle, but “even more people” is about as subtle a hint at a cheaper model as you can get.

Two frames from the film show the vehicle at dusk. At the rear, “LUCID” is spelled out in red illuminated letters across the tailgate, flanked by thin red light strips, with a spoiler cutting across the base of the rear glass. Up front, a sculpted LED headlight with a row of vertical segments sweeps across a red body panel.

That rear end matches the Cosmos design Lucid registered with the EU Intellectual Property Office in May, which showed the same spelled-out “LUCID” tailgate and rear-glass spoiler. It also lines up with the thin red light strips on the camouflaged Cosmos prototype spotted on Highway 101 in California last month.

Lucid unveiled its plans for Cosmos and Earth at its first Investor Day in March. Both ride on the new midsize platform and start under $50,000, roughly half the price of the Gravity.

On paper, the Cosmos is impressive. Lucid claims about 300 miles of range from a 69 kWh pack, thanks to efficiency of up to 4.5 mi/kWh, which it says is 10% better than its closest competitor. It also claims 200 miles of range in 14 minutes of charging, and its new Atlas drive unit has 30% fewer parts and costs 37% less than the Zeus unit in the Gravity.

The problem is timing. Production was supposed to start in late 2026. In August, CEO Silvio Napoli pushed the Cosmos to 2027, “most likely the second half.”

That puts it on the same schedule as Rivian’s $45,000 base R2, and well after the Tesla Model Y it was caught testing next to in Arizona back in May.

Lucid needs this car badly. It delivered 3,806 vehicles in Q3, down 7% year-over-year, while building only 2,954 after cutting the second shift at its Arizona factory.

I want Lucid to succeed. Period. I love the Air Pure and would like to get one at some point, but the cost is out of my budget while the tech is more than I want or need.

But I think what impresses me more than anything with Lucid is that they are one of the few who actually speak efficiency. It seems that they actually care about being efficient where companies like GM and others just build vehicles with bigger batteries in order to get more range. So for that, I give Lucid much credit.

The company is building hype a year out. It has nothing else new to sell until the Cosmos arrives, and the company reports Q3 earnings on Monday. They need to remind everyone what they are working toward.

A midsize SUV doing 4.5 mi/kWh would be best in class by a wide margin, and Lucid’s efficiency claims have held up on the Air. If it really lands under $50,000 with 300 miles of range, it’s a very compelling alternative to the Model Y and the R2, which still have room to take over market shares from ICE vehicles.

But Lucid has already slipped this car once; it says its cash lasts “well into 2027,” and the Cosmos is due in the second half of 2027. That’s a tight overlap. I think the Cosmos will be a good car, but can Lucid fund it all the way to customers’ driveways?

#5052. Why Did MARA Sell $3B in Bitcoin in 2026?

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Why Did MARA Sell $3B in Bitcoin in 2026?

On-chain data and Q3 filings reveal a major shift in the mining giant’s treasury playbook.

On-chain alerts lit up after Bitcoin mining giant MARA Holdings moved another 366 BTC ($23.33 million), capping off a massive 2026 sell-off totaling 40,581 BTC worth $3.03 billion.

While a multi-billion-dollar miner sell-off sounds like a bearish warning sign for retail investors, MARA’s third-quarter 2026 financial disclosures reveal a calculated financial reset: the company sold near market highs to wipe out $1.4 billion in debt and unlock 17,477 BTC in collateral.

According to on-chain tracking data shared by Lookonchain, MARA transferred 366 BTC worth roughly $23.33 million out of its wallets while Bitcoin traded near the $63,700 level. The transfer immediately drew market scrutiny toward MARA’s broader 2026 treasury activity.

Bitcoin miner MARA Holdings (@MARA) continues to dump $BTC, transferring out another 366 $BTC ($23.33M) 8 hours ago.According to official disclosures, #MARA sold 40,581 $BTC ($3.03B) in 2026 at an average price of $74,446.https://t.co/fTOd8FQMxR pic.twitter.com/SQgTLUdrve

Per company disclosures and on-chain tallies, MARA has offloaded 40,581 BTC in 2026 for approximately $3.03 billion. The miner executed those sales at an average price of $74,446 per Bitcoin.

For years, publicly traded Bitcoin miners adhered to a strict “HODL everything” playbook, borrowing cash against their Bitcoin reserves to fund operations and hardware expansion. In 2026, MARA aggressively reversed that strategy.

In an official statement on X accompanying its Q3 2026 earnings release, MARA confirmed that it deployed $750 million from post-quarter Bitcoin sales to fully repay its Bitcoin-backed debt.

Clearing those credit facilities released all 17,477 BTC that had been pledged as collateral, bringing MARA’s total debt reduction since the beginning of 2026 to $1.4 billion—a 37% cut to its overall debt load.

After quarter-end, we used bitcoin sale proceeds to fully repay $750 million of bitcoin-backed debt, releasing all 17,477 bitcoin pledged as collateral.Since the beginning of 2026, we have reduced total debt by $1.4 billion, or 37%.Q3 letter: https://t.co/CQ2hTXcEV4 pic.twitter.com/CsnmtN2k0b

The company also highlighted its ambition to evolve from a pure-play Bitcoin miner into an integrated, energy-backed AI and digital infrastructure platform.

According to MARA, Bitcoin mining built the physical footprint, access to low-cost power created the opportunity, and AI/HPC and cloud services offer higher-margin, longer-duration, and more predictable returns. The company is now shifting from building capacity to commercializing it as an integrated “energy-to-compute” operator.

MARA’s energized hashrate reached 71.2 EH/s in Q3 (up 18% year-over-year from 60.4 EH/s), cementing its position as the largest publicly traded Bitcoin miner by computing capacity.

The company captures roughly 5.4% of all global Bitcoin miner rewards, producing 2,247 BTC and winning 644 blocks during the quarter—a slight dip from 5.9% in Q2 as overall network difficulty grew faster than MARA’s deployments.

At the end of Q3 2026, MARA held $3.8 billion in total liquidity, consisting of 34,012 BTC (valued at ~$2.8 billion) and $946.5 million in unrestricted cash.

MARA’s Bitcoin sales could add further downward pressure on the price in the short term, particularly as demand remains weak and U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded nearly $240 million in outflows yesterday.

However, the move could be positive from a longer-term perspective. By repaying $750 million in debt, MARA freed up 17,477 BTC previously pledged as collateral, significantly reducing the risk of forced liquidations if Bitcoin falls further.

MARA’s move reflects a broader shift across the Bitcoin mining industry. Publicly traded miners are no longer simply holding Bitcoin; they are actively managing their treasuries, reducing debt and expanding into energy and AI infrastructure. This could make the sector more resilient during market downturns and reduce the risk of forced Bitcoin selling.

#5051. Clarity Act Defeat ‘Failure of American Leadership’: Trump’s Former Crypto Chief

Clarity Act Defeat ‘Failure of American Leadership’: Trump’s Former Crypto Chief

The Senate's failure to pass the Clarity Act was "a failure of American leadership," according to Patrick Witt, President Donald Trump's former top crypto adviser.

"It was extremely disappointing," Witt said in an on-stage interview at Solana's Breakpoint conference in London. "I was knee deep in Clarity. I lived it for a year plus negotiating that bill," he noted, adding that the bill, "would have been incredibly impactful."

Witt served as executive director of the President's Council of Advisers for Digital Assets until last month and led the White House push for the bill, which would have set out a framework for regulating crypto markets. It failed a key procedural vote in the Senate in September.

"We ultimately lost against the clock," he said, adding that, "The policy was good. We resolved pretty much all of the policy differences." But with the midterm elections approaching, "the political issue of ethics really consumed everything else," he said.

Democrats had raised concerns over the Trump family's crypto ventures. Witt acknowledged that Trump's involvement in crypto was "arguably the issue," but said the president had agreed to two ethics provisions. The second would have barred federal officials, including the president, from issuing or sponsoring digital assets, forced the president to divest certain digital assets or put them in a blind trust, and let state attorneys general enforce violations. "There's no precedent for those kinds of things in American law," he said.

The GENIUS stablecoin bill also lost an initial procedural vote in 2025 but recovered because it came early in Trump's term, Witt noted. With Clarity, "we knew that we were going to get one shot at it, and unfortunately we just came up short," he said.

Looking ahead, Witt said the odds of passing a comprehensive replacement are low. "I would expect that market structure in the United States is going to be less written on Capitol Hill and more written in the Federal Register, which is where the agencies do their rulemakings and issue their guidance," Witt said.

Parts of the Clarity Act could still pass in larger legislative packages, and lawmakers could grant targeted powers, such as giving the Commodity Futures Trading Commission authority over spot crypto markets, he said.

Witt said regulators already have "ample authority," and that the administration had to "hold them back a little bit" while the bill played out. The SEC and CFTC have since moved ahead with their own crypto rules, though House Financial Services Committee Chairman French Hill (R-AR) has said those efforts fall short of what the legislation would have achieved.

SEC Chair Paul Atkins and CFTC Chair Michael Selig "are committed to moving markets on chain," Witt said, framing the issue as one of American competitiveness. "Capital is going to go where it's treated best," he said.

Despite Democrats winning control of both chambers of Congress in this month's midterms, Witt said he is "not bearish on the industry overall," since the regulators and the president remain in place. Democrats are reportedly planning to push their own, broader market structure bill.

He said opposition to crypto is increasingly generational rather than purely partisan. While some Democrats, such as Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA), are "going to be 100% opposed to anything that seemingly is favorable to crypto," younger lawmakers on both sides see it as "part of the fabric of financial infrastructure," he said, pointing to Sens. Angela Alsobrooks (D-MD) and Ruben Gallego (D-AZ).

Witt left the administration in October, The Hill reported. He said he has held talks with crypto firms, venture capital firms and defense companies, and hopes to announce his next move in the coming weeks.

#5050. Cardano Stablecoin DJED Faces Year End Shutdown, Here’s Date to Note

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Cardano-based stablecoin DJED has announced its sunset, as DJED and SHEN services will wind down on December 31, 2026, at 11:59 pm UTC.

DJED represents a crypto-backed, overcollateralized stablecoin protocol on Cardano whose US-dollar peg is maintained algorithmically against an ADA reserve (with SHEN as the reserve coin), rather than by fiat reserves.

"DJED and SHEN services will wind down at the end of December 31, 2026, at 11:59 pm UTC," a DJED-focused X account wrote.

ATTN: DJED & SHEN Holders DJED and SHEN services will wind down at the end of December 31, 2026 at 11:59pm UTC. Please redeem and convert any DJED and SHEN you hold before then. All redemptions are subject to the reserve ratio.

Users are urged to redeem and convert any DJED and SHEN they hold before this deadline. Users are to redeem on the protocol and convert their DJED and SHEN back to ADA on the official Djed page. Users who hold DJED or SHEN in liquidity pools, lending markets, or other DeFi platforms are urged to withdraw those positions first, then redeem or swap.

The Djed team said all systems are fully operational and redemptions are now open, subject to the reserve ratio. It further added that the Djed protocol will be maintained and remain available until December 31, 2026. After this date, all remaining funds will be allocated to the outstanding DJED/SHEN holders once the wind-down is complete.

Djed’s shutdown comes almost three years after its launch, which came in 2023. The stablecoin was officially launched on the Cardano mainnet on January 31, 2023, and was developed by COTI in collaboration with the Cardano ecosystem to be used on DeFi protocols on Cardano as a stable alternative to volatile cryptocurrencies.

In positive news for the Cardano ecosystem, Monument Bank Limited has launched tokenized deposits on the Midnight network, marking a milestone for the network and the decentralized finance ecosystem.

At present, Monument is the first UK bank to tokenize retail deposits on a public blockchain, offering clients a 5.2% AER fixed-term return on savings directly through its existing banking app, delivering institutional onchain finance through a familiar and user-friendly banking experience.

Up to £250 million of client deposits will be represented on Midnight in this opening phase, with deposits recorded one-to-one in sterling.

#5049. Dumping to stacking in just three months? What’s going on with XRP whales?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

XRP’s price dipped under $1, and most of the market looks ready to walk away. Not the whales, though. At least not yet.

According to recent data, XRP whales have taken out more than $127 million in XRP off Binance since 10th November. Net outflows went between $12-$33 million over the past week, including about $21 million on the 16th alone!

Not too long ago, it was the other way around.

In August, XRP went up by about 72% and back above $1.50. Whales were dumping as much as $75 million on Binance in a single day!

If you look further back, you’ll see that XRP hit $3.55 in the summer of 2025. In that time, some days saw more than $100 million flood in.

When coins leave an exchange, people usually want to hold. However, don’t get excited yet. Some of this might just be Binance moving its money around.

Remember when it was topping up its reserves after the October hack?

The rest of the market isn’t seeing it the way the whales are yet.

XRP closed last week at about $0.97 on the charts. That hinted at its lowest weekly close in two years. It even dipped as low all the way down to $0.89 during the week.

Still, it’s worth thinking. This is where XRP broke out in 2024, right before it sprinted past $3.60.

Futures traders don’t seem to want to be here. The OI dropped from about $1.05 billion on the 9th to around $752 million at press time. Funding went from red on the 14th and to a slim green at 0.0013%.

ETFs haven’t been helping either; $19.1 million in outflows between 9th and 13th November.

Everyone except whales have been heading for the door.

So, here’s the thing. If whales keep taking XRP out of Binance, there isn’t enough of them to be sold. That could help turn $1 into a floor.

Or the rest of the market could drag XRP down anyway. Only time will tell.

#5048. Binance Hacker Moves Over $105 Million of Stolen Ethereum

The transfer has raised concerns across the crypto community about what the hacker might be up to and about the possibility of Binance recovering the stolen funds, especially after they are moved to another wallet.

Although the source did not provide sufficient details about the highly concerning transfer, it showed that the attacker behind the 2026 Binance hack moved 60,000 ETH on Wednesday in 2026.

The broader crypto market has continued to suffer extreme volatility since the attack happened in late October. Hence, the total portion of Ethereum tokens transferred from the stolen stash is now worth $108.78 million at ETH’s current market price.

While the reason behind the sudden move after a few weeks of silence remains uncertain, further data showed that the assets were moved in five separate transfers of 12,000 ETH each.

Considering the history of the transferred funds, the move has sparked speculation that the hacker might already be preparing to spend the stolen funds, further sabotaging Binance’s chances of full recovery.

However, it is important to note that there is no evidence that the attacker has sold the Ethereum, as the receiving wallet has not been identified.

#5047. Satoshi Ally Adam Back Flags Bitcoin’s New $68,000 ‘Mathematical Floor’

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Bitcoin's 200-week moving average (200WMA) has climbed above $68,000 for the first time, Blockstream CEO Adam Back noted on X on Nov. 18. "Bitcoin passes $68k 200wma," he wrote. Back attached a 200-week moving average heatmap from Look Into Bitcoin, and the chart shows the indicator rising throughout Bitcoin's history. On Nov. 18, the indicator was between $67,955 and $68,145.

#bitcoin passes $68k 200wma https://t.co/dgIzS9Nsvw https://t.co/xzYdP0EvZh

Back is the cryptographer behind Hashcash. The algorithm laid the groundwork for proof-of-work, and Satoshi Nakamoto cited it in the Bitcoin white paper. Back was also one of the first people Satoshi contacted before the launch of Bitcoin in 2009.

The Blockstream CEO has posted about the 200WMA on X several times this year. On March 27, he wrote that the "200wma passes $59k." The indicator was just above $60,000 in early May, and on May 30, Back reported that it had crossed $61,000. In September, the 200WMA rose above $65,000 for the first time.

In a follow-up post in May, Back cited a maxim of the late Charlie Munger. According to Munger, an investor who buys only high-quality assets when they trade at the 200-week moving average would beat the S&P 500 over time. Back also noted that Munger and Warren Buffett never embraced Bitcoin.

In mid-September, Bitcoin was trading at around $76,000 to $77,000. At that time, the gap between the price and the 200WMA was more than $10,000.

On Wednesday, Bitcoin was trading at $64,150, about 6% below the 200WMA. On Nov. 15, BTC dropped as low as $62,000 before bouncing 5.5%. Back called the move "bouncy" in a post on X.

In a CNBC interview on Nov. 12, Back said that Bitcoin could reach $1 million by 2028. According to Back, more than 95% of potential institutional inflows into Bitcoin are still ahead. He named investment trusts, asset managers and corporate treasuries among the buyers, and he said that their buying was just beginning.

Back also pointed to the Bitcoin halving in the spring of 2028. He said that miners would sell fewer coins after it.

He made a similar point at Paris Blockchain Week in April. "When the Bitcoin price is low, the miners have to sell a bigger proportion… and when the price is higher, they don't have to sell as much," Back said at the time.

#5046. Analyst: XRP Below $1 Revives a 70% Bounce Bet

Home » Trendsetter News » Analyst: XRP Below $1 Revives a 70% Bounce Bet

XRP’s dip below $1 has revived hopes of a roughly 70% rebound, with one analyst citing a similar August rally.

XRP could repeat a roughly 70% rebound from below $1, according to a crypto commentator who points to an August rally as the template. In a new YouTube episode, Zach Rector argues that another sharp recovery is possible, though the case rests largely on a historical chart comparison rather than a confirmed catalyst.

The chart discussion puts XRP’s latest low near $0.89. The analyst says an earlier slide to $0.98 in August was followed by a rise toward $1.70 within four or five days.

“I’m not here to guarantee that that’s coming again,” Zach Rector says, before overlaying the earlier price pattern onto the latest decline. The analyst also suggests XRP could revisit $2 quickly, without committing to a date.

The explanation centers on leverage: the host argues that downside liquidations have largely cleared, leaving market makers an incentive to drive prices higher. The transcript provides no liquidation figures, order-book data or positioning metrics to substantiate that view.

That distinction matters. A previous bounce illustrates how sharply XRP can recover, but it does not establish that selling pressure is exhausted or rule out another decline.

The more consequential adoption discussion concerns the analyst’s claim that 1,700 institutions, corporations and enterprises use Ripple solutions. Rector explicitly acknowledges that there is no breakdown showing how many use products involving XRP.

Zach Rector also defends Ripple’s emphasis on its RLUSD stablecoin, arguing that greater stablecoin liquidity could attract activity to the XRP Ledger.

A statement attributed to Old Glory Bank supports offering both XRP and stablecoins, with regulated stablecoins potentially better suited to payments where businesses require certainty of value.

Still, stablecoin issuance on the ledger should not be confused with evidence that every payment requires XRP as an intermediary. The YouTube video does not establish that connection, despite the host’s bullish interpretation.

Zach Rector also identifies a proposed lending protocol as a potential source of collateral demand.

For crypto enthusiasts, these are separate propositions to track: a short-term rebound based on chart resemblance, and a longer-term investment case that depends on measurable network activity, liquidity and actual XRP usage.

#5045. Frontier AI models adopt fake data, exposing a hidden flaw

AI supervisors adopted forged answers more often when weaker models handled evidence, exposing reliability risks in delegated tasks.

A study submitted to arXiv on Nov. 12 found that stronger AI models could repeat forged answers when weaker helpers read the evidence, exposing a reliability risk in systems that split work across models.

In their November paper, researchers Keegan Wang and Anantika Mannby tested agents on fictional records and two-option questions.

They measured harmful revisions: adoption of the attacker's wrong answer against a correct baseline.

In one Anthropic's Claude Code test, an Opus 5 supervisor chose the planted wrong answer in 1 of 180 trials when reading files itself, compared with 75 of 180 when it delegated reading to Haiku 4.5. This stacked-forgery condition combined several forms of false evidence.

A separate OpenAI's Codex test used a forged annotation claiming authority, and GPT-5.6-sol made no harmful revisions in 180 direct-reading trials, versus 44 in 180 when it relied on GPT-5.6-terra. The Claude Code and Codex comparisons used different attacks, limiting comparisons between the products.

Delegated Claude Code trials mechanically limited the supervisor to its Task tool, removing direct file access, while Codex's delegation restriction was instructional and checked per trial.

In a Nov. 12 study using fictional records, a stronger supervisor could repeat a weaker reader's error. Compare the tested configurations.

Opus 5: 1/180 reading directly, 75/180 with Haiku 4.5, and 0/180 with a same-tier helper, under stacked forgeries.

GPT-5.6-sol: 0/180 reading directly, 44/180 with GPT-5.6-terra, and 1/180 with a same-tier helper, under an authority gloss.

The model handling the evidence can determine whether a stronger supervisor repeats a forged answer.

Sandbox tasks with planted forgeries. Claude Code delegation was Task-only; Codex restriction was instructional and checked per trial. The two selected products used different attacks. Rates describe those configurations, not customer prevalence.

Reported harmful-revision counts from Table 1. Each arm uses 180 fictional facts. Bar lengths are count / 180 on a shared scale; zero is an outlined marker. Conditions are separate tests, not a timeline or product ranking.

Sandbox tasks with planted forgeries. Claude Code delegation was Task-only; Codex restriction was instructional and checked per trial. The two selected products used different attacks. Rates describe those configurations, not customer prevalence.

The stacked attack placed five forged documents in a 35-file store, combining correction notices, an authoritative annotation and repeated false accounts. Detectable forgeries carried two clues: they predated the records they purported to correct and lacked genuine accession lines.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Instructions directed at automated readers were resisted wherever the researchers tested them. Some successful attacks instead looked like factual records, allowing a helper's report to carry a false conclusion upward.

Results also varied by attack: a correction-notice condition fooled Codex in 172 of 180 direct-reading trials.

Using same-tier helpers reduced failures in the tested comparisons. Claude Code recorded no harmful revisions in 180 trials per condition under the annotation and stacked attacks, while Codex recorded 1 in 180 under the annotation attack.

Clean-store delegation also produced no harmful revisions in 180 trials for each product.

In one real-harness test with 40 trials per arm, requiring original-record quotations reduced harmful revisions from 15 to 3. A verifier told to be skeptical reduced them to 1.

The authors identify lost provenance and reduced checking as possible contributors. Their evidence about fewer tool turns is correlational, leaving suppressed verification as a hypothesis.

The experiments measure performance on generated fictional tasks in a sandbox. Their rates apply to the tested configurations, with variation across repeated runs, but they offer no estimate of ordinary customer failures.

The practical warning is that evaluating a supervisor alone can miss failures introduced by the model handling its evidence.

#5044. Tom Lee Blames Nvidia and Anthropic IPO for Ethereum Plunge as Bitmine Buys the Dip

Tom Lee Blames Nvidia and Anthropic IPO for Ethereum Plunge as Bitmine Buys the Dip

Bitmine Immersion Technologies kept buying Ethereum through a punishing selloff, adding 7,653 ETH worth roughly $14 million, as chairman Tom Lee argued the token’s steep decline runs counter to improving fundamentals and echoes a dip that preceded a major tech IPO earlier this year.

The NYSE-listed company said Monday its holdings reached 6,077,745 ETH, worth about $11.1 billion at a reference price of $1,823 per coin, far below where the firm’s stack was valued just weeks ago.

That marks a stark drop in paper value: Bitmine’s total crypto, cash and “moonshot” holdings now stand at $11.8 billion, down from $14.3 billion a week earlier, almost entirely because of ETH’s price slide rather than any selling.

The position still represents 4.97% of Ethereum’s 122.2 million token supply, leaving Bitmine 99% of the way to its goal of owning 5% of the network, the target it calls the “Alchemy of 5%.”

Lee did not sugarcoat the move. “ETH tumbled 28% in the past two weeks,” he said, calling the “waterfall decline” inconsistent with what he views as positive fundamentals, including banks and financial institutions announcing tokenization projects built on Ethereum. (Disclosure: Tom Lee is an investor in Dastan, Decrypt’s parent company.)

He pointed to external pressures weighing on the price, including the fallout from Nvidia’s turmoil triggering margin selling in Asia and investors raising cash ahead of Anthropic’s initial public offering, whose pricing is reported for Nov. 18.

Lee also drew a historical parallel, arguing the drop mirrors the sell-off ahead of SpaceX’s $75 billion June IPO, when ETH fell 39% over eight weeks and bottomed three trading days before pricing. Lee suggested the current slump, down a similar amount ahead of the Anthropic listing, could follow the same pattern.

Bitmine remains the world’s largest Ethereum treasury and the second-largest crypto treasury overall, behind Michael Saylor’s Strategy, which now holds 849,327 BTC. Strategy also kept buying through the downturn, with Saylor nudging the market to rally with the quip “this dip could use a little orange.”

Bitmine has 5,227,037 ETH, about 86% of its holdings, staked through its MAVAN platform, with projected annualized revenue of roughly $245 million at current levels.

It also holds 216 Bitcoin, $448 million in cash and marketable securities, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries and a $67 million position in Eightco Holdings.

#5042. Tesla hikes Cybertruck prices up to 10% after announcing it will kill production

Tesla has raised Cybertruck prices in the US by up to 10% and stopped taking orders for the base Dual Motor AWD trim. The cheapest Cybertruck you can actually order now costs $92,490.

The move comes right after Tesla confirmed it will end Cybertruck production at Gigafactory Texas around mid-2027.

Here’s what Tesla’s online configurator shows today, compared to the prices set in August:

The Dual Motor trim still shows a new, higher price on Tesla’s website, but the option is greyed out. So Tesla raised the price of a version it isn’t selling anymore.

This is the second price hike in less than three months. Tesla raised prices by $5,000 on two trims in August, when the Dual Motor went from $69,990 to $74,990 and the Premium AWD from $79,990 to $84,990.

Add it up and the Premium AWD is now $12,500 (15.6%) more expensive than it was in early August. And the entry price for any Cybertruck you can order went from $69,990 to $92,490. That’s a 32% jump in about 10 weeks.

For reference, Tesla unveiled the Cybertruck in 2019 with a promised starting price of $39,900.

The price hikes make little sense from a demand standpoint. Tesla doesn’t break out Cybertruck deliveries, but its “Other Models” category, which also includes the Model S, Model X, and Semi, fell 48% year-over-year to 8,295 units in Q3. We estimate Cybertrucks made up 7,000 to 8,000 of those, about 12% of the line’s 250,000-unit annual capacity.

Tesla also tried the opposite approach earlier this year. It offered a dual-motor Cybertruck at $59,990 in February for “only the next 10 days,” then pulled it. A single-motor RWD version was discontinued after just 173 sales. None of it moved the needle.

The Cybertruck isn’t sold in Europe or most right-hand-drive markets, and Tesla canceled it for Australia and New Zealand last month.

So, Tesla has now discontinued more models than it currently offers. Looks like the great leader isn’t particularly good at writing product specs. And the hype has been over for quite a while anyway. In Europe, owning a Tesla has actually become something of an image liability.

This is what a wind-down looks like. Tesla isn’t trying to sell more Cybertrucks anymore. It’s trying to make as much money as possible on the last ones, with the hope that announcing the end of production will encourage some people to secure their order.

Once you’ve decided to end a program, volume stops mattering. You have a set number of build slots left, a parts supply you want to burn down, and probably a small group of fans who’ll want one before it’s gone. Pausing the cheapest trim and charging $7,500 more for the rest is the obvious play. You push everyone into higher-margin configurations and stop pretending the base truck is a real product.

But it does say a lot about the last two years of Cybertruck pricing. Tesla went from a $59,990 “limited-time” offer in February to a $92,490 entry price in November. That’s a company that never figured out who the truck was for.

If you’re buying an EV, powering it with home solar can cut your fuel costs to nearly zero. With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. And with lease and PPA options, you can do it with zero upfront cost and start saving immediately. If you want to find the best deal, check out EnergySage. #5041. MEXC Pulls The Plug On VISA Card Months After Launch

Customers on MEXC’s global exchange can’t use their platform-issued debit cards following an issuer problem.

The exchange announced that its Global Card service ends today, November 18, 2026, at 15:30 UTC after its third-party card issuer made service adjustments. Existing cards will no longer process transactions and new applications are suspended.

The virtual Visa card launched on August 31 with features that included up to 10% USDT cashback and high spending limits. It lasted under three months.

MEXC Global Card Service Update We regret to announce that, following service adjustments by our third-party card issuer, MEXC Global Card services will be discontinued on Nov 18, 2026, at 15:30 UTC. We sincerely apologize for any inconvenience this may cause. We remain… pic.twitter.com/QXLFMeRvd7

The shutdown follows the earlier discontinuation of MEXC’s APAC Card on November 11 over similar issuer issues, and it comes after a temporary maintenance pause that began on November 14.

Cardholders can still withdraw their remaining balances to their MEXC Fiat account before the cutoff.

Any funds left on the card will be snapshotted on November 21 at 15:30 UTC and credited to Spot accounts within three business days.

Refunds for prior transactions will go to Fiat accounts once received, pending cashback continues on schedule, and users are advised to cancel or switch any recurring payments tied to the card.

MEXC has not identified the card issuer or detailed the exact nature of the adjustments. Funds are not frozen; they are being returned to users’ exchange accounts.

#5039. Bitcoin power-law models predict a rebound without real proof

Bitcoin's power-law model projects a $383,000 median price by 2030 using a recovery assumption that historical tests have yet to prove.

The power-law model links Bitcoin's price to its age, but the recovery forecasts in a Nov. 10 preprint also assume that departures from the long-run curve reverse, a tendency its historical analysis leaves unresolved.

Authors Carlos Baquero and Raquel Menezes argued that Bitcoin's long-run growth rate has slowed under the conditions their test allows.

The study's price record ends on Sept. 19, so its results do not explain November's selling.

Model advocate Stephen Perrenod made the attractor argument explicit in a June 5 analysis, arguing that tests of price deviations supported an attractor around which bubbles and bear markets occur.

He also cautioned that those tests did not predict next month's price or prove the relationship must continue indefinitely.

Reports on power-law likewise described an attractor framework while leaving the force sustaining it unsettled.

Baquero and Menezes used 820 weekly observations from January 2011 through September 2026. Within a declared family of possible price deviations, their test found evidence of a slowing long-run growth rate.

It rejected constant drift in expected log price at a nominal 5% significance level, compatible with a power-law trend, but does not identify it uniquely.

Their main family allowed shocks to persist as much as a random walk, where a shock need never fade. When a prespecified sensitivity check allowed even more persistent changes, the rejection disappeared, showing how the conclusion depended on the persistence assumption.

The paper also found that a Gompertz curve, which approaches a ceiling, described the historical record almost as well as the power law.

The authors said the fitted models for price deviations revert toward their trends, so most forecasts anticipate a rise from the approximately $81,000 September endpoint.

The restoring tendency entered through the chosen models even though the historical analysis cannot establish it within the evaluated approach.

For Dec. 28, 2030, the power-law model produced a $383,000 median and a 90% interval of $150,000 to $1,015,000. Across all five trend models, the medians ranged from $85,000 to $1,011,000.

The authors warned that their predictive intervals are probably too narrow and are not calibrated. Each forecast combines the fitted distribution with simulated and refitted alternatives, but the refits explore only neighboring choices for how deviations behave.

That leaves broader uncertainty about persistence only partly represented.

The result is a model that assumes a pull toward trend and cannot use its projected rebound as evidence that the pull exists.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The study separates the historical trend question from the reversion its forecasts assume. Compare the two parts of the reasoning.

The slowdown finding holds within the declared shock family; allowing more persistent changes removes it. Reversion remains unresolved.

The fitted forecast models supply reversion, so most project a rise from the September endpoint. This assumption is not established by the historical analysis.

A forecast that assumes a pull toward trend cannot establish that pull through its projected rebound.

The paper's historical conclusion depends on its persistence family. Reversion remains unresolved within the evaluated models and six-feature method. Diagrams show reasoning, not measured causal forces or guaranteed price paths.

Conceptual dependency diagrams, not price charts. The historical view separates the conditional slowdown result from unresolved reversion; the forecast view shows the fitted trend and assumed reversion feeding a projected rise. No numerical extrapolation or arithmetic.

The paper's historical conclusion depends on its persistence family. Reversion remains unresolved within the evaluated models and six-feature method. Diagrams show reasoning, not measured causal forces or guaranteed price paths.

The paper describes itself as a preregistered reanalysis of a record the authors had already studied. They reported freezing the protocol and posting its hash on Oct. 6, applying the historical test once on Oct. 7, then registering the results and forecasts later that morning.

Those forecasts target the last Saturday of each year from 2027 through 2030 and will be assessed jointly using a log-density score, which measures how well the predicted distributions match the eventual prices.

Better performance would demonstrate better prediction of those four observations. The distinction remains consequential for a Bitcoin recovery case: a historical growth curve can frame a forecast, but an assumed return toward it is still a proposition for future prices to test.

#5038. Hackers Redirected Bitcoin Miner Rewards via AI Attack, Luxor CEO Says

Hackers Redirected Bitcoin Miner Rewards via AI Attack, Luxor CEO Says

Hackers briefly hijacked computing power from Bitcoin miners using Luxor’s fleet management software, in what the company’s CEO called an AI-enabled attack.

Luxor Technology said Sunday that its internal monitoring flagged an unauthorized redirect of hashrate through Commander, its tool for managing mining machines.

Hashrate is the computing power miners devote to securing Bitcoin and earning rewards. Just over a dozen client accounts were affected, with their machines pointed to a different mining pool for a limited window, effectively sending the value of their work elsewhere.

Luxor said it patched Commander within two hours and began restoring affected machines to their original settings. No data was leaked or taken from its systems, and the issue was isolated to Commander, according to the company.

Luxor Pool and its LuxOS software weren’t affected, the company said, adding that every affected client will be paid in full for the redirected hashrate, as if their machines had mined normally.

Luxor CEO Nick Hansen said the compromise began around 6 p.m. Pacific time Saturday through “a sophisticated AI-enabled vector.” He didn’t detail how the attack worked. “You trust us with your fleet, and we need to be better,” he wrote on X.

Around 6:00p PDT yesterday, a subset of Commander customers were compromised, through a sophisticated AI-enabled vector. We were able to identify and patch quickly so that it didn't continue, then the hard work of reverting started.…

In a follow-up post, Hansen said crypto businesses are now “inundated with AI-enabled attacks,” with abliterated models, or AI models stripped of their safety guardrails, probing systems around the clock for weaknesses.

Luxor has responded by buying its own bare-metal GPUs to run such models defensively. It has also put most new feature work on hold to harden its infrastructure by rotating keys, updating kernels and pinning dependencies. Hansen urged other companies running similar services to do the same.

Bitcoin developers are grappling with the same threat. Bitcoin Core updated its security policy to address AI-driven threats, and an AI “red team” effort has turned up critical vulnerabilities throughout the year.

The incident also follows other attacks on Bitcoin infrastructure. Blockstream’s Liquid Network restored full Bitcoin backing earlier this month, two months after a $320 million exploit.

Luxor said it would soon publish a full post-mortem of the hack.

#5037. Kia’s PV7 electric van stretches over 17 feet and seats 9

Kia is expanding its electric van lineup with the PV7, a larger PV5 sibling that can carry nine people or a whole lot of cargo.

The PV5, Kia’s first fully electric van from its Platform Beyond Vehicle (PBV) business, is proving to be a bigger hit than expected.

A year after it went on sale, the PV5 quickly became Europe’s most popular small electric van. In the first half of 2026, it was the top seller in its segment, accounting for 37% of electric C-segment van sales across Europe.

Next year, Kia will launch the PV7, the second electric van from its PBV business. Kia revealed the PV7 at IAA Transportation in Hanover, Germany, in September.

It’s based on the same E-GMP.S platform as the PV5, but the PV7 features a considerably longer wheelbase and stretched body, opening up more space inside.

The PV7 is over 17.5 ft long, measuring 5,350 mm long, 1,995 mm wide, and 1,990 mm high, with a 3,500 mm wheelbase. In comparison, the PV5 is just 4,695 mm long, 1,895 mm wide, and 1,915 mm tall, with a 2,995 mm wheelbase.

That’s even bigger than the Volkswagen ID. Buzz Long-Wheelbase (LWB), which measures 4,962 mm in length with a 3,239 mm wheelbase.

Like the PV5, the PV7 will initially be available in Passenger and Cargo body styles, but Kia plans to launch 23 different variants, or “top hats,” over the next few years.

It will be offered with two nickel-cobalt-manganese (NCM) battery packs: 71.5 kWh and 96.2 kWh. The Long Range Cargo model offers an all-electric WLTP range of over 460 km (285 miles). Even at full capacity, Kia said the new PV7 achieves nearly 400 km (248 miles) of range and still has 350 liters of trunk space.

A plena capacidad la nueva #PV7 consigue casi 400 km de autonomía y aún reserva 350 litros de maletero #Kia #KiaPV7 #MovementThatInspires pic.twitter.com/fzIvviYZuE

Front-wheel drive (FWD) will be standard at launch, but Kia plans to add an all-wheel drive (AWD) option later.

Depending on the configuration, the FWD electric powertrain delivers up to 268 horsepower (200 kW) and 309 lb-ft (420 Nm) of torque.

Thanks to its 800V architecture, the Kia PV7’s battery can charge from 10% to 80% in about 25 minutes with a 350 kW DC fast charger. It will be Kia’s first vehicle with two charging ports, including a front AC/DC port and an optional rear side AC port.

With vehicle-to-load (V2L) capabilities, it can serve as a mobile power source for tools, equipment, a campsite, or other external electronic devices.

The PV7 Cargo, designed for businesses, fleets, and logistics, is available in 2- and 3-seat configurations and offers up to 6.1 cubic meters of cargo space. The High Roof variant offers even more, with up to 8 cubic meters.

Maximum payload is up to 1,165 kg (2,568 lbs) for the Cargo Long and up to 1,200 kg (2,645 lbs) for the compact model.

The PV7 Cargo Long features a 550 mm loading height and, according to Kia, enough capacity for up to three Euro pallets (800 x 1,200 mm). An added heavy-duty mode adjusts the vehicle’s response to varying payloads to help maintain stability and control while driving.

Depending on the use, buyers can choose between full-open twin-swing tailgate doors or a lift-up tailgate.

The PV7 Passenger, on the other hand, is designed for personal use and families, or can also be used as a shuttle with room for up to nine passengers in the global version. In Korea, it will seat up to 11 passengers.

The 7-seater features Long-Rail Easy Remove Seats, enabling various interior seating configurations. The 9-seater variant, on the other hand, is available with or without a fourth row.

Inside, both versions feature Hyundai’s new Pleos Connect infotainment system, with 12.9″ and 14.6″ display screens and physical controls for commonly used functions.

The system runs Android Automotive OS, making it more like a smartphone with apps and customizable screens. It also includes Hyundai’s Gleo AI, an AI-powered in-car companion that can answer questions, control navigation or vehicle functions, and more.

Nice vehicle but once again Kia/Hyundai short changes on battery capacity. A 138" (3500 mm) wheelbase means plenty of room for a 140-150kwh battery (BMW and Rivian do that with vastly less space). That would give it a proper EPA range of over 300 miles with passengers or cargo. Instead this will be in the low 200s unloaded and 170-190 IRL. Offer it as a $7k upgrade. smh

Kia said the PV7 is based on a “Mobile Workspace” concept, with a layered dashboard and center console to optimize space.

The Kia PV7 Passenger and Cargo will go on sale in Korea and Europe in the second half of 2027. It will then roll out to new markets with new derivatives and conversions.

Kia will announce PV7 prices closer to its launch. In Korea, the PV5 Passenger and Cargo Compact starts at 45.9 million won ($34,000) and 41 million won ($30,000), respectively. In Germany, the PV5 Passenger 5-seater starts at €38,690 ($43,000).

#5035. Countdown to Upgrade: Only 4 Votes Needed for XRP Ledger Vault to Go Live in 2026

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The Single Asset Vault amendment on the XRP Ledger is getting closer to activation on the mainnet as validator support builds for the network’s preparation to enable better DeFi functionality.

According to data from XRPScan, the SingleAssetVault amendment, or XLS-65, has received yes votes from 25 of 35 trusted validators, currently standing at 71.43% consensus as of November 18, 2026. It needs more than 80% of 35 votes (29) to start its 14-day countdown to activation. That means it needs four more votes to get there and could reach the magic number before the end of 2026.

The XRP Ledger’s amendment process requires a proposed amendment to be approved by more than 80% of trusted validators for 14 days before it is implemented. Validator support may shift during this time, and if the required 80% threshold is lost, activation may be delayed.

The Single Asset Vault amendment creates a structure for aggregating assets from multiple depositors and is intended to be used with the on-chain Lending Protocol. The amendment introduces a Vault object that defines the state of a tokenized vault, and the Vault entry is one of the ledger entry types it provides.

The Vault object holds important information such as available assets, shares, total value, and other relevant information. Users can create a Vault object with the VaultCreate transaction; the Vault object is tracked in an Owner Directory owned by the Vault Owner account.

The XRP Ledger lending amendment has gained additional yes votes. According to XRPScan, the LendingProtocolV1_1 amendment, also known as XLS-66, has received 24 yes votes out of 35 trusted validators, meaning it has 68.57% consensus as of November 18, 2026. It needs more than 80% of the 35 votes (29) to trigger a 14-day activation countdown.

This implies that there are five votes left for it to reach this threshold, with the possibility of it securing the required number of votes before the end of 2026 as well.

According to XRP enthusiasts, LendingProtocolV1_1 represents the biggest amendment for unlocking capital and liquidity on the XRP Ledger and is anticipated by institutional and retail users.

#5034. Monument Bank puts £250M on Midnight, but hides strict bank rules

Monument Bank's £250 million tokenized deposit plan on Midnight leaves transfer, withdrawal and collateral rights under bank control.

Monument Bank’s Tokenised Saver records customers’ deposits on the Midnight blockchain, but savers cannot transfer the token, sell it to another customer, or pledge it to an outside lender.

In a Nov. 17 post, Charles Hoskinson celebrated the bank’s launch, quoting Midnight's congratulations to Monument and its partners:

“250 million pounds on-chain! That's some serious TVL.”

Monument’s March 27 announcement described a target of up to £250 million in client deposits, so the immediate test is what a customer can do with the record today. Monument’s blockchain record leaves withdrawal and collateral rights governed by the bank’s account terms.

The Tokenised Saver summary says the token mirrors the deposit, while Monument’s records remain the official account of the savings. If the two differ, the bank’s records apply, subject to correcting errors. Customers retain the right to query or challenge an error.

Neither the account nor its token record may be transferred, sold, or assigned, including to another Monument customer. Monument’s FAQ also says the bank owns and controls the wallet, so customers do not manage a private key or seed phrase.

The token record cannot be used as security, while the savings account can serve as security only in Monument’s favor, with the bank's written agreement. That leaves room for an agreed bank arrangement, but supplies no right to take the token to another lender.
