Monument’s savings contract supplies the rights. Its Midnight token record mirrors the deposit without adding transfer or collateral rights.

The bank controls the token record. The underlying deposit and its agreed terms remain authoritative.

Monument may pause or stop tokenization. The deposit, interest, maturity and FSCS treatment remain unchanged.

The token feature can stop while the bank deposit continues on its agreed terms.

Current product summary and FAQ as read November 17, 2026. A paused feature is a possible contractual state, not a reported outage. FSCS cover applies to eligible deposits under aggregate limits. No issued balance or flow size is depicted.

Schematic of the contractual relationship between a deposit and its mirrored record, not a transaction sequence. The selectable pause/stop state illustrates a power described in current terms.

Current product summary and FAQ as read November 17, 2026. A paused feature is a possible contractual state, not a reported outage. FSCS cover applies to eligible deposits under aggregate limits. No issued balance or flow size is depicted.

Monument can also change, pause or stop the tokenization feature. Its summary warns that the record can be delayed, unavailable, or wrong, while saying the deposit, interest rate, interest, maturity date, and FSCS protection remain the same.

The bank deposit continues independently of whether its digital copy is functioning.

The product currently advertises a 12-month term at 5.2% annual equivalent rate, or 5.08% gross, with interest calculated daily and paid monthly. Interest can go to the customer’s linked account or back into the saver.

Eligibility is limited to existing Monument clients who are UK residents aged 18 or older. Accounts are held in one person’s name and require a personal UK bank account linked to the account.

Customers must hold at least £25,000 across Monument savings accounts, while the Tokenised Saver maximum is £100,000, excluding interest.

Customers have 14 days from account opening to fund the account and can cancel without penalty during that window. Otherwise, withdrawals and early closures are prohibited during the term, which begins with the first deposit.

Monument says eligible deposits are protected by the Financial Services Compensation Scheme up to £120,000 per person, aggregated across eligible deposits with the bank.

In March, Monument said deposits were the first phase and that it would explore tokenized investments and new lending options within its app afterward.

The current saver’s published terms do not provide an investment-backed lending facility. For investment-backed lending, the relevant terms would need to identify the collateral, establish the lender’s rights over it and explain how customers obtain spendable funds.

Customer-controlled transfers or external token collateral would also require rights and arrangements beyond this saver’s present restrictions.

For savers today, the concrete offer is fixed interest and an additional bank-controlled record, while future customer liquidity depends on new product rights and operating arrangements. Disclosure of the funded and tokenized balance would answer the separate question of the program’s scale.

#5032. Bitcoin's $2 Billion Liquidation Weekend Leaves Traders Watching $60,000

Bitcoin's $2 Billion Liquidation Weekend Leaves Traders Watching $60,000

Bitcoin fell to around $62,100 on Sunday as roughly $2 billion in crypto derivatives positions were forcibly closed, mostly bets that prices would rise. The weekend slide sent the cryptocurrency to its lowest level since July 14, adding another ugly chapter to six consecutive weeks of losses.

Bitcoin had recovered to around $64,300 early Monday. But the options market is showing why traders aren't necessarily treating that bounce as an all-clear.

Liquidations happen when an exchange closes a trader's position because the money posted to support a borrowed bet is no longer enough to cover its losses. CoinGlass figures from Saturday showed $1.9 billion liquidated in one 24-hour period, about $1.8 billion of it bullish positions across cryptocurrencies, not just Bitcoin.

U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs had already been losing investors before the weekend. Data from Decrypt’s ETF tracker show $185.4 million in net outflows on Friday, extending their losing streak to seven trading sessions and bringing withdrawals since November 5 to approximately $1.91 billion.

BlackRock's IBIT accounted for $143.5 million of Friday's withdrawals, while Fidelity's FBTC lost $41.9 million. The funds' earlier weakness had helped explain the broader exodus, although ETF flow totals measure fund creations and redemptions rather than every trade between investors.

Those numbers also come with a timing distinction: U.S. ETFs do not trade over the weekend, while Bitcoin trades around the clock. Friday's outflows show weakened demand before the crash, not that ETF shareholders were redeeming shares during Sunday's plunge.

Bitcoin's technical indicators suggest the selling may be overstretched, but that doesn't necessarily mean the worst is over.

The daily Relative Strength Index sat near 24 in oversold territory on the daily charts. Meanwhile, the Average Directional Index stood above 33, indicating a strong prevailing trend, with bearish directional pressure still dominating. The weekly RSI, near 38, pointed to a weak longer-term picture, although it had not reached oversold territory.

The cryptocurrency bounced after approaching support around $62,000. A recovery would face potential resistance near $66,500 to $67,200, followed by another obstacle around $69,300, based on the chart's Fibonacci levels that measure support and resistance marks.

A decisive break below $62,000, meanwhile, would put the psychologically important $60,000 level back in focus—precisely where options traders have accumulated significant downside exposure.

Bitcoin's next test will be whether fresh U.S. ETF flow data show more withdrawals for Monday and whether traders keep paying for downside protection. The heavily watched November 27 options expiration is still ahead, with $50,000 puts among the contracts drawing the most recent trading activity.

#5031. Cardano faces $5 million adoption test after Hoskinson declares original roadmap complete

The Foundation is seeking fresh treasury funding as its CEO acknowledges that years of technological progress must translate into sustainable economic activity.

Cardano Foundation CEO Frederik Gregaard defended the blockchain's future as his organization sought nearly 25 million ADA to promote adoption.

In a Nov. 15 statement, Gregaard argued that Cardano had become technologically stronger and more decentralized, pushing back against growing concerns about the network's future following controversial remarks by founder Charles Hoskinson.

The comments followed a backlash against Hoskinson's declaration that Cardano's original development roadmap was complete, with some investors questioning whether the network had delivered on its broader ambitions.

Hoskinson later clarified that he meant the completion of Cardano's original engineering roadmap, not that development or his involvement with the ecosystem had ended.

However, Gregaard argued that Cardano's future should not depend on any single individual or organization. He said:

“Decentralization means that no single individual gets to decide when Cardano is finished.”

Gregaard pointed to several developments he believes have strengthened Cardano's competitive position, including decentralized governance, alternative node implementations and new infrastructure for financial institutions.

He highlighted Amaru and Dingo, alternative implementations of Cardano's blockchain software written in Rust and Go, respectively, as advances that could reduce reliance on a single technology stack.

The executive also cited the introduction of ADA futures on CME Group and the launch of CIP-0113, a programmable token standard designed to support regulated financial assets.

According to Gregaard, the new standard has already enabled the tokenization of 850,000 shares in Veridian, a Swiss company spun out of the Cardano Foundation, structured as legally recognized securities under Swiss law.

The development lets companies embed compliance requirements, investor eligibility, and transfer restrictions directly into blockchain-based assets.

Gregaard believes these capabilities could position Cardano to compete in the growing market for tokenized stocks, bonds and other traditional financial instruments. He said:

“The Cardano I see today is fundamentally stronger, more decentralized, and more capable than the Cardano I came into over five years ago.”

However, the executive also acknowledged that technological progress alone would not determine Cardano's long-term success. Gregaard said:

“Of course, we need to translate more of these initiatives into production deployments, recurring transactions and sustainable economic activity.”

He added that Cardano needs to become more competitive, even as he defended the progress made across its ecosystem.

That challenge has become particularly relevant as the Foundation seeks additional community funding to expand Cardano's presence at major industry events.

On Nov. 16, the Cardano Foundation submitted a governance proposal requesting 24,995,775 ADA from the network's community treasury.

The request carries an estimated value of nearly $5 million based on an assumed ADA price of $0.20 and would finance Cardano's participation in 12 international conferences throughout 2027.

The proposed spending includes approximately $2 million for sponsorships, $1 million for exhibition infrastructure and $702,000 for travel support. Another $695,000 would finance accompanying events, including four hackathons.

The Foundation argued that coordinated participation in industry conferences gives Cardano developers access to businesses, investors and regulators they might otherwise struggle to reach.

It cited results from its previous events program, which generated 8,191 booth contacts, engaged 1,169 developers and initiated discussions around 219 potential collaborations between August 2025 and July 2026.

The program also supported 79 ecosystem projects and generated 1,045 Cardano wallet registrations, according to the Foundation.

However, the latest proposal highlights the distinction between attracting industry interest and establishing sustainable blockchain activity.

Its 2027 targets include 9,520 booth contacts, 955 developers, and 184 potential collaborations. While the Foundation also promises to track on-chain transactions generated through event contacts, the proposal sets no numerical target for that activity.

The proposal also acknowledges financial risks associated with ADA's price. Should the token fall below the assumed $0.20 valuation, the Foundation says it would reduce or cancel planned activities rather than exceed its funding allocation.

The funding request is now before Cardano's delegated representatives, who have until Dec. 20 to vote on whether to approve the withdrawal.

Their decision will determine whether the Foundation secures another year of treasury-backed international outreach as it attempts to translate Cardano's technological progress into more measurable economic adoption.

#5030. $20 XRP Demand Puts Exposes Ripple’s Legal Boundaries

Home » Trendsetter News » $20 XRP Demand Puts Exposes Ripple’s Legal Boundaries

An open letter urging Ripple to push XRP to $15–$20 has raised concerns about the legal implications of explicit price support.

A demand that Ripple should drive XRP price to $15 – $20 could undermine the legal argument holders spent years defending, wealth focused commentator Kamilah Stevenson warns in her video.

Her concern is that an explicit price-support pledge could strengthen claims that buyers expect profits from Ripple’s efforts.

The open letter, posted anonymously on X on November 1, addresses Ripple executives Brad Garlinghouse and David Schwartz. It accuses the company of prioritizing RLUSD and other businesses over XRP and calls for market action to lift the token’s price.

Dr. Stevenson cites attorney Bill Morgan’s objection: the letter asks Ripple to provide the very support that holders argued they had not relied on when buying XRP. “XRP holders bought a digital asset, not shares of Ripple,” she says, describing pushback against the letter.

That distinction matters under the Howey test, which examines whether an arrangement involves an investment of money in a common enterprise with an expectation of profits derived from others’ efforts.

In July 2023, Judge Analisa Torres distinguished between Ripple’s institutional sales and its programmatic exchange sales.

About $728 million in institutional sales constituted investment contracts; the programmatic sales at issue did not, with the court emphasizing the circumstances of those transactions, including buyers’ lack of knowledge about whom they were paying.

The ruling did not give every future XRP transaction blanket protection. Equally, one holder’s letter does not change XRP’s legal treatment: Dr. Stevenson’s warning concerns what Ripple might promise or do in response.

The YouTube video recalls the trading restrictions that followed the SEC’s December 2020 lawsuit. Coinbase suspended XRP trading in January 2021 and restored it after the July 2023 ruling, leaving U.S. customers without that trading venue for roughly two and a half years.

Dr. Kamilah Stevenson argues that Ripple should instead build reasons to use XRP, pointing to payments and its acceptance as collateral at Ripple Prime. Those activities are different from promising holders a particular XRP price.

#5029. Bitcoin’s failed Nvidia earnings rally puts $62,000 support back in the spotlight

Bitcoin's post-Nvidia earnings rally faded within 35 minutes as ETF outflows and weak demand threaten a sustained recovery.

Bitcoin climbed to about $64,600 after Nvidia beat Wall Street’s revenue expectations Tuesday, then gave back most of the gain within 35 minutes.

In its first earnings report since Jensen Huang’s departure, Nvidia reported $115.8 billion in fiscal third-quarter revenue, up 103% from a year earlier, and topping the $109.1 billion Bloomberg analyst consensus.

Nvidia projected $130 billion in fourth-quarter revenue, plus or minus 3%, above the $125.2 billion analyst estimate Yahoo Finance reported. The forecast covers a quarter with an extra week and assumes zero data center compute revenue from China.

Nvidia’s board accepted Huang’s resignation effective Nov. 11 and disclosed it Nov. 12, according to the company’s SEC filing. Colette Kress took over as interim CEO while retaining her job as chief financial officer.

Nvidia released the figures at 4:20 p.m. ET, and Bitcoin traded close to $64,000 at the end of the one-minute interval before the report. It touched $64,602.98 during the 4:33 p.m. minute and was back to $64,246.47 by 4:55 p.m., still about $244 above its pre-release level.

Separately, Nvidia traded at $157.83 at 4:56:47 p.m. ET, up 1.52% from its $155.46 regular-session close.

Coinbase BTC/USD one-minute closing trades on Nov. 17, 2026. The first point is the trade immediately before Nvidia’s 4:20 p.m. ET release. Intraminute highs are outside this closing-price series.

Source: Coinbase Exchange BTC/USD candles · Coinbase Exchange candle definitions · Nvidia release timestamp

In research published before Nvidia’s earnings, Glassnode found that losses from coins sold below their purchase price had reached levels seen around previous market lows, relative to profits over the preceding year.

Its model estimated that the Nov. 13-14 liquidations had cleared most leveraged long positions stacked above $62,000, leaving fewer of those previously mapped positions vulnerable to forced closure.

Glassnode estimated that about 20% of short-term holders’ coins had been acquired between $62,000 and $66,000. Within 5% of Bitcoin’s market price, the Binance and Coinbase order books showed about 2.3 times as much buy-side as sell-side liquidity.

Traders can withdraw those orders, so order book size only indicates how much buying might materialize. If the lower edge of the range gives way, Glassnode pointed to the June low near $58,500 and the aggregate realized price near $53,700, based on the prices at which coins last moved.

#5028. Bitcoin Wallet Maker Trezor Says Customer Data Was Exposed Again

Bitcoin Wallet Maker Trezor Says Customer Data Was Exposed Again

The “days since last third-party breach” counter at Trezor has reset to zero once again.

This time, hackers accessed shipping partner Skladon's systems and likely exported information tied to approximately 27,000 hardware-wallet orders, according to a company disclosure published Monday.

The intrusion occurred November 7, but Skladon did not notify Trezor until November 15. The exposed records cover orders placed between August 9 and November 7, including customers' full names, phone numbers, delivery and email addresses, plus the products they bought and their prices.

Trezor said its own systems were not compromised, and no wallet backups, PINs or passphrases were involved. The breach does not, by itself, let an attacker move a buyer's coins, but it gives them a potentially dangerous combination: a home address and evidence that someone bought a device designed to store crypto.

The latest incident follows an August shipping partner hack and a September email provider breach, making three separate third-party security incidents in a little over three months.

Trezor said some customers have already reported fraudulent phone calls. Criminals could cite the exact model and price of a wallet to make an unsolicited call, text or letter seem legitimate, or use a home address to identify someone who may hold cryptocurrency.

In August, logistics provider ShipMonk suffered a breach that initially appeared to affect fewer than 14,000 Trezor customers. An updated investigation uncovered records for another 67,000 U.S. buyers from 2019 to 2021 that ShipMonk had retained despite assurances they had been deleted, bringing the total to 80,689 people.

September brought another incident, this time at marketing email provider Brevo. Hackers used Trezor's legitimate email channel to distribute a fake security warning urging recipients to install an app that requested their wallet backups. Trezor later said 347,149 email addresses had been exported.

These weren't Trezor's first brushes with the problem. A Mailchimp insider attack targeted Trezor users in 2022, and a 2024 breach of its external support portal put the contact details of up to 66,000 people at risk.

The fallout can go beyond inbox spam. In February, fake letters were sent to hardware-wallet owners, complete with Trezor branding, holograms and QR codes leading to phishing sites.

Asked why an order from Trezor's official store involved an outside company, Trezor responded that it’s basically a logistics question, not a tech issue. “Companies of our size and global reach can’t run efficiently without third-party service providers,” the company replied to a comment on X.

Hi, companies of our size and global reach can’t run efficiently without third-party service providers. Handling as much as possible internally is the goal, but doing every part of the business ourselves isn’t realistic. Skladon is one of the partners that delivers Trezor orders,…

It said Skladon held only the information needed to deliver parcels, and handling every stage of global distribution in-house was not realistic.

Trezor says Skladon does not ship to the United States, so American buyers were not affected by this incident. Affected customers have been notified where possible, though some people who ordered between August 9 and 14 may not receive an email because Trezor had already anonymized their records.

The company has introduced anonymous pickup at parcel lockers or collection points in 18 European countries, allowing buyers to avoid supplying a home address. It is also working to shorten retention of order data from 90 to 45 days and says shipping partners will have to follow the same policy.

Trezor advises customers to ignore unexpected calls and messages about their orders, check any concerns through its official support site and never share a wallet backup. It has notified a data protection authority, but has not yet disclosed how attackers got into Skladon's systems.

#5027. Ripple’s 126.8 million XRP valued at $0 in Evernorth filing as $505 million write-down emerges

Ripple's massive XRP contribution carried no accounting value, raising questions about what Evernorth's reported assets actually represent.

Ripple contributed 126.8 million XRP to Evernorth at a recorded value of zero, according to a Nov. 17 SEC filing.

The disclosure adds another wrinkle to the valuation of Evernorth's XRP treasury, which has already suffered roughly $505 million in cumulative write-downs.

The zero-cost lot reflects the basis inherited from Ripple, while the impairment reflects reductions in the carrying amount of Evernorth's historical XRP position. Neither figure establishes what the combined business is worth today.

Evernorth's Sept. 30 financial statements report 346.3 million XRP with an original cost of $846.6 million and a carrying amount of $341.9 million.

Only $6.9 million of impairment was recognized in the third quarter. The nine-month charge was $271 million, while the rest of the cumulative reduction predates 2026.

Under the policy disclosed in the filing, later price recoveries do not reverse the written-down basis, so the gap between book value and market value can persist.

The new amendment clarifies the distinction by adding financial statements for Pathfinder, Evernorth's XRP-holding subsidiary, and updated combined pro forma accounts.

Pathfinder's statements said Ripple contributed 126,791,458 XRP on Oct. 19, 2025. Because the transfer was a transaction between entities under common control, Pathfinder recognized the tokens at Ripple's historical carrying value, which was zero.

The statements also report a merger-related transfer of 346.5 million XRP from Evernorth to Pathfinder. Adding that transfer to Ripple's historical contribution reconstructs approximately 473.3 million XRP from the two disclosed transactions.

The combined pro forma accounts continue historical-cost-less-impairment treatment without a fair-value adjustment. They show $342.2 million of net digital assets, a carrying amount based on that accounting policy.

The 10-Q describes Evernorth's ownership of Pathfinder common units as approximately 71.5%. For the Oct. 9 closing, the pro forma table separately reports 55 million Evernorth shares across both classes and 23 million outside Pathfinder units.

As of Nov. 9, Evernorth reported 58.4 million Class A and Class C shares. Evernorth defines its XRP-per-share KPI as tokens attributable to the public parent divided by the combined number of shares in both classes.

The pro forma balance sheet reports $76 million in cash and $46.3 million in total liabilities, including a $30 million convertible note. It also presents $229.5 million of redeemable noncontrolling interest separately.

The balance sheet presents that outside interest separately from debt, and the note is already included in total liabilities.

The 10-Q subsequently reports $35.3 million in gross warrant proceeds through Nov. 9, plus 8.7 million warrants still outstanding at an $11.50 exercise price. The convertible note can also accrue 4% payment-in-kind interest and has conversion provisions.

The next useful valuation comparison needs a common date for XRP inventory, Evernorth's economic ownership, cash, liabilities, and the share count, with potential dilution shown separately.

The amendment explains why a large write-down and a zero-book-value XRP lot can coexist, while a current market valuation still requires the dated financial bridge.

#5025. The BMW iX3 smashed its EPA range rating, driving well over 400 miles in a real-world test

BMW’s new iX3 outperformed the Volvo EX60 and Mercedes-Benz GLC EV, driving well over 400 miles during a real-world range test.

In a recent nationally representative survey, BMW found that Americans want more EV range than they actually need.

The study found that 73% consider 75 miles of range, or less, enough to cover their daily driving. Meanwhile, 42% said they would require at least 300 miles of range to consider an EV.

Another 53% said they would be more likely to consider an EV if it could add about 150 miles of range in 10 minutes.

BMW’s new iX3 checks all the boxes. Based on BMW’s sixth-generation (Gen6) eDrive 800V system, the 2027 iX3 50 xDrive delivers an EPA-estimated range of up to 434 miles (with 20″ summer tires).

With the 22″ summer tires, the iX3 50 xDrive has an EPA-estimated range of up to 392 miles. Still, plenty for at least a week of commuting.

It can also add 170-185 miles of range in 10 minutes with up to 400 kW DC fast charging. In everyday, real-world driving, it may drive even further than its official EPA numbers suggest.

During Edmunds‘ EV Range test, the 2027 BMW iX3 50 xDrive (with 22″ summer tires) drove 436 miles on a single charge, topping its EPA estimate of 392 miles by 44 miles.

The electric SUV was also more efficient than advertised. The iX3 achieved an energy consumption of 28.3 kWh per 100 miles, beating its EPA estimate of 32 kWh per 100 miles by roughly 11.6%.

BMW’s new iX3 is now the longest-range luxury electric SUV that Edmunds has tested, outperforming rivals including the 2027 Volvo EX60 (352 miles) and Mercedes-Benz GLC Electric (392 miles).

The Edmunds real-world range test is designed to provide a more accurate estimate of an EV’s range in everyday driving conditions.

It consists of 60% city and 40% highway driving with an average speed of 40 mph. The vehicle stays within 5 mph of the posted speed limit, and the climate control is set to auto at 72 degrees.

The 2027 BMW iX3 50 xDrive is available to order in Sport, M Sport, and M Sport Professional trims, priced from $61,500, $64,000, and $65,500, respectively.

Average speed of 40 mph in perfect weather is an absolute best case scenario for EVs. I think cold weather 70 mph would be the worst case and it should be tested.

That said, I think BMW and Mercedes tend to underrate their cars published range (I think they publish worst case condition range). BMW iX and Mercedes have historically outperformed EPA ratings according to various articles and reddits posts.

But I think regardless of what we think of Edmunds tests, these are some crazy range figures and with roughly 100kwh usable battery capacity, you are still looking at close to 4 miles per kwh. As an example across the Ultium platform anything from Chevy or Cadillac barely breaches 300 miles. The big Escalade and pickup trucks have 225+ kwh battery so I am not counting them.

If the depreciation rate for luxury EVs holds up, in 2-3 years, they will be awesome value.

Powered by a 108.7 kWh battery, the new iX3 with 20″ Summer Tires delivers up to 434 miles of range on a single charge.

With a charging rate of up to 400 kW, the new BMW iX3 can recharge from 10% to 80% in about 21 minutes, or add about 185 miles in 10 minutes.

Inside, the electric SUV features the Neue Klasse interior design. The setup includes BMW’s new Panoramic Vision, a full-length windshield display, and a 17.9″ central infotainment screen running the latest Operating System X. Buyers can also upgrade to the optional BMW 3D Head-Up Display.

#5024. Alibaba's Qwen 4 Max Leads China's AI Agent Race—But You Can't Download It

Alibaba's Qwen 4 Max Leads China's AI Agent Race—But You Can't Download It

Alibaba released Qwen 4 Max on Monday, its most powerful AI model yet and the first flagship built on its new Qwen 4 architecture.

It is also the highest-ranked Chinese model on Arena's agent leaderboard, putting the company ahead of Tencent, Moonshot AI and DeepSeek in that particular test.

The new model has 2.4 trillion parameters, can process text, images and video, and accepts up to one million tokens of context. Parameters are all the different dials a model can handle and tweak when processing information. Tokens are basically the smallest unit of information a model can handle. Its current configuration is enough room for lengthy codebases or stacks of documents.

Alibaba uses a mixture-of-experts design, which keeps most of those parameters idle for any given word it processes. Qwen 4 Max activates 71 billion at a time, compared with 95 billion for Qwen 3.8 Max, allowing a very large model to use less computing power per token.

On Agent Arena, Qwen 4 Max placed 14th among individual models and made Alibaba the fifth-ranked laboratory overall.

The leaderboard evaluates real agent sessions using task completion, user satisfaction and how well models respond to corrections, then scores each model by its net percentage improvement over an average competitor. Qwen 4 Max posted a preliminary 7.46% score across 14,727 sessions, the highest among Chinese entries.

Its visual work may be the more interesting part for regular users. The Code Arena web-development ranking compares websites built by AI models using head-to-head user votes and a points-based rating system. Qwen 4 Max debuted sixth globally at 1,750 points, up 74 from its predecessor, and placed eighth in categories for branding and reference-based design.

That puts it in the same neighborhood as the best American models at turning a request, a screenshot or a rough idea into a working interface that actually looks good.

Alibaba's demos go further, showing the model building game environments and translating factory layouts into 3D models, although those are company-selected examples rather than independent tests.

The visual capability is for understanding images and video and producing code or other text-based outputs. It is not the same as releasing a new image generator.

Qwen 4 Max costs $1.50 per million input tokens and $4.50 per million generated tokens through QwenCloud, a 25% reduction from the previous flagship's $2 and $6 rates. Reused input served from an automatic cache costs $0.075 per million tokens, which can make repetitive workflows substantially cheaper.

Alibaba says its internal testing across hundreds of professional specialties found that the model used 35% fewer tokens and reduced average task costs by roughly 60% compared with Qwen 3.8 Max. In other words, it should be cheaper to execute tasks on this model even against competitors with a similar price..

The bigger limitation for the community is that Qwen 4 Max is closed-weight. Developers can use it through an API or Alibaba's apps, but they cannot download its internal parameters, modify it or run it privately on their own hardware.

Qwen has become a foundation for the open-model ecosystem. An August report from Hugging Face counted 151,448 Qwen-based derivative models on its platform, about 4.7 times the number built specifically on Meta's Llama family. Those include community fine-tunes and optimized versions that power local assistants and specialized tools.

Alibaba opened the weights of Qwen 3.8 Max earlier this year, despite having kept some previous Max models proprietary. It also previewed elements of the Qwen 4 architecture with smaller models months before Monday's release.

The launch lands in a crowded Chinese race. Moonshot's Kimi K3 pushed enormous open-weight models toward Western performance, while DeepSeek has been competing hard on API prices.

Qwen 4 Max leads some Chinese rankings, but American labs still hold the top spots in Arena's overall agent and web-development tables. They are also considerably more expensive.

Alibaba says smaller Qwen 4 models will be released with open weights, giving developers a chance to build on this generation rather than simply rent its biggest model. It has not announced their sizes or release dates.

#5022. Bitcoin miners found a way to profit without mining BTC, but the advantage may not last

Electricity credits can reward operators for shutting down, while recovering computing power threatens to reverse Bitcoin's latest difficulty cut.

Surging Texas electricity prices made shutting down more profitable than mining Bitcoin for some operators in early November.

The disruption contributed to Bitcoin's 9.13% mining difficulty reduction on Nov. 14, its largest since June, as expensive electricity forced miners to reconsider whether keeping their machines running was worthwhile.

In a Nov. 17 analysis, mining services firm Luxor found that West Texas electricity prices exceeded the mining revenue of a machine operating at 25 joules per terahash during 45% of the hours between Oct. 29 and Nov. 14. That compares with just 9% during the preceding difficulty period.

The pressure intensified between Nov. 7 and Nov. 11, when average daily electricity prices ranged from $64 to $122 per megawatt-hour. Meanwhile, the modeled mining equipment generated approximately $54 in revenue for every megawatt-hour consumed, before electricity and other operating costs.

Luxor estimated that higher electricity prices alone accounted for roughly four-fifths of the increase in unprofitable mining hours, suggesting energy costs drove the disruption rather than Bitcoin's declining price.

The resulting shutdowns reduced the computing power securing Bitcoin's network, contributing to the difficulty adjustment that lowered the measure to 134.82 trillion from 148.36 trillion.

While miners exposed to wholesale electricity prices could avoid losses by shutting down, companies with favorable power contracts had another opportunity.

Bitcoin mining company Riot Platforms, for example, can temporarily suspend operations and return contracted electricity to the grid in exchange for credits when market prices exceed its fixed electricity rates.

According to its third-quarter SEC filing, Riot earned $24.7 million in power curtailment credits during the three months ended Sept. 30, including $22.9 million from voluntarily reducing electricity consumption.

Those earnings preceded November's electricity surge but demonstrate how the arrangement works.

Consider a miner generating $54 in Bitcoin revenue per megawatt-hour while paying a fixed electricity rate of $35.

Continuing operations would produce $19 after electricity costs. However, if wholesale electricity prices reached $100 and the operator could return its contracted power, it could receive a $65 credit instead.

Under those assumptions, switching off would generate $46 more per megawatt-hour than continuing to mine.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The same power price can make a wholesale miner stop to avoid a loss and give a miner with resale rights a reason to collect a credit.

With a fixed electricity price and resale rights, giving up $19 in mining margin can earn a $65 credit in this example.

Illustrative operating economics before other costs. $100/MWh and $35/MWh are scenario assumptions. Real credits depend on the contract and applicable market price. This does not estimate Riot’s rates or actions in November.

Per MWh, subtract $100 from $54 for wholesale mining, $35 from $54 for fixed-rate mining and $35 from $100 for the return credit. The credit is $46 above the fixed-rate mining margin. All bars share a zero and use the same dollars-per-MWh scale within each view.

Illustrative operating economics before other costs. $100/MWh and $35/MWh are scenario assumptions. Real credits depend on the contract and applicable market price. This does not estimate Riot’s rates or actions in November.

That difference helps explain why electricity markets can influence Bitcoin mining activity independently of cryptocurrency prices.

However, the relief from November's shutdowns may prove short-lived.

Luxor estimated that Bitcoin's computing power recovered to approximately 1,100 exahashes per second as West Texas electricity prices eased between Nov. 13 and Nov. 15, even as Bitcoin's price continued falling.

The recovery accelerated block production after the difficulty reduction, raising the prospect of another substantial adjustment.

Through Nov. 17, Bitcoin blocks had averaged approximately 9.11 minutes since the reset, faster than the network's targeted 10-minute interval.

If that pace persists throughout the current difficulty period, the next adjustment could increase difficulty by approximately 9.85%, reversing nearly all the previous reduction. Even if block production slows to 10 minutes for the remaining period, the early acceleration would still produce an estimated 2.72% increase.

The extent of that rebound will depend partly on whether Texas electricity prices remain low enough to keep miners operating.

#5020. Bitcoin buyers pile 66,000 BTC into critical $62,000 support zone

Glassnode data shows a growing concentration of short-term-holder coins near Bitcoin's November low as exchange buying signals begin to recover.

Bitcoin's plunge toward $62,000 on Nov. 15 coincided with a sharp increase in coins concentrated around the selloff low, potentially strengthening a key support zone.

In its Nov. 17 report, Glassnode estimated that approximately 66,000 BTC entered the $62,000 to $66,000 short-term-holder cost-basis band during the two heaviest days of selling.

The price range, which initially formed around Bitcoin's August low, now accounts for roughly 20% of short-term holders' coins, making it an important level to watch if selling pressure returns.

Exchange trading data suggests buyers have begun absorbing some selling pressure after the crash.

Glassnode reported that sellers dominated trading during the selloff before buyer-initiated volume overtook seller-initiated activity in the 24 hours preceding its Nov. 17 snapshot.

Standing buy orders within 5% of Bitcoin's market price also outweighed sell orders by approximately 2.3 to 1 across Binance and Coinbase. The largest single Binance buy level since August was near $60,000.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Bitcoin fell to about $62,000 on Nov. 15, the lower edge of the cost-basis band. Two days later, Glassnode put Binance’s largest bid level since August near $60,000.

The price band and the open orders measure different things. A retest would show how much selling buyers actually absorb.

The 66,000 BTC figure estimates change in the short-term-holder cost-basis band. The $60,000 marker identifies a Binance order-book level. Orders can be canceled, and the depth ratio measures open orders rather than executed volume.

Glassnode data as of Nov. 17 at 13:00 UTC. The price axis is linear. Bars encode the reported approximate ratio of resting buy to sell size within 5% of spot across Binance and Coinbase, using sell size as one unit.

The 66,000 BTC figure estimates change in the short-term-holder cost-basis band. The $60,000 marker identifies a Binance order-book level. Orders can be canceled, and the depth ratio measures open orders rather than executed volume.

However, the recovery in buying demand has been uneven.

Bitcoin briefly traded at a premium on Coinbase during the Nov. 15 crash, but that advantage disappeared by Nov. 17. A separate Coinbase Premium Index from Axel Adler Jr. showed Bitcoin trading at a 0.110% discount on Coinbase relative to Binance that day.

More recent trading activity nevertheless suggests renewed buying interest.

With Bitcoin trading as high as $65,365 in the last 24 hours, Blockmetric data showed aggressive buying on Binance's spot market exceeded selling by about $5.5 million during the six hours ending at 07:54 UTC.

The platform also recorded $12.7 million in standing Binance buy orders between $62,500 and $62,600, reinforcing the evidence of demand around the lower end of Glassnode's support zone.

The combination of growing holder concentration and stronger exchange buying suggests the $62,000 to $66,000 region could play an important role in Bitcoin's next price move.

If Bitcoin retreats toward $62,000, the critical test will be whether buyers can absorb renewed selling pressure. Sustained buyer-led trading and a recovery in Coinbase's premium would strengthen the case for the support zone holding.

#5018. Solana Foundation Backs CoinGecko as Crypto Data Giant Eyes Trading

Solana Foundation Backs CoinGecko as Crypto Data Giant Eyes Trading

CoinGecko, one of crypto’s most widely used price-tracking sites, wants to do more than show users where markets are heading.

The crypto data platform said Tuesday that it has received backing from the Solana Foundation, the Switzerland-based nonprofit that supports the Solana network, to speed its expansion into market intelligence, new digital asset products and, potentially, trading.

The announcement came on the final day of Solana’s Breakpoint conference in London.

The Solana Foundation did not specify the size of the commitment, but a representative for the foundation told Decrypt it is taking a majority stake in CoinGecko. The terms were not disclosed.

CoinGecko said it will focus on three areas: expanding data coverage and analytics across blockchain ecosystems, exploring trading features that let users act onchain, and building simpler experiences for users worldwide.

“We’ve helped millions of people discover and understand this industry, but the opportunity ahead is much bigger,” said Bobby Ong, CoinGecko’s CEO and co-founder.

Vibhu Norby, chief product officer at the Solana Foundation, said the foundation believes the “next wave of adoption will come from making crypto dramatically easier to use.” (Disclosure: Decrypt’s parent company, Dastan, is building products on Solana, and Decrypt uses CoinGecko market data.)

Founded in 2014, CoinGecko says it provides data on more than 50 million crypto assets across more than 2,000 exchanges.

CoinGecko said it will keep serving users with the same commitment to “reliable, comprehensive market coverage and transparent methodologies.” Co-founder and President TM Lee said its commitment to accurate, reliable data “remains unchanged.”

The deal is one of several Solana announcements timed around Breakpoint. DTCC said last week that it plans to connect its tokenization service to Solana, with tokenized stocks, ETFs and Treasuries expected on the network in the second half of 2027.

At a Breakpoint panel, executives from DTCC and Barclays said tokenization’s biggest near-term payoff lies in putting idle collateral to work.

Editor's note: This article was updated after publication to include comment from the Solana Foundation.

#5015. America’s $1 trillion AI data center boom just suffered its first slowdown in over a year

US data center construction plunged 28% in the third quarter, but an October rebound suggests the infrastructure boom could still set another record.

US data center construction fell 28% in the third quarter of 2026, marking its first quarterly decline in more than a year as questions mount over the sustainability of the artificial intelligence infrastructure boom.

According to data released by Cleanview, developers began construction on facilities representing 8.4 gigawatts of power capacity between July and September, down from a record 11.7 GW in the preceding quarter.

The number of buildings starting construction also fell to 65 from 96, although the quarter still ranked third-highest in Cleanview's records and was up 55% from the same period last year.

US buildings that began vertical construction in October 2026.

The slowdown raises questions about an industry whose expansion has become central to the global AI investment boom.

Michael Thomas, founder of Cleanview Energy, estimated that data centers whose construction began this year will cost approximately $1 trillion to build and equip. He said:

“A lasting slowdown would reach the utilities planning new power plants, the companies making chips and turbines, and a stock market that has bet heavily on AI.”

The findings are based on Cleanview's analysis of thousands of satellite images tracking construction activity at more than 1,300 data center buildings across the United States.

Despite the third-quarter slowdown, construction activity rebounded sharply in October, with developers starting facilities representing 4.6 GW of capacity.

That was the second-highest monthly total Cleanview has recorded and nearly double the capacity that began construction in October 2025.

Three major projects accounted for approximately 35% of the month's activity. These included 720 megawatts at Nscale's Monarch campus in West Virginia, 461 MW at Related Digital's Stargate Michigan campus and 450 MW at Crusoe's Abilene campus in Texas.

Together, the projects represented approximately 1.63 GW of new construction starts, highlighting the scale of investment still flowing into AI infrastructure.

In view of this, Thomas said it was too early to conclude that the third-quarter decline signaled a lasting slowdown.

This is because October's strong rebound could push fourth-quarter construction starts to a record 14 GW if the pace continues through November and December.

#5012. Judge Acquits MIT Brothers in $25M Ethereum MEV Case, Ending Prosecution

Judge Acquits MIT Brothers in $25M Ethereum MEV Case, Ending Prosecution

A federal judge in Manhattan has acquitted Anton and James Peraire-Bueno, the MIT-educated brothers accused of exploiting Ethereum's transaction ordering system to take $25 million from automated trading bots.

U.S. District Judge Jessica Clarke granted the brothers' motion for a judgment of acquittal on Monday, clearing them of wire fraud, conspiracy to commit wire fraud and conspiracy to commit money laundering, according to her ruling. The decision closes the criminal case without a second trial.

"The question before the Court is not whether the Peraire-Buenos' conduct was right or moral, or even whether it violated uncharged crimes," Clarke wrote. To convict on wire fraud, prosecutors had to show the brothers made a material misrepresentation, and none of the government's theories did so, she found. Ethereum, she noted, is a "trustless, highly adversarial environment with no terms of use."

While the brothers' scheme "may have frustrated the spirit" of MEV-Boost, software that lets Ethereum validators outsource the ordering of transactions to specialized block builders, that was not enough to make out fraud, Clarke said. In a footnote, she added that the prosecution "raises serious due process concerns," though she declined to rule on that question.

Because the money laundering charge relied on the proceeds of the alleged wire fraud, it fell with the fraud counts.

The case stemmed from an April 2023 exploit targeting so-called sandwich bots, automated traders that profit from maximal extractable value (MEV) by placing their own trades on either side of other users' transactions.

According to the ruling, the brothers placed bait transactions designed to lure specific bots, then exploited a bug in a relay's code to see the bots' bundled trades before committing to a block. They then swapped the bait transactions for trades that sold the tokens instead, leaving the bots with about $25 million in losses in roughly 12 seconds. Authorities arrested the pair in May 2024, saying they spent months planning the attack and took steps to hide the proceeds.

At trial in Manhattan last year, an insider testified that the brothers had allegedly planned the attack, and the defense had earlier sought to block evidence of their Google searches. Clarke declared a mistrial in November 2025, after the jury deadlocked.

The case was closely watched in the crypto industry. Clarke's ruling described it as a "first of its kind" prosecution, a label the government itself used in its indictment.

#5010. Bitcoin faces a new threat as US prices surge at fastest pace in 38 years

Industrial output fell 0.4% even as imported machinery and technology costs hit a record monthly increase, while futures markets pointed to higher interest rates through March.

US prices for imported capital goods such as computers and machinery rose 1.5% in October from September, the biggest monthly increase since the Bureau of Labor Statistics began monthly publication in December 1988. Industrial production fell 0.4% in the Federal Reserve's preliminary estimate.

The two reports, released November 17, matter for Bitcoin traders who finance positions with borrowed dollars. Softer output can make a case for lower rates, while rising equipment costs show that price pressure extends beyond fuel. Before the Fed minutes, interest-rate futures pointed to higher rates into early 2027.

The BLS reported that computers, peripherals and semiconductors, electrical equipment and machinery drove the increase in capital-goods prices. Nonfuel import prices rose 0.8%, up from 0.5% in September. Fuel import prices rose 3.6% after rising 6.3% in September, and overall import prices rose 1.1% in October.

How much of the equipment price increase will reach consumers remains uncertain. The BLS also left some annual comparisons blank because the 2025 federal shutdown prevented the agency from collecting the year-earlier survey data.

The Fed's preliminary industrial production estimate put October's drop in output at 0.4% after a 0.7% rise in September. Utilities output fell 4.3% and mining fell 0.8%. Manufacturing rose 0.3% but edged down 0.1% excluding motor vehicles and parts. Overall production was still 2.1% higher than a year earlier.

October’s monthly changes in two import-price indexes and the Federal Reserve’s industrial production index.

#5008. Nvidia Board Not Considering Jensen Huang's Return: Report

Nvidia Board Not Considering Jensen Huang's Return: Report

Nvidia's board is not considering bringing back Jensen Huang as CEO, despite a growing campaign for his return led by some of the biggest names in tech and backed by President Donald Trump.

The board is focused on its investigation into Huang and is not in discussions to reinstate him, a person familiar with the matter told CNBC on Monday. The independent investigation, led by law firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz, is examining Huang's conduct and whether it compromised national security or company secrets.

Huang resigned as Nvidia's CEO on Thursday, after 33 years at the helm of the company he co-founded. His departure followed his admission of an affair and reports that he had been romantically involved with Alice Huang, an executive at an Nvidia customer whom U.S. officials suspect of spying for China, as well as with Nvidia employees. The week of revelations saw hundreds of billions of dollars wiped from Nvidia's market value.

The report cuts against a weekend of growing pressure for Huang's return. The Financial Times reported that Huang had discussed a comeback with allies, though Altimeter Capital's Brad Gerstner, one of the investors urging the board to keep "all options" open, told CNBC that Huang had not discussed returning with him. A representative for Huang declined to comment on the speculation.

An online petition calling for Huang's reinstatement had drawn 191,996 signatures by Monday. Its backers include Elon Musk, who said on Friday that he was signing it, as well as Y Combinator CEO Garry Tan and Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney. The site notes that signatories' affiliations are self-reported and unverified. Nvidia employees have also pushed internally for his return.

Trump said on Monday that he would welcome Huang back. "I would certainly not mind seeing that," he told reporters, adding that national security had not been put at risk, as Decrypt reported. That puts him at odds with Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent, who said on Sunday that Nvidia should look outside the company for its next CEO.

Others in tech have stopped short of backing a return. OpenAI CEO Sam Altman, who was briefly ousted from his own company in 2023, said at a Semafor conference on Monday that Huang had "long been a hero of mine," but that he did not know enough to say whether Huang should come back.

Nvidia shares closed down 4.1% at $156.14 on Monday, and were down a further 0.5% at $155.67 in premarket trading early on Tuesday, according to Yahoo! Finance data.

Nvidia's board said on Friday that it was in the process of selecting a search firm for the CEO role. Chief financial officer Colette Kress, the interim CEO, will lead the company's quarterly earnings call on Tuesday.

#5006. Bitwise launches ZDAY, its first non-crypto ETF, to bet on AI cyber defense

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The crypto asset manager's new fund will trade on the NYSE and carries a 0.75% management fee and targets 10 to 30 holdings.

Bitwise Asset Management launched the actively managed Bitwise AI Cyber Defense ETF, which invests in companies developing defenses against AI-enabled cyberattacks and tools to secure AI systems. The fund marks the crypto asset manager's first exchange-traded product outside of digital assets.

The fund began trading on the NYSE under the ticker ZDAY. It carries a 0.75% management fee and targets 10 to 30 holdings, including Palo Alto Networks, CrowdStrike, Cloudflare, and Zscaler.

"Crypto has lived at the leading edge of cybersecurity since its inception," Bitwise CIO Matt Hougan said in a release. "We've watched a multibillion-dollar security industry assemble around it in five years. The same thing is now happening to AI, on a much larger scale."

The growing security risks associated with AI also helped shape Bitwise's decision to launch the fund, with the company pointing to a July incident involving Hugging Face as an example.

"We realized the playing field was going to change dramatically," CEO Hunter Horsley told The Block. "Not just for crypto, but for the entirety of the internet."

In July, Hugging Face, a platform for building, sharing, and deploying AI models and applications, disclosed that an autonomous AI agent had intruded into its systems. Five days later, OpenAI said its own models were responsible: they had broken in while being tested internally for cybersecurity capabilities, with some safeguards loosened for the test.

"ZDAY is simply a product we wanted ourselves and thought should exist," Horsley said. "We couldn't think of a good reason we shouldn't do it. Or that a legacy asset manager would create something better."

Bitwise manages more than $14 billion in client assets, with a suite of cryptocurrency ETFs covering bitcoin, ether, XRP, Hyperliquid, and other digital assets.

#5004. Trezor Shipping Partner Breach Exposes 27,000 Orders as Customers Report Scam Calls

Trezor said Monday that a data breach at Skladon, one of its shipping partners, exposed personal information tied to about 27,000 hardware-wallet orders, and that some customers have already reported scam calls. The company’s own systems were not compromised, and no wallet backups, PINs or passphrases were involved.

Skladon notified Trezor on Nov. 15 of unauthorized access to systems that held customer shipping data. The breach itself took place on Nov. 7. Trezor believes the attacker exported records for orders placed between Aug. 9 and Nov. 7. An investigation is ongoing.

The exposed records included full names, phone numbers, delivery addresses, email addresses, order dates, and the products purchased and their prices. Trezor said neither it nor Skladon ever holds wallet backups, PINs or passphrases, so the breach does not, by itself, let an attacker move a buyer’s bitcoin. It does give a stranger a home address and proof that the person bought a device designed to store crypto.

Anyone who received Trezor’s incident email and ordered in that window was affected. A small number of customers who ordered between Aug. 9 and 14 may also be included, but the company cannot email them because those details had already been anonymized under its 90-day retention policy. It is reaching those buyers through its blog and posted the same warning on X. Skladon does not ship to the United States, Trezor said, so U.S. customers were not affected by this incident. The company added that Skladon delivers to many countries, and that the leak is not limited to one region.

Trezor told customers to treat any unexpected call, text, email or letter about their order as suspicious, and to be alert to potential risks to their physical security. Scammers may quote real order details, including what was bought and what was paid, to make a message look genuine. Some customers have already reported scam calls.

“Trezor will never cold-call you or ask for your wallet backup,” the company said. Customers should contact support only through the company’s website, never through a link, QR code or number in an unsolicited message, and should never enter a wallet backup anywhere except on their device. If a message contains a threat or makes someone feel unsafe, Trezor said to contact local police first.

This is not the first time a Trezor contractor has lost customer data. In August, a breach at shipping provider ShipMonk exposed records from 13,689 customers, including 11,742 with full names, emails, phone numbers and shipping addresses. Trezor later said another approximately 67,000 U.S. customers who ordered between November 2019 and August 2021 were also affected, after ShipMonk failed to delete records it had assured the company were gone.

In September, a breach at marketing platform Brevo let an attacker send a phishing email through Trezor’s account and, the company later confirmed, export 347,149 newsletter addresses. Trezor took down the malicious domain within 20 minutes, but about 2,500 people had already clicked the link.

“We know this is not the first time customer contact details and addresses have been exposed in an incident involving one of our partners,” Trezor said Monday, adding that it is sorry, especially to anyone affected again. Hardware-wallet makers have faced this risk before. In 2020, an unauthorized party accessed Ledger’s e-commerce and marketing database, leaking over 1 million email addresses; Bitcoin Magazine later reported that more than 200,000 people also had home addresses exposed, and that some victims were later mailed fake devices.

In response, Trezor said anonymous pickup is now available in 18 European countries, so a name and home address need not reach the company, and that it is working to launch the option in the United States. It is also cutting how long it keeps order data from 90 days to 45, with plans to reduce that period further, and it has informed the data protection authority. The company said it is reviewing its relationships with third-party partners and the security requirements it places on them.

#5003. Solana’s Trump memecoin launch tested infrastructure ahead of institutional push, Lily Liu says

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Liu said the launch tested Solana's capacity under heavy activity, with the network reaching 60,000 transactions per second during a marketwide liquidation wave.

Solana Solana (SOL) Foundation President Lily Liu said the January 2025 launch of the TRUMP memecoin and the October 2025 crypto liquidation wave served as live stress tests for the network’s financial infrastructure ahead of its institutional momentum.

Speaking to The Block's Gareth Jenkinson at Solana Breakpoint in London, Liu described the token's launch as the decade's biggest trading event, arguing that the network's decentralized infrastructure handled the activity even as centralized financial platforms struggled to keep pace.

She said trading began on a Friday evening and continued through a weekend when traditional markets were closed, with Solana's validators, wallets, liquidity pools, and transaction routers processing activity independently.

“You can't stage a test like that of infrastructure,” Liu said. "To be literally pounced upon on a Friday evening and for all of that to go perfectly seamlessly.”

Liu acknowledged that investors have different views about memecoins as an asset class but argued that their trading activity should also be considered separately from the infrastructure processing it.

"You're not going to test that infrastructure with Apple stock, you're just not going to," she said.

She also pointed to the challenge of supporting potentially thousands of new assets, with unpredictable trading activity around the clock.

Liu also recapped another major test she said Solana faced during the Oct. 10, 2025, crypto market selloff, when approximately $20 billion in positions were liquidated across the market within two hours. The network briefly reached 60,000 transactions per second during the episode without experiencing any downtime.

"Last year when there was the 10/10 event — that was a big stress, it was a very stressful event for everywhere in blockchain," she said. "But then if you looked at how Solana handled it, nothing went down."

Liu said infrastructure could only be properly tested when markets were live, with trading activity unfolding at a scale no one could reliably predict.

Liu's comments come as traditional finance institutions are advancing their onchain initiatives, with major names taking them to Solana.

Last week, the Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation announced plans to bring tokenized versions of eligible securities to the network in the second half of 2027. The DTCC’s tokenization service launched on Oct. 28 on Canton Network and its private Besu network.

Schroders also deployed its first tokenized fund on the network in November, and Formation Securities has notified the SEC of its plan to operate a tokenized securities venue on Solana, targeting an early 2027 launch.

#5000. Optimizely Hack Hijacked Ledger, BBC and 176 Other Sites to Swap Crypto Wallet Addresses

Optimizely Hack Hijacked Ledger, BBC and 176 Other Sites to Swap Crypto Wallet Addresses

An attacker hijacked Optimizely, a platform widely used by companies to run experiments on their websites, to serve malware that swapped visitors' crypto wallet addresses for their own, on sites including hardware wallet maker Ledger.

Optimizely said in a status update on Monday evening that an unauthorized party had exploited a vulnerability in its Experimentation product to add malicious JavaScript to some customer projects. The company said it had fixed the flaw, removed the injected code, secured affected user accounts and added protections to stop unauthorized code being delivered.

Earlier today, Optimizely identified and contained a security incident affecting some Optimizely Web Experimentation customers. The vulnerable path is blocked, the injected code was removed, and customers have been contacted directly.

— Optimizely (@Optimizely) November 17, 2026

Researchers at security firm Report URI, which sells tools to detect malicious website scripts, found the malicious code in 188 of the 1,611 Optimizely scripts they examined, belonging to 178 organizations, according to a blog post by founder Scott Helme. The affected sites included Zoom, the BBC, CNN, Salesforce, GitLab, IKEA and HSBC, as well as crypto and trading firms such as Ledger, IG and OANDA.

Because the code was served from Optimizely's own trusted servers, affected websites loaded it without their own systems being breached.

The malware's main purpose was to steal crypto. The script scanned web pages for valid wallet addresses on networks including Bitcoin, Ethereum, Tron, Solana and TON, and replaced them with addresses controlled by the attacker, according to Report URI. It also swapped addresses copied to a user's clipboard, meaning anyone who copied an address while an affected page was open could have pasted the attacker's instead.

The attacker's wallets received about $7,150 in crypto, mostly in Bitcoin, Litecoin and USDT, according to Report URI's update on Tuesday. The attacker emptied the wallets early on Tuesday. One Tron address used to cash out had also been used in earlier operations in March and on November 3, allowing Report URI to link the attacker to other attacks, the firm said.

The script also skimmed card details typed into some sites' own payment forms, though not those entered into hosted payment fields such as Stripe's, and on iPhones and iPads it attempted to load exploit kits known as Coruna and DarkSword, which target older versions of iOS. Report URI said it could not see how many card numbers were stolen or how many devices were compromised.

The injected code first appeared early on November 16 UTC, and had been removed from all of the scripts Report URI could find by late that evening.

Optimizely spokesperson Victoria Johnson told Gizmodo that an unauthorized party had "exploited a vulnerability in the Experimentation account signup flow," and that the company had blocked access to the vulnerable path, deployed a fix and notified all affected customers. The company has not said how the attacker gained access to other customers' projects.

Helme said visitors to affected sites on November 16 should check any crypto transfers they made, update their iPhones and iPads, and watch their card statements. Website operators should review custom code in their Optimizely projects and rotate credentials, he added.

#4998. Anchorage, Kalshi Prime let institutions use custodied bitcoin as margin

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The partnership builds on Anchorage Digital's expansion into institutional collateral management.

Anchorage Digital has partnered with Kalshi Prime to let institutional clients use bitcoin and stablecoins held in custody at Anchorage Digital Bank as margin for trading on Kalshi Prime's HODL Margining network.

The arrangement uses a tri-party collateral model in which Anchorage retains custody of pledged assets while Kalshi Prime recognizes them as margin, allowing clients to trade without transferring or selling their holdings.

The partnership extends Kalshi Prime's perpetual futures and prediction market offerings to institutions seeking to use bank-custodied digital assets as collateral.

"Institutions should not have to choose between holding Bitcoin and trading with it," Anchorage Digital CEO Nathan McCauley said in a release. "With HODL Margining, a client keeps its digital assets in custody at a federally chartered bank and uses them as margin on a CFTC-regulated venue, with no wire, no sale, and no change of custodian."

The partnership builds on Anchorage's expansion into institutional collateral management. In March, the company expanded its Atlas platform to offer collateral management and oversight services for institutional lenders and credit providers.

The development also follows guidance from the Commodity Futures Trading Commission earlier this year, allowing bitcoin, ether and USDC to be used as collateral in derivatives markets.

Separately, The Wall Street Journal reported Monday that Kalshi acquired New York-based broker FB Securities and plans to launch stock trading. The company plans to offer stocks, ETFs and similar products as it expands toward becoming a multi-asset exchange, sources told the publication.

#4997. Coinbase Launches ChatGPT Plugin for Trading Bitcoin, Stocks, and Derivatives

Coinbase launched an official plugin for ChatGPT on Tuesday that lets users connect a Coinbase account and trade bitcoin, other cryptocurrencies, stocks and derivatives without leaving the conversation, provided they approve every order.

The company said the tool puts trading “in the same chat you use for everything else.” Users can connect an account, review a portfolio and place orders from ChatGPT on the web, on mobile or on desktop. Chief executive Brian Armstrong wrote that “starting today anyone with ChatGPT can connect their Coinbase account” and can “trade right inside your Coinbase account.”

Coinbase posted a link to the official plugin in ChatGPT’s directory. Its developer documentation describes a short setup: sign in, review the access being requested and choose which portfolios the plugin can see. Interactive confirmation cards in the chat cover new orders, edits, cancellations and conversions between dollars and USDC. Read-only requests, such as checking a balance, do not use them.

The announcement did not single out bitcoin, but the plugin sits on the same rails as Coinbase for Agents, the company’s toolkit for connecting AI apps to a trading account. That documentation lists spot trading in more than 900 pairs, including bitcoin, along with eligible U.S. stocks and dated futures from Coinbase Financial Markets. The examples include a market buy of bitcoin and a dated bitcoin futures contract. Orders go through Advanced Trade, so those fees and eligibility rules apply. Fees vary by product, fee tier and whether an order adds or takes liquidity, and they are shown on the order preview.

The official plugin is narrower than the full agent toolkit. A separate custom connection is required for the company’s Global Derivatives products and for x402 payments, which agents can use to buy market data. Coinbase already offers U.S. customers stock and ETF trading on its own platform, but the agent documentation says equity orders trade during regular market hours by default. The plugin should not be read as a new around-the-clock stock venue. What a user can buy still depends on the products already attached to that account.

Armstrong’s “anyone with ChatGPT” line is broader than the documentation. The official plugin is currently available to U.S. Coinbase users, and availability also depends on a user’s ChatGPT account and workspace. If the plugin does not appear, the company tells users to check their workspace’s plugin policy. In a disclaimer attached to Tuesday’s announcement, it said product availability, eligibility and Coinbase entities “vary by jurisdiction,” that trading can result in a total loss, and that “AI agents may fail.”

The same post said users direct all AI activity and assume full responsibility. The developer documentation adds that agents can misinterpret instructions or produce inaccurate output, and that the user is responsible for reviewing and authorizing trades. Access can be revoked later in an account’s security settings, under connections.

OpenAI’s help pages describe a distinct Finances feature in the United States that can connect bank and credit-report data but cannot place trades, and note that third-party plugins that support transactions are a separate product.

Tuesday’s plugin is the most visible version of work the company has been shipping for months. In June, it introduced Coinbase for Agents, a login and a command-line tool that let an AI app trade inside limits set by the user, including a gradual rebalancing toward a bitcoin-heavy portfolio. On Nov. 11, it added Agent Controls, server-side caps on trade size and 24-hour volume, plus an auto-stop if a portfolio falls by a set percentage. Those controls live on the company’s servers, so an agent cannot switch them off.

The official plugin is meant for people who want to stay in the loop. The company also documents a custom ChatGPT connection for automated workflows that does not add a confirmation card before each trade, though sign-in, consent and authorization for financial actions still apply. For the official plugin, every bitcoin, stock or derivatives order still needs a human yes.

#4996. Alpaca says tokenization network tops $10 billion in minting and redemption volume

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Alpaca helped partners tokenize Circle shares and S&P 500 ETF holdings ahead of DTCC's tokenization service launch.

Alpaca's Instant Tokenization Network has processed more than $10 billion in cumulative minting and redemption volume, co-founder Hitoshi Harada told The Block's Gareth Jenkinson on The Starting Block at Solana Breakpoint in London.

Alpaca is a U.S. broker-dealer that provides trading and custody infrastructure for fintech and crypto platforms, including services for tokenized stocks. Harada said market makers are increasingly using the network to mint and redeem tokenized shares in kind, but that the $10 billion figure excludes secondary-market trades, which accounted for much higher volume.

xStocks was one of the partners Alpaca launched the network with in 2025. In December, Kraken said Alpaca would be its preferred provider for sourcing and custodying the U.S. shares that back xStocks. Harada said Alpaca has more than 90% of the market for custody of the shares backing tokenized stocks offered outside the U.S.

In January, Alpaca announced it had raised $150 million in a Series D funding round at a $1.15 billion valuation. Drive Capital led the round with participation from Citadel Securities, BNP Paribas's VC wing Opera Tech Ventures, DRW Venture Capital, Bank Muscat, and Kraken, among others.

Alpaca was among the first brokers to join DTCC's tokenization service, with Circle shares and holdings in the SPDR S&P 500 exchange-traded fund among the assets Alpaca's partners converted into tokens and back during test trades. DTCC's service went live on Oct. 28, and Alpaca clients can now use the broker's API to mint DTCC tokens from eligible securities they already hold, Harada said.

DTCC plans to offer tokenized securities on Solana Solana (SOL) in the second half of 2027, connecting its DTCC Tokenization Service to the Layer 1 blockchain. Alpaca is also one of the distributors Formation Securities has lined up for its Solana venue. Formation expects trading to start in early 2027 with tokenized shares of Exodus Movement, Forward Industries, and Galaxy Digital.

#4994. 'The Biggest Opportunity in Crypto Is Already Underway': Solana Bets Big on AI Agents

'The Biggest Opportunity in Crypto Is Already Underway': Solana Bets Big on AI Agents

AI agents are becoming the internet's main users, and the Solana Foundation wants its blockchain to be how they pay for things.

"The biggest opportunity in crypto is already underway," Rishin Sharma, who leads AI at the Solana Foundation, said at the Breakpoint conference in London.

Sharma used his presentation to showcase Solana.ai, a new homepage for the network's AI ecosystem, which the foundation launched last week. More than 120 teams are now building AI products on Solana across payments, trading and infrastructure, he said.

Bots and other automated software accounted for more than 53% of global web traffic in 2025, up from 51% the year before, according to cybersecurity firm Imperva's latest Bad Bot Report, which Sharma cited. Agents have also come to dominate AI usage: API keys classified as agentic accounted for 76% of the tokens processed through model marketplace OpenRouter in October 2026, up from 8% a year earlier, according to the company's monthly data recap.

At the same time, AI is getting much cheaper. Sharma's slides showed the price of a million tokens falling from $37.50 for GPT-4 to $0.14 for GPT-5 nano, a 270-fold drop, while monthly token use has risen around 330-fold.

"Which model is smarter matters a little bit less, and what matters more is what the model can actually do," Sharma said. "What are the tools it's using? What are the data it's able to access? And what money can it functionally use?"

Payments are where that shift is showing up first, Sharma said. The x402 standard, which lets AI agents pay for online services, has processed more than 400 million payments and $100 million in volume, up from zero a year ago, he said. Solana-based BlockRun, which he described as the largest x402 server, has handled more than 60 million transactions in the past two months.

The foundation has also launched payment channels that it says can handle 1 million payments per second, a private AI service offering attested private inference on Solana, and Pay.sh, a server integrating more than 100 APIs that agents can pay for, Sharma said. He added that the foundation is working with companies including Google, Alibaba and ByteDance to let them charge for their AI models through agent payments.

Next, the foundation plans to publish benchmarks for how well AI models perform financial tasks, and to launch an on-ramp that lets users convert cash to stablecoins and hand them to their AI assistants, he said.

Macro investor and Real Vision founder Raoul Pal, speaking earlier at the conference, argued that agents are not yet real economic actors. "They're like kids," he said. "These are young kids who spend nothing on a few sweets and stuff." But as stocks and other assets move onchain, he said, agents will start using blockchains to trade and settle, and "the value accrues to the underlying layer one."

Solana has been building toward this for months. The foundation teamed up with Google Cloud on a stablecoin payments service for AI agents in May, while the x402 Foundation, backed by Coinbase and the Linux Foundation, is developing the payments standard. Franklin Templeton has described agentic AI as a killer use case for crypto.
