#4942. Honda just showed off its first high-end electric mountain bike

Honda makes just about everything with a motor, from lawnmowers and generators to cars, motorcycles, and even airplanes. Now the Japanese giant is adding another electrified vehicle to the list – a premium electric mountain bike.

Unveiled at EICMA 2026 in Milan, the new Honda RN0 marks the company’s first entry into the premium electric mountain bike market. And unlike many automotive-branded e-bikes that amount to little more than a licensing deal and a familiar logo slapped on someone else’s frame, Honda appears to have actually put some engineering muscle behind this one.

The RN0 was jointly developed with German high-end e-MTB manufacturer ROTWILD and features a proprietary carbon monocoque frame and distinctive gull-arm swingarm inspired by Honda’s motorcycle chassis development experience.

There’s actually some real history behind that connection. Honda experimented with mountain bike development in the early 2000s, culminating in its wild RN01 downhill racing bike. In 2005, that project won the overall series championship in the UCI Mountain Bike Downhill World Cup. Honda never turned the RN01 into a consumer bicycle, but more than two decades later, the RN0 is effectively picking up where the company previously left off.

The roughly 24 kg (53 lb) RN0 is set up as a multi-role electric mountain bike, featuring 160 mm FOX 36 FLOAT Factory Kashima front suspension paired with a FOX FLOAT X Factory Kashima rear shock. DT Swiss H1900 SPLINE wheels use a mullet setup with a 29-inch wheel up front and 27.5-inch wheel in the rear, while Shimano DEORE XT four-piston brakes handle stopping duties.

Those are some quality components, but the drivetrain may be even more interesting.

Honda is using Shimano’s new 2.3 kg drive unit, rated for up to 100 Nm of torque and 750 watts of maximum output, paired with a sizeable 720 Wh battery.

Shimano’s AUTO ASSIST can automatically vary motor assistance based on terrain and rider input, while AUTO SHIFT handles gear changes to maintain an appropriate cadence.

There’s even an Assist Carryover function that briefly continues providing power after pedaling stops, potentially making it easier to clear rocks, roots, and other technical obstacles that normally would see the assist grind to a halt right when it is needed most.

A 2.2-inch color display is integrated directly into the top tube, and there’s USB-C charging plus Shimano app connectivity for adjusting motor settings and electronically locking the bike.

Honda hasn’t yet announced pricing in its release, but with a carbon chassis, Factory-level FOX suspension, XT brakes, and premium Shimano electronics, don’t expect this to be the everyman’s electric bike.

If anything, the RN0 looks much more like Honda is finally bringing its motorcycle engineering mentality back to mountain bikes – this time with a battery and motor along for the ride.

#4941. Cathie Wood's ARK Sells $85 Million in Nvidia, Buys $40 Million in Crypto Stocks

Cathie Wood's ARK Sells $85 Million in Nvidia, Buys $40 Million in Crypto Stocks

Cathie Wood's ARK Invest sold roughly $85 million worth of Nvidia shares Monday while buying close to $40 million in crypto-exposed stocks, rebalancing exposure to technology and fintech stocks during a broad selloff.

The firm's daily trade file for November 16 shows sales of 543,092 Nvidia shares across two actively managed exchange-traded funds. Using Monday's $156.14 closing price, those shares were worth approximately $84.8 million; the disclosures list share counts rather than the exact prices ARK received.

ARK bought 277,523 shares of payments company Block, worth about $23 million at the close. It also added 180,534 shares of stablecoin issuer Circle, valued at $10.4 million, and 53,760 shares of crypto exchange Coinbase, worth around $6.4 million.

The purchases were spread across funds rather than concentrated in one vehicle. ARK's flagship Innovation ETF (ARKK) bought Circle and most of the Block shares, while its Next Generation Internet ETF (ARKW) bought Coinbase and the remaining Block stock.

All three crypto-linked stocks fell Monday. Circle dropped 7.3%, Coinbase declined 6.1% and Block lost 1.9%, while Nvidia slid 4.1% as investors continued selling technology shares.

That makes the whole movement seem like a shift in holdings, not evidence that ARK expects cryptocurrencies to recover before AI stocks. The manager says its funds can rebalance every trading day, and its trade notifications don't disclose why each position was changed or establish that particular sales financed particular purchases.

ARK had bought 90,443 Nvidia shares just one week earlier, on November 9, during another selloff. The latest sale therefore reverses some of that buying without necessarily suggesting the firm has abandoned the chipmaker or its long-standing investment case for artificial intelligence.

ARK also purchased 378,515 shares of SpaceX, worth roughly $52 million, while selling 30,659 shares of crypto exchange Bullish and units of Ethereum and Solana staking funds. So in this case, Wood would be selling crypto to buy one single AI stock.

Those sales included 300,000 shares of the 3iQ Ethereum Staking ETF and 350,000 shares of the 3iQ Solana Staking ETF. The firm's buying of crypto companies coincided with cuts to other crypto-related investments, a distinction that matters when trying to read a trading strategy from one day's activity.

Wood has repeatedly bought companies that handle crypto trading and payments during market selloffs. In September, ARK bought $37 million in Block stock in one session, and it previously increased its Circle and Coinbase stakes while reducing exposure to other crypto names.

The companies offer different ways to invest in digital assets without holding tokens directly. Block runs Cash App and Bitcoin-related products; Circle issues the USDC dollar-backed stablecoin; and Coinbase, the only American crypto exchange listed on the stock market, earns revenue from cryptocurrency trading and other services.

ARK's strategy has never been a simple choice between crypto and AI. Its flagship fund already counted Circle, Coinbase and SpaceX among major positions in August, alongside businesses tied to artificial intelligence and financial technology, as earlier holdings showed.

Nvidia's leadership changed five days before Monday's trades. Co-founder Jensen Huang resigned as CEO on November 11, with finance chief Colette Kress appointed interim chief executive, according to the company's SEC filing.

The chipmaker is due to report fiscal third-quarter results after the market closes Tuesday, with a conference call at 5 p.m. Eastern. Those results will give investors their first earnings update since the leadership change—one day after ARK sold more than half a million shares.

#4939. DBS lets eligible institutions in Asia mint and redeem USDC around the clock

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Building on their Project BLOOM collaboration, Singapore's DBS and Circle are giving eligible institutions direct, 24/7 access to USDC issuance and redemption.

DBS and Circle Circle Internet Group, Inc. (CRCL) are giving eligible institutional clients in Asia direct access to direct USDC minting and redemption through the bank, with settlement between dollars and the stablecoin available around the clock.

In a statement on Wednesday, the Singapore-based bank said the service will help institutions reduce their reliance on secondary market liquidity and extend settlement outside banking hours and cutoff times.

The service targets treasury, settlement, and liquidity management as institutions increase their use of tokenized money, it added.

"We believe the future of financial services will be multi-modal and multi-instrumental, with different forms of tokenized money serving different needs," said Evy Theunis, head of digital assets at DBS Bank's Institutional Banking Group. "By supporting USDC, alongside other trusted tokenised money instruments, we are providing institutions greater choice in how they move and manage value, based on what best serves their needs."

The DBS-Circle partnership also builds on their participation in Project BLOOM, a Monetary Authority of Singapore initiative testing interoperability between forms of tokenized money and financial networks.

Circle's USDC averaged $73.3 billion in circulation during the third quarter, up 8% from a year earlier, according to the company's latest financial results. Onchain transaction volume reached $13.9 trillion, up 49% year over year, though the stablecoin's market share slipped to 27%.

According to The Block's data, USDC is the world's second-largest stablecoin after Tether's USDT, with a supply of $69.3 billion as of Wednesday, down from $75.3 billion at the beginning of the year. USDT's supply has also decreased to $183 billion from about $187 billion over the same period.

The European Banking Authority classified USDC as the first "significant" e-money token under MiCA, with direct oversight of its reserves, liquidity and redemption plans from Nov. 12.

Circle has also expanded the stablecoin's commercial reach this year. Binance bought $100 million of Circle shares in September under a five-year USDC promotion arrangement and the company also signed a Chelsea FC sponsorship deal in August.

On Tuesday, the World Bank Group said it was working with Circle and Citi to test regulated dollar stablecoins for fund disbursements, initially using an intermediated and interoperable model.

#4938. 5 American, 6 Canadian: Workhorse W56 arrives in the True North

Electric truck brand Workhorse is bringing its W56 Class 5 and 6 electric trucks to Canada thanks to a new agreement with a Canadian dealer group and fleet management provider offering truck and trailer sales, parts, repair, and 24/7 roadside assistance and towing services across the Northern nation.

Building on years of electric step van deployments in Canada and underscoring the company’s commitment to Canada as a growth region, the new dealer agreement with Tristan Fleet Management marks the first-ever Canadian dealer partnership for Workhorse.

“Canadian fleets need proven electric trucks that deliver on quality and performance in any condition,” said James Griffin, Chief Revenue Officer at Workhorse. “The W56 has already proven itself on this front in real-world fleet service across Canada. Tristan’s strong reputation, its extensive network, and ability to provide around-the-clock roadside support will be extremely valuable as Workhorse continues to grow our presence in the country.”

Workhorse has been selling and supporting electric trucks in Canada since 2021 through its partnership with Purolator, a Canadian-owned leading integrated freight, package and logistics solutions provider that has grown alongside the rapid expansion of online shopping for everything from groceries to car parts and road-ready motorcycles has also seen the expansion of last-mile delivery contractors. These fleet operators and drivers are essential workers who keep the online economy rolling, and they are getting positively pummeled by this year’s record fuel prices, a product of America’s latest “dumb oil war” in Iran.

And, after Workhorse’ fleet logged its 20 millionth mile driven with zero tailpipe emissions, you could hardly claim that it’s an unknown quantity – a fact that Tristan understands.

“Our customers have been exploring electrification for some time now,” explains Daniel Barbu, Tristan’s Vice President and General Manager. “With generous incentives from Écocamionnage, as well as the current extremely high cost of fuel, the business case for going electric has never been stronger … we’re looking forward to helping Workhorse expand its customer base across Canada and supporting our customers meet their own goals for sustainability and vehicle performance.”

Tristan operates out of seven locations throughout Québec and Ontario. In addition to its Workhorse offerings, the company is also an authorized Michelin tire, Cummins engine, and Vanguard trailer dealer.

#4936. TD Cowen tempers ether outlook, maintains Buy rating on Sharplink

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Sharplink's holdings rose from 892,708 ETH by the end of the third quarter to 896,003 ETH as of Nov. 16, with the recent increase coming primarily from staking rewards rather than new purchases.

TD Cowen on Wednesday maintained its Buy rating on Ethereum Ethereum (ETH) treasury company Sharplink SharpLink Inc. (SBET) and kept its price target at $13.

Senior Analyst Lance Vitanza said the firm is taking a more conservative view of ether’s price path after observing a sharp drop in ETH burn per transaction, even as network activity continues to grow.

TD Cowen now assumes ether ends the fourth quarter of 2026 at approximately $2,413, down 10% quarter-over-quarter from the third quarter, with a flatter appreciation trajectory thereafter.

“Ethereum activity remains robust, but more transactions are producing materially less ETH burn, prompting a more conservative price outlook," Vitanza wrote. "The flatter appreciation profile is deliberate. We remain constructive on Ethereum adoption but now place greater emphasis on declining ETH burn per transaction."

At the same time, he noted that Sharplink’s shift toward more active treasury management could generate incremental ether-denominated returns.

Sharplink's holdings rose from 892,708 ETH by the end of the third quarter to 896,003 ETH as of Nov. 16, with the recent increase coming primarily from staking rewards and related yield rather than new purchases. Cumulative staking rewards reached 31,820 ETH.

The treasury firm's management has also deployed capital into liquid staking via a $200 million allocation to Lido and into the Galaxy Sharplink Onchain Yield Fund.

"We view these initiatives as potentially important sources of incremental ETH-denominated return and an increasingly differentiated part of the Sharplink story," the note said. "For now, however, we largely leave that potential upside unmodeled."

In the case of a favorable ether price environment, the TD Cowen analyst said he expects Sharplink to "substantially outperform" spot ether exchange-traded products by increasing its holdings of ether per fully-diluted share. If ether price remains depressed, Vitanza said the company's staking yield should be more than enough to fully cover operating costs.

Sharplink reported third-quarter net income of $751.4 million, or $3.45 per diluted share, compared with $104.3 million a year earlier, driven largely by $676.7 million in unrealized gains on its ether holdings. Revenue rose 24% year over year to $13.5 million, almost entirely from staking income of $13.1 million.

Its CEO Joseph Chalom said the company is focused on “maximizing the productivity of our ETH holdings” while maintaining disciplined capital allocation.

"Our relentless focus translates into tangible financial results, highlighted by double-digit revenue growth, improved operating leverage and a material increase in net income," Chalom added.

#4934. Bitcoin Investors Are Capitulating—That’s Historically Been a Bottom Signal

Bitcoin Investors Are Capitulating—That’s Historically Been a Bottom Signal

Bitcoin’s latest crash has pushed investors into the kind of loss-taking that has historically shown up near market bottoms.

Realized losses, which investors lock in when they sell coins for less than they paid, have climbed relative to the profits taken over the past year.

That measure now sits at levels seen only near the lows of 2018, 2020, 2022 and early 2023, according to a report published Tuesday by onchain analytics firm Glassnode. It’s the first such reading since then.

Most of the selling came from recent buyers. Holders of coins less than six months old took losses into early 2026, cashed in gains during the rally into September and October, and are now selling at a loss again, Glassnode said. Leverage has been flushed too, with Nov. 13 and 14 bringing some of the largest long liquidations relative to open interest since 2023.

Bitcoin fell to about $62,000 on Nov. 15 before bouncing, and it traded near $64,970 on Tuesday. More than $2 billion in liquidations over the weekend left traders watching $60,000 as a potential next stop, Decrypt reported. The slide deepened last week as fears of further Federal Reserve rate hikes erased Bitcoin’s summer rally. Rising interest rates generally do not bode well for risk assets like Bitcoin, since investors typically rotate to safer bets like U.S. Treasuries when rates elevate.

But there are early signs of support, according to Glassnode. Buyers absorbed about 66,000 BTC in the $62,000 to $66,000 range during the two days of heaviest selling, adding to a zone where recent buyers accumulated around the August low.

Resting bids on Binance and Coinbase within 5% of the price now outweigh asks by about 2.3 to 1. The Coinbase Premium, which compares Bitcoin’s price on Coinbase with global markets, briefly turned positive at the low as U.S. buyers stepped in.

Still, Glassnode stopped short of calling a bottom. Broad demand remains thin: stablecoin supply has shrunk 4.2% over 90 days, and the Coinbase Premium has slipped back to a small discount.

Meanwhile, U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs extended their outflow streak to eight sessions on Monday and have bled about $2.2 billion so far in November, according to Decrypt’s ETF tracker.

That makes the $62,000 to $66,000 range the line to watch. Short-term holders bought very few coins below $62,000, and a large cluster of leveraged long positions sits just beneath it, between about $59,500 and $62,200.

A daily close below $62,000 could trigger a fast drop toward the June low of $58,500 and Bitcoin’s realized price of $53,700, the average price at which every coin last moved, Glassnode said.

On the upside, a move back above $74,100, the average cost basis of short-term holders, would confirm that a bottom is forming, according to the firm. Options traders on Deribit, meanwhile, price roughly a one-in-three chance that Bitcoin settles below $53,700 at the September 2027 expiry.

The Fed’s next rate decision is Dec. 9, a day before November inflation data is released.

#4932. Ex-Robinhood engineer pleads guilty to $125K Hyperliquid trading scheme

The former Robinhood engineer used confidential information about upcoming Robinhood token listings to trade on Hyperliquid.

Former Robinhood software engineer Huaisong “Jerry” Xiang has pleaded guilty to wire fraud after using confidential information about upcoming crypto listings on the platform to trade perpetual futures on the decentralized exchange Hyperliquid, earning $125,523 in illicit profits.

On Monday, the US Department of Justice said that Xiang entered the plea before US District Judge Colleen McMahon and agreed to forfeit $125,523. Xiang faces a maximum sentence of 20 years in prison and is scheduled to be sentenced on Feb. 25, 2027.

Prosecutors said the software engineer had access to nonpublic information about which tokens Robinhood Crypto planned to list and when. Between 2025 and 2026, he repeatedly bought perpetual futures tied to those tokens before the announcements were made public.

The plea follows September charges against Xiang and another former Robinhood engineer, Hefu Chai. Both were accused of exploiting internal listing information to trade on Hyperliquid, with prosecutors initially alleging that each made over $50,000.

The DOJ’s Monday announcement did not provide an update on Chai’s case.

In May, crypto analytics firm Kaiko flagged suspicious Hyperliquid trading ahead of Robinhood’s Jan. 15 listing of Lighter (LIT). One of the wallets identified in its investigation matches an address that prosecutors later linked to Chai, who allegedly used it to trade LIT perpetuals ahead of the listing.

#4931. ‘Gift from the market’: Bitcoin shows signs of a local bottom after six weeks of losses, analyst says

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

K33's Vetle Lunde said bitcoin was an attractive buy below $70,000, while Glassnode said buying demand remains thin.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell 14% over the past week following six straight weekly losses, a streak analysts at research and brokerage firm K33 said has only been witnessed twice before, between Jan. 26 and March 1, 2026, and from April 3 to May 29, 2022.

"BTC is now deeply oversold, with daily RSI reaching 19.95 on Saturday and remaining below 25 since Friday. Furthermore, BTC has fallen below its 200-week moving average after recording its worst weekly return since June 7," K33 Head of Research Vetle Lunde noted in a new report.

At one point during the selloff, bitcoin was about 8.5% below its 200-week moving average, a deeper deviation than the 7.3% recorded at June's $58,500 lows, he noted, adding that 51.5% of circulating bitcoin is now held at a loss.

"Any entry below $70K is a gift from the market," Lunde said. "Together, these signals suggest BTC is trading in deep value territory, offering an attractive buying opportunity for medium- to long-term investors. Such conditions have typically been associated with market bottoms."

Meanwhile, in the options market, demand for downside protection pushed the one-month 25-delta skew above 20 for the first time since July, according to K33. "Similar levels have historically coincided with selling exhaustion near local bottoms," Lunde wrote. However, he said previous recoveries from similar levels had involved "prolonged consolidation," suggesting a choppy recovery rather than an immediate rebound.

Bitcoin is currently trading for around $64,000 on Wednesday, down 1.1% over the past 24 hours, after setting a record 13 consecutive days of lower daily highs on Tuesday, according to Lunde.

Bitcoin touched $62,000 on Sunday, with investors realizing losses on a scale last seen near the lows of 2022 and early 2023, according to analysts at Glassnode. Short-term holders added about 66,000 BTC to their holdings in the $62,000 to $66,000 range during the two days of heaviest selling, Glassnode said.

Bitcoin traded at a premium to the global price on Coinbase near Sunday's low after spending most of the previous few weeks at a discount. Glassnode said a consistent premium would be a sign of sustained U.S. buying, though the discount had returned by Tuesday. On Binance, the largest concentration of buy orders at a single price since August sits near $60,000, though traders can pull those orders before they get filled.

Glassnode said a daily close below $62,000 would put the August buyers at a loss, with a large cluster of long liquidations beneath, opening the path to revisiting the June low near $58,500 and the Realized Price, the average price at which circulating bitcoin last moved onchain, at $53,700.

Meanwhile, in a note to clients on Tuesday, Bitwise Chief Investment Officer Matt Hougan traced the selloff to a sharp shift in Fed rate expectations. After the Nov. 10 report showed U.S. consumer prices rose a hotter-than-expected 0.4% in October, traders went from pricing in a 66% chance of a 25bps hike in December to treating it as a certainty, Hougan said, adding that thin weekend trading made the drop worse as roughly $2 billion in leveraged positions were liquidated.

Hougan also noted that the Crypto Fear and Greed Index, a gauge of crypto market sentiment, fell to 12 over the weekend, below its reading at bitcoin's June low.

"That’s good news, in a way. Because when you know why markets are falling, you can check whether the cause has played out. And in this case, I think it has," Hougan said. "Markets have now priced in aggressive rate hikes, and a lot of leverage has been flushed out."

#4929. Yamaha’s new electric scooter takes aim at Europe’s 125cc commuters

Yamaha is expanding its electric scooter lineup with the new XENTER E, a 125cc-equivalent electric scooter designed specifically with European city riding in mind.

Unveiled around EICMA 2026 in Milan alongside the new NMAX E, the XENTER E is a high-wheel scooter riding on 16-inch wheels front and rear.

That’s an important distinction in European cities, where larger wheels can make quick work of the cobblestones, potholes, and tram tracks that can turn smaller-wheeled scooters into teeth-rattling experiences.

The XENTER E uses Yamaha’s new eAxle powertrain, combining the motor, controller, and reduction gearbox into a compact frame-mounted unit. The motor produces 7.5 kW (10 hp) of peak power and 33.3 Nm of torque, putting the scooter into Europe’s A1 license category.

Mounting the motor to the frame also means Yamaha can use a more conventional motorcycle-style swingarm rather than hanging the motor on the rear suspension like many scooters. That reduces unsprung weight and should help improve handling and ride quality.

Power is supplied from an integrated 3.5 kWh battery mounted low in the chassis. Yamaha claims 108 km (67 miles) of range on the WMTC Class 1 cycle, though real-world range is expected to be somewhat lower.

An 800W onboard charger can refill the battery in around 5.5 hours from a public charging station. Yamaha will also offer a cable for charging from a household outlet.

Other niceties include three riding modes, traction control, regenerative braking, a reverse mode for maneuvering at low speeds, smart-key operation, and a 5-inch TFT display. Smartphone connectivity adds navigation, battery information, maintenance reminders, and parking-location tracking.

For about four days now, I am thinking this could be my next ride. A 4 or 4,5 Kwh battery would be a real nice. But for now, it fits perfectly in my daily commute and I could trade in my 2024 Honda supercub. However, € 7800 is too much.. for this kind of money, or just a tad more, there are several low mileage BMW's CE04 on the market.

There’s also enough underseat storage for a full-face helmet, depending on its size, plus USB-C charging and provisions for accessories such as a windscreen and 34-liter top case.

The XENTER E shares its powertrain and battery with Yamaha’s new NMAX E, but the high-wheel layout gives it a distinctly more European commuter flavor.

Yamaha hasn’t officially announced pricing for each market, though early reports from Milan point to €7,799 in Germany. Deliveries are expected to begin in spring 2027.

It’s certainly not cheap, but the XENTER E looks like Yamaha is finally getting serious about building electric scooters that can replace its bread-and-butter 125cc urban machines, rather than simply supplement them.

#4928. World Bank Taps Circle and Citi to Test Stablecoins for Development Payments

World Bank Taps Circle and Citi to Test Stablecoins for Development Payments

The World Bank is turning to stablecoins to modernize how it moves development money, announcing Tuesday it will explore using Circle’s USDC and blockchain-based payment rails to speed up and cut the cost of disbursing funds around the world.

The institution said it will work with Citi and Circle to test how dollar-denominated, regulated stablecoins could operate alongside or improve its existing funds-distribution system, which runs on correspondent banking, the slow and costly network of intermediary banks that routes international payments.

Under the arrangement, Citi would supply its connections to established banking rails while Circle provides the infrastructure to mint and settle funds in USDC.

The logic is that even small efficiency gains, applied across the vast sums the World Bank moves, could free up meaningful resources for development. “This initiative is about exploring ways to make the financial plumbing of development work better for the people who need it most,” said World Bank Group President Ajay Banga, arguing that faster, cheaper money movement means “a health clinic gets built on time, a farmer gets paid before the harvest season ends.”

The effort builds on FundsChain, a World Bank blockchain platform designed to make fund disbursements more traceable. More than 350 projects across 96 countries are already onboarded to its digital ledgers, letting everyone involved in a project see how money is being spent.

The stablecoin exploration would focus initially on an intermediated, interoperable model, limited to borrower countries where digital assets are permitted, with plans to involve other banks over time.

Circle CEO Jeremy Allaire framed development finance as one of the most important applications for his company’s technology, saying money that moves “at the speed of the internet shouldn’t be reserved for the world’s biggest banks and corporations.”

Today, the @WorldBankGroup announced work with @circle and @Citi to bring the benefits of USDC to global funds distribution. A big step towards a more efficient and inclusive financial system.https://t.co/hq436tA5IR

— Jeremy Allaire (@jerallaire) November 17, 2026

Citi’s Shahmir Khaliq said the collaboration would deliver “24/7 capabilities at scale so that critical funds move in real time.”

The move hands the stablecoin industry a powerful institutional validator, and it fits a broader wave of traditional finance embracing the technology.

Wall Street is currently racing to claim the onchain dollar, with Goldman Sachs, Citi and other banks committing roughly $270 million to a joint stablecoin venture, while Deutsche Bank recently went live with crypto custody after a five-year buildout.

For Circle, whose USDC is the second-largest stablecoin, a role in the World Bank’s payment plumbing marks a notable step toward mainstream legitimacy, even as the broader crypto market endures a rough stretch of ETF outflows and falling prices.
